That range sits wholly inside the text band and nowhere near the protein one, so nothing about this separation moves — but **the separation's own width-robustness is untested**, because only the text side was swept and no protein arm was rebuilt at another width; D2.h carries the sweep and what it was for. **A certification asymmetry behind this comparison closed on 2026-08-12 (EXP-R2-181), and it moves nothing.** Every dense arm certifies its replacement estimand with a live equality — each block's own output rebuilt from the residual its normalisation read plus the intercepted feed-forward output, refused unless bit-equal — while the ProGen3 reference arm was certified by declaration alone. Pre-registered with three outcomes and then run: the worst absolute difference is **exactly 0.0**, on every one of the ten layers individually, reconstructed from three independently captured tensors rather than from an algebraic tautology. So both sides of the dense-versus-ProGen3 comparison now carry the same class of certification, and nothing published changes, because the identity holding is what these numbers already assumed. It verifies the *interception* and not the eager-MoE conversion's numerical fidelity, which is what the loader band gates (L24), and it was measured at one dtype, one device and one batch | EXP-R2-141–150, EXP-R2-181; replacement artefacts named in those log entries | **citable at declared cohorts; separation band-robust; proximal cause open** |
| F12 | **F10's companion on the same question, with retrieval excluded by construction instead of estimated and subtracted — and the binding baseline turns out not to be retrieval.** On 130 de novo designs certified to carry no detectable UniRef50 homologue (D1.e), 40 design series, 110,730 variants, scored against measured folding stability from the same file and assay as their own natural control, **ProtGPT2 beats both fragment-level corpus baselines** — MODEL − 3-mer conditional **+0.1439 [+0.1070, +0.1784]**, MODEL − 4-mer **+0.1270 [+0.0923, +0.1599]** — and beats corpus composition (+0.1261 [+0.0827, +0.1681]), mutation position and wild-type hydropathy, five of seven baselines in all — the two fragment rows now qualified by the order amendment at the end of this row, which both narrows them and bounds the certificate they rest on. **What it does not beat is free physicochemistry.** A one-parameter Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy-change sum reads **+0.4385 [+0.4171, +0.4593]** on the designs against the model's own **+0.0837 [+0.0408, +0.1268]**, so MODEL − hydropathy is **−0.3548 [−0.4031, −0.3051]**, and MODEL − BLOSUM62 is −0.0779 [−0.1292, −0.0243]: both intervals exclude zero in the wrong direction over 40 series. The model's margin over corpus statistics survives the exclusion of retrieval; its margin over a hydrophobicity scale does not merely vanish, it reverses. **That reversal is a property of designed sequence, confirmed under its own pre-registration — and confirming it narrowed what it means (EXP-R2-193).** Tested as an *interaction*, one joint interval on the difference rather than two contrasts read side by side, the same arm on the same file and assay reads MODEL − hydropathy **+0.1147 [+0.0446, +0.1862]** on natural domains inside the designs' own length span against −0.3548 on the designs, an interaction of **−0.4695 [−0.5535, −0.3889]** that holds in all sixteen arm × design-subset cells and is sign-invariant at every length pad. **Length is excluded by measurement rather than by matching:** a random half-split of the length-matched natural side excludes zero in 1 of 20 draws, exactly nominal, and a length shift the size of the one separating designs from their controls moves the contrast by **+0.0450 [−0.0934, +0.1858]** — zero, and the wrong sign. **What the pre-registered specificity control corrected is the reading, not the verdict: the interaction is not hydropathy-specific.** Six of the seven baselines carry it, −0.1755 to −0.4695 with intervals excluding zero, the exception being wild-type hydropathy, whose own reading collapses on designs. The halves say why: going from length-matched naturals to designs the **model falls 0.229** (+0.3128 to +0.0837), which every channel shares because it is the model's own term, while **hydropathy rises 0.241**, which only that channel does; the two are near-equal, they add, and hydropathy is simply the only channel where the sum crosses zero instead of shrinking a margin. So what is established is a **broad degradation of this decoder's ranking on designed sequence, not a defeat by hydrophobicity** — the model still beats the fragment and composition channels on the designs, by a smaller margin than on naturals. **Designedness is not separated from amino-acid usage, and that is non-overlap rather than underpower:** designs carry a median glutamate frequency of 0.186 against the naturals' 0.086 with 73% of them above the naturals' 90th percentile, no restriction in this cohort balances both sides on all covariates at once, and at 40 series against 35–76 clusters more data from this cohort cannot fix it — so "natural" here means natural **or compositionally natural-like**, and no narrower reading is licensed. **F12's own ProGen2 caveats do not travel onto the interaction, and carrying them there would be as much a defect as dropping them here**: the interaction is a within-arm difference between two sides of one file under a baseline no corpus is needed for, so BFD30 has no bearing on it, and ProGen2-small is read normally on it because the gate its earlier reading failed was a conjunction against a baseline, which this is not. **The verdict is `negative` under a rule frozen before any score existed** — beat every free and fragment baseline on the disjoint side *and* on the natural control, or the arm does not pass — and it is a null about **ranking against a baseline, not about an empty model**: the design-side correlation excludes zero, the per-design median is **+0.1078**, and **97 of 130** designs read positive. **Identification is per arm and may not be borrowed between arms.** ProtGPT2's declared pretraining corpus *is* the searched database, so its exclusion is identified; ProGen2-base and ProGen2-medium return the same two failures plus mutation position, but both saw BFD30, which was not searched, so a design absent from UniRef50 may still be in the corpus they read and their rows are bounds in the direction that **flatters** the model. **ProGen2-small fails its own control** — MODEL − hydropathy is +0.0304 [−0.0274, +0.0885] on the natural domains — so under the frozen rule its design side is **not read at all**, whatever it says: the pre-registered `uninterpretable_instrument_bound` is a statement about that interface and never a result about that model. The pre-registered strict companion, the 36 entirely zero-hit series, reproduces the verdict on every arm. This bounds one protein decoder's stability prediction against physics that needs no corpus, on sequences its corpus does not contain; it does **not** show that the model has learned biology, and it adjudicates no mechanism. Absence is certified against one UniRef50 snapshot, so the identified claim is a ProtGPT2 claim; and the phenotype is folding stability from one proteolysis assay, so nothing here speaks to function. **The surviving half was then stress-tested against the strongest fragment channel this corpus can hold. It stands, on ten channels, and it is materially narrower than the two rows above report — and the test bounded the certificate itself in a way that reaches beyond this row (EXP-R2-196).** *Taking the further-reaching part first: what the certificate excludes is alignable sequence, and essentially nothing below the alignment scale.* At fragment orders 3 through 6, **not one** fragment of any scored variant of the 130 certified zero-hit designs is absent from the searched corpus — zero unseen of 4,427,168 full-order positions at order 6, where the natural-domain control has four — and at order 7 the designs read **99.57%** of their full-order 7-mers present in UniRef50 against the naturals' **99.65%**. At fragment scale the corpus-disjoint referent is *more* completely covered than the natural domains it is contrasted with. So "retrieval excluded by construction" means **whole-sequence retrieval excluded** — which is the thing F10 could only estimate and subtract, and which no natural referent in this benchmark could have offered — and never meant corpus information excluded; every claim resting on the certificate inherits that. *The margin is order-dependent and loses two thirds of its size across the orders that can be computed.* Interpolated conditionals scoring every position run **+0.1590 → +0.1453 → +0.1113 → +0.0792 → +0.0516 [+0.0213, +0.0807]** on the designs from order 3 to order 7, with per-order steps still at full size at the top of the range; F12's own two channels reproduce exactly, and order is not confounded with the scoring convention — the convention change alone moves the contrast **+0.015 and +0.018** at orders 3 and 4, in the direction that flatters the model, against an order effect of **−0.107**. *The sweep stopped at a representational ceiling, not at a turning point.* Held-out cross-entropy on withheld corpus records falls monotonically through order 7 on both draws under both schemes, its per-order gains at the top of the range about three times those at the bottom and showing no flattening, while a dense order-8 count vector is 25.6 G cells and 204 GB and could not be built. Nothing here identifies where a fragment channel stops being a model, only that it had not by order 7, and **a linear fit reaching zero near order nine is an extrapolation past the last computable order rather than a prediction**. *The verdict rests on ten channels* — five orders by two smoothing schemes, both declared before scoring and both reported, neither selected — with ProtGPT2's series-clustered interval wholly above zero on every one. **The highest-order ProGen2 rows are bounds, and none of the three still shows a demonstrable margin there**: at order 7 base reads +0.0135 [−0.0109, +0.0400] and medium −0.0041 [−0.0280, +0.0198], both covering zero, and small −0.0421 [−0.0718, −0.0116], wholly below, the channel beating that arm outright. Two asymmetries run in opposite directions on those rows and neither is resolved: the channel counts only the searched corpus while those arms also read the unsearched BFD30, so it is an incomplete and therefore **generous** model of the statistics they saw, while their retrieval certificate is equally unidentified, which runs the other way. What remains unpriced is no longer the fragment family, which is now measured to the highest order a dense count vector holds, but a corpus channel of a different shape — a profile, an alignment-free embedding, or any longer-range dependency, none of which the order argument bounds. **Nothing in that amendment reaches this row's other findings**: the hydropathy and BLOSUM62 failures that produce the negative verdict rest on free baselines needing no corpus, the design-versus-natural interaction is a within-arm difference under one of them, the composition non-overlap bounding it is untouched, and the per-arm corpus identification is unchanged | EXP-R2-192, pre-registered before the scores existed and read against its own truth table; EXP-R2-193 for the interaction, pre-registered before any difference was computed and run at zero GPU over EXP-R2-192's own pinned scores — its pooled row is the **origin** and not confirmatory, its three ProGen2 arms are **blinded** rather than out-of-sample replication, and its three design families are a **generalisation** test over a partition of an estimate already taken across all 40 series, so none of the three axes is independent replication and the family split must not be upgraded into one. EXP-R2-196 for the fragment-order amendment, likewise pre-registered and zero-GPU, its higher-order counts verified byte-identical by digest to F12's own pinned background at orders 3 and 4. `results/transfer/designed_referent/`, over the certificate in `results/transfer/megascale_disjointness/` | **citable for ProtGPT2 at this interface; the ProGen2 rows are bounds on the retrieval claim only and ProGen2-small is not read on it; the design-versus-natural interaction is confirmed against length, is not hydropathy-specific, and is not separated from composition; the fragment-baseline margin holds to order 7 and is three times smaller there than the order-3 and order-4 rows show, with no turning point reached and the certificate excluding whole sequences rather than fragments; one benchmark, one phenotype** |

**Two readings sit beside F12 and neither is part of it.** *Refused rather than read:* the pre-declared topology companion resamples the design side at the dataset's own coarser unit, **6 units against the 8-unit floor**, so no percentile interval is published and no gate can pass; the seven "failed" gates its artefact shows for every arm are that refusal, not seven findings, and the dependence between series of one topology is bounded only by it. *What licenses comparing this stage with F10 at all:* restricted to the 64 ProteinGym Tsuboyama wild types, matched 64 of 64, this stage reads **+0.3683** for ProtGPT2 and **+0.3620** for ProGen2-medium against the +0.367 and +0.365 read off F10's own artefacts — a different code path over a different file (MegaScale parquet against ProteinGym CSV) agreeing to 0.003 or better.

Two items remain deliberately outside the register. The ProtGPT2 PAA head-retrieval **point value** is not citable — it is broadly dispersed across draws (0 to 8 hits over thirteen) and its median moved with K until K=13; cite the classification, not the number. And no additional part-1 modality claim is citable beyond F7, for the structural reason §2 records: the only family spanning both modalities is GPT-2, so every modality coefficient rests on ProtGPT2 alone. F10 and F12 are bounded results about a fitness-evaluation interface, not modality claims.

**A draw-count discrepancy in the evidence column, recorded rather than reconciled (2026-08-09).** F1 and D2.c's plan row cite "~55 arm-draws"; F1b(i) and F3 cite "62 on-condition arm-draws" for what reads as the same campaign. They are not the same population. "~55" is D2.c's own campaign count at closure; "62" is the EXP-R2-131 failure audit's draw list *before* the eight ByGPT5 draws were added to it, which is the count F1b(i)'s own "8 of 62 otherwise identical arm-draws" is taken over. The audit artefact `logs/paa_audit_seedfix/paa_failure_audit.json` now holds **70** on-condition arm-draws over twelve arms, 62 of them non-ByGPT5, so neither figure in the table is the current size of that file. These are provenance counts and no claim rests on them; no row is edited on this basis.

**The strongest directly measured part-2 result available from EXP-R2-071/072/077/079 is a limitation of the *method*, not of the transfer: an all-grid rank correlation between a head-prevalence census's score and a query-local causal readout is confounded with layer depth on every arm, the text controls included.** *This sentence read "the prefix-matching induction census is a causal proxy on text decoders and is not one on protein decoders" until 2026-08-09, when it was corrected for stating a scope its own next paragraph withdraws.* The narrowing is immediately below (EXP-R2-120, quantified by EXP-R2-115), and F5 above records the re-scoping. The evidence as originally measured is not withdrawn — it is the raw statistic, and the raw statistic is what is confounded.

> **SCOPE NARROWED — read this before the paragraph below (EXP-R2-120, quantified by EXP-R2-115).** The separation is **not identified against layer depth**, and most of it is that confound. The causal readout is depth-biased on every arm without exception (`r(layer, |effect|)` = +0.36 to +0.77, because the sender patch and the metric act at the query row only), and **ProtGPT2 is the sole arm whose census score *falls* with depth** (−0.387 against +0.00 to +0.50 elsewhere). Read on the K-invariant dispersion measure this document already adopted for the raw statistic — the gap in pooled boundary-arm standard deviations — the two statistics are not comparable in strength:
>
> | statistic | boundary arms | gap | in pooled boundary-arm sd |
> |---|---|---:|---:|
> | raw all-grid ρ | gpt2-xl vs ZymCTRL | +0.189…+0.246 | **+5.08 to +7.18** |
> | within-layer | gpt2-xl / gpt2-large vs **ProtGPT2** | +0.026…+0.069 | **+0.55 to +1.39** |
>
> **Depth-controlled the sign survives in all four conditions and the separation does not: it is inside one standard deviation of its own boundary arms in three of the four.** The boundary arms also change — ProtGPT2 is the panel minimum raw (−0.105) and the protein maximum within-layer (+0.357), a swing of +0.46 on one arm from holding depth fixed. **The defensible statement is a method limitation demonstrated on the text control as much as on the protein arms: an all-grid rank correlation between a census score and a query-local causal readout is confounded with depth.** The evidence as originally measured follows, and is not withdrawn — it is the raw statistic, and the raw statistic is what is confounded.

With every head patched rather than only the census's own selections, the Spearman correlation between census score and causal-effect magnitude separates the modalities completely — the text minimum (+0.371, gpt2-xl) exceeds every protein maximum (+0.282, ZymCTRL) in all **48** arm × condition cells on **seven** text and five protein arms, the matched pair included. **Two alternative explanations are ruled out by their own controls.** *Distribution mismatch:* `dialogpt-small`, a text decoder that §5.05(a) shows is off-distribution on the evaluation corpus at −4.08 nats, does **not** collapse (+0.518 to +0.640). *Architecture:* two rotary/GQA text lineages from other labs read +0.479 to +0.575, while ProtGPT2 — which shares gpt2-large's exact architecture — reads −0.226 to −0.006, so the separation tracks modality rather than model family (EXP-R2-079). The ordering also survives an independent corpus draw on the four arms that define the gap, with the matched pair separating *further* (EXP-R2-077). The failure mode is recall in the bulk of the grid, not at its top, and the consequence for this programme is direct — an unknown part of §4's head-count gap belongs to the instrument rather than to the models. This does **not** show that protein decoders have induction heads after all. **The margin is quoted against a measured spread (EXP-R2-093, extended to 39 draws by EXP-R2-095): the separation holds in all four conditions with each side taken at its most adverse draw** — gpt2-xl's *worst* draw exceeds ZymCTRL's *best* by **+0.108 to +0.142**. **EXP-R2-095 attacked this on its binding side and it got stronger:** gpt2-xl is the text minimum in every condition, and taking it from K=3 to K=7 moved its pooled all-grid value *up*, from +0.437 to +0.458, widening the panel separation to +0.221 on the eleven-arm panel it measured. *(The +0.216 recorded here until 2026-08-04 is EXP-R2-101's twelve-arm recomputation — +0.458 against +0.242 — not EXP-R2-095's figure; both stand, and the attribution was wrong.)* **One sub-clause is corrected rather than carried.** The earlier form — "each gap exceeds the draw range of both arms that define it" — now holds in **two of four** conditions, and the reason is a defect in the statistic, not in the claim: the expected range of *n* draws grows from ~1.69σ at n=3 to ~2.70σ at n=7, so an arm is penalised on that clause for having been measured *more*. Restated on a dispersion measure that does not grow with K, **the gap is 3.36 to 3.95 pooled boundary-arm standard deviations in all four conditions** (gpt2-xl σ 0.039–0.050 at K=7, ZymCTRL σ 0.020–0.024 at K=4). The quoted upper gap falls from +0.177 to +0.142 because the added draws sampled gpt2-xl's distribution more fully. **Protein arms are also not the noisier ones across draws** (median cell range 0.047 protein against 0.048 text, maximum 0.093 against 0.133), which is an independent failure of the attenuation objection EXP-R2-081 answered within runs. **What that separation is *about* is now much narrower than the sentence above suggests (EXP-R2-096).** The all-grid statistic is dominated by the 576 heads a census never reports, and on induction that stratum is at the noise floor on both arms — median |effect|/SE 0.99 on gpt2-large and 0.92 on ProtGPT2. Split by census rank, ProtGPT2 reads **+0.724 in the top 5%** against gpt2-large's +0.558, and its census top-20 retrieves **15.7/20** of the causally largest heads against the text control's **11.7/20**. The same shape holds on copy suppression (EXP-R2-094). So the headline measurement stands and the failure really is "recall in the bulk" — but **the bulk is where the effects are not measurable, and the part a census actually publishes does not fail.** The diagnostic §8 item 0 proposed to build on this statistic is refuted as a result; see that item.

**Extended to all twelve arms, and it is not a matched-pair artefact — but it does resolve into two different failures that the aggregate had been conflating (EXP-R2-101, CPU, existing artefacts).** Across the panel the all-grid separation holds (worst text arm +0.458 against best protein arm +0.242) and **every stratified version of it fails**: top 5% −0.561, top 20% −0.139, bulk 80% −0.016, with the four ProGen2/ProtGPT2 arms holding four of the five highest top-5% values measured on any arm. The reason an aggregate can separate when none of its strata do is heterogeneity — an all-grid Spearman penalises an arm that is valid at the top and invalid in the bulk more than one that is mediocre throughout — and **the four GPT-2/ProGen2-lineage protein arms are the four most stratified arms in the panel** (+0.526 to +1.014, against gpt2-xl's +0.395 next). On the matched pair, where architecture and top-5% stratum size are identical so no head-count or attenuation caveat applies, ProtGPT2's top-5%-minus-bulk spread is **+1.014 at K=7** against gpt2-large's **+0.292 at K=7**; gpt2-large's aggregate is representative of its own top (+0.509 against +0.508) and ProtGPT2's is not (−0.108 against +0.749). **So the modality gap is substantially about heterogeneity rather than uniformly worse recall, and it conflates two situations.** *Mode 1* (ProtGPT2 and all three ProGen2 arms): valid where published, invalid in the bulk that is never reported. *Mode 2* (**ZymCTRL alone**): mediocre in every stratum **and failing to retrieve** — top-5% **+0.131** and hit@20 **2.9/20** against gpt2-large's 11.4 and ProtGPT2's 14.1 on the same 720-head grid. Taking it from K=4 to K=6 (EXP-R2-102) *sharpened* it rather than softening it. **The definition is retrieval, not the spread, and that correction is forced:** at K=4 llama-3.2-3b has the panel's *lowest* spread (−0.079, below ZymCTRL's −0.057) while retrieving **8.5/20** at 14× its chance level, so a flat census can still be a usable one. A negative spread alone does not identify an untrustworthy census; failing to retrieve does. **ZymCTRL is the only arm on which "this census's output is untrustworthy" is supported by the evidence**, and it is separately the arm irreducibly excluded from D2.c's shared window. Two comparability corrections are applied rather than assumed away: `hit@20` has a grid-dependent ceiling of n/20 and is compared only within a grid size, and the top-5% stratum is floored at 8 heads, so the 144–192-head arms are thin evidence.

> **EVERY EXP-R2-116 CORRECTION IN THE PARAGRAPH BELOW IS ITSELF UNDER WITHDRAWAL (EXP-R2-119, read 2026-08-04).** EXP-R2-116 recomputed D2.c "from the retained per-instance matrices" with ProtGPT2's FASTA line-break instances excluded, and reported a sign flip from +0.2145 to −0.0880. **That correction is one-sided and could only ever have been one-sided.** The causal side retains a per-instance matrix (`delta_m_gap`, 720 × 800) and can be filtered; the census side does not — `paa_specific_matched_per_sequence` is `(sequences, layers, heads)`, already averaged over the instances inside each sequence, so no layout instance can be removed from the census score after a run. The figure therefore correlated a layout-**contaminated** census score against a layout-**clean** causal effect. Reproduced exactly on the three source draws: raw +0.2260 / +0.2169 / +0.1833 against one-sided −0.0953 / −0.1213 / −0.0708, mean −0.0958 against the logged −0.0880. **EXP-R2-118 then repaired the guard at the root, and EXP-R2-119 rebuilt both pools under it: ProtGPT2 reads +0.1806 and gpt2-large +0.4425 at ban 3 / n=600, both passing A1.** The rebuild carries **0** layout instances where the old pools carried 3064/10040 and 979/21649, and ProtGPT2's contamination sits on exactly the side the offline pass could not touch — the Spearman between its contaminated and its rebuilt census score is +0.447, against +0.936 on gpt2-large. `logs/drivers/d2c_layoutfix.sh` pre-registered the tie-break before the result existed: *"If the two disagree, the rebuild is the one to believe and the offline figure is the artefact."* **What EXP-R2-116 established and keeps:** the missing layout guard was a real defect, ProtGPT2's PAA pool was 28–33% line breaks, and the A1-verdict discipline breach it recorded is real. **What it loses:** the withdrawal of "two answers inside one modality", the "STRENGTHENED … gap +0.3643, ratio 2.49" restatement, and every layout-corrected column derived from the one-sided pass — including EXP-R2-117's, which was computed on the same column. **No replacement value may be quoted yet:** the rebuild is at K=1 per arm and evidence-discipline rule 4 admits no single-draw point estimate. EXP-R2-121 is taking the matched pair to K≥2; the ProGen2 arms are additionally condition-mismatched (published at n=200, a condition EXP-R2-118 shows is not available to ProtGPT2 at all) and are owed a re-run at n=600. **Read the paragraph below as the record of the reasoning, not as current values.**

**A second part-2 result now stands beside it, on a different mechanism and a different statistic — and unlike the one above it needs no census at all (EXP-R2-099).** D2.c was posed as *"does effect size separate arms where prevalence cannot?"*, and every statistic run against it until now answered a *ranking* question instead, because ranking is what L22 defined. Answered directly, from the instrument's own A4 record, and now on **ten arms** (EXP-R2-105/107/109): **the absolute effect does not separate the modalities at all.** Excluding dialogpt-small (below), the strongest prediction-addressed head moves the margin by 0.0156–0.0626 nats on six text arms and 0.0089–0.0159 on four protein arms — **ProtGPT2's 0.0159 exceeds gpt2-xl's 0.0156**, so the two scales touch. **~~What separates … is the denominator: mean clean M-gap 0.270–0.518 on text against 0.996–3.297 on protein.~~ WITHDRAWN (EXP-R2-116).** ProtGPT2's 3.297 is its FASTA line-break tail: its PAA-pool **median** margin is **+0.021** against gpt2-large's **+0.316**, making it the *least* confident arm on the panel by medians, not the most. The claim that *protein decoders operate at 2–12× larger decision margins at prediction-addressed positions* is false of the only modality-identifying protein arm and is withdrawn. It remains true of the ProGen2 arms, for which no matched text control exists. **And that difference belongs to the census's instance filter rather than to the models (EXP-R2-111, corrected by EXP-R2-112).** Measured at *all* scored positions on the same cohorts — no census, no knockout, no head grid — the same margin **does not separate the modalities at all**: text 1.466–1.913 against protein 1.110–1.852, overlapping, and on medians the protein arms are the *less* confident ones. The census's PAA instances, by contrast, sit at **0.18–0.26** of their arm's typical margin on four text arms and **0.90–1.78** on four protein arms. ~~Text decoders are four to five times less confident where a token recurs.~~ **Retracted: under a uniform definition of recurrence every arm is *more* confident where a token recurs (ratio 1.13–13.30), so the effect is not about recurrence.** The census filter excludes induction targets and requires a decoy pool and a distance range; on text that strips the easy repeats and leaves genuinely ambiguous cases, and on protein it does not. **So the D2.c denominator gap is a property of the evaluation interface — the instrument's instance selection interacting with modality — not of the models.** **Measured directly (EXP-R2-113):** the margin at the census's PAA instances, as a fraction of the margin at *all* recurrent positions of the same arm, is **0.143–0.199 on six text arms and 0.283–0.927 on four protein arms** — disjoint. The census selects positions five to seven times harder than a typical recurrent position on every text arm, and only 1.1–3.5× harder on the protein arms. Their ratio therefore separates too — text 3.19–18.40% against protein 0.49–0.89%, no overlap — but the result must be stated as *the same absolute intervention is a much smaller fraction of a protein decoder's decision margin*, **not** as "the mechanism is weaker in protein models", which the absolute scale refutes. ~~An alphabet-based account of this was offered and is withdrawn (EXP-R2-112).~~ **The test was a byte-level *English* decoder, and it refutes the alphabet reading**: ByGPT5, text modality with a 384-symbol vocabulary and a 0.83 recurrent fraction, reads 1.47–1.52 on the uniform statistic — inside the BPE text band of 1.13–1.71 and below every protein arm. A coarse alphabet does not make a text decoder behave like a protein one. **The uniform recurrence statistic is also a poor modality instrument in its own right** and no modality claim is made on it: it separates (text 1.13–1.71, protein 1.77–13.30) but its cross-modality gap of 0.064 sits against a within-protein spread of **11.53**, a ratio of **0.0055**, because the recurrent *fraction* is a tokenisation property spanning 0.09 to 0.90 and drives the statistic — ProtGPT2's multi-residue BPE makes exact recurrence rare and therefore highly informative when it happens. Two biases in the statistic run *toward* the null of no protein deficit — A4 is a maximum over the grid, which favours the arm with more heads (720 against ProGen2's 432) and the arm with noisier estimates (ProGen2) — and a third fact runs the other way and is recorded rather than buried: **A3 passes on every arm, and ProtGPT2 carries more confidently suppressive heads than gpt2-large** (206 against 127 of 720). A suppressive population exists on protein. *Two limitations, both measured rather than anticipated.* The normalisation is the module's own (`delta_m_gap_relative_to_clean`), but a larger clean margin also means the selected positions are simply easier to predict, so the ratio mixes mechanism strength with task confidence and cannot by itself separate the two. And **its denominator is a free-running quantity that can cross zero**: dialogpt-small, the off-distribution text arm, has a mean clean M-gap of **−0.785** — at most PAA-selected positions it is not predicting the antecedent token — so A4's division by `max(|mean|, 1e-9)` leaves its 5.00% without a defined sign. That arm is excluded from the size comparison and the reason is recorded; a mean is not a safe summary of this denominator.

**What the census contributes to that picture is *directional*, and it is a text-side property measured on two text arms (EXP-R2-099, EXP-R2-105/106).** The module's convention is explicit — a positive ΔM-gap means the head was *suppressing* — and the audit pre-registered the concern that "a magnitude ranking conflates suppressive heads with promoting ones" (§ below, EXP-R2-078 note). Measured: on gpt2-large the census top is **19–22% suppressive across the top 1/5/10/20%**, against its own control heads at exactly **0.50** and its own grid at 0.41 — a ~21-point enrichment for *promoting* heads, stable over four cuts, two conditions and nine draws, with the top-5% median effect at −0.00242 and a head-level bootstrap CI excluding zero. On ProtGPT2 the top is **not enriched in either direction** relative to its own grid (0.61 against 0.67; median +0.00106, CI [−0.00073, +0.00324], includes zero). So what transfers to ProtGPT2 is a magnitude ranking; what does not is any information about which way the head pushes. **A second text arm confirms it at K=3: gpt2-medium reads −0.128, and the two text arms span −0.13 to −0.22 against the four protein arms' −0.015 to +0.038** — a single draw of gpt2-medium had read −0.05 and briefly forced the narrower wording. gpt2-xl remains the independent check.

The earlier holder of that position remains valid and narrower: `paa_specific` does not provide a valid cheap ranking screen for copy-suppression on GPT-2-large, despite the causal mechanism itself being detectable. Neither result establishes that head-prevalence censuses generally have a narrow domain. Measurability is now a *demonstrated* contributor to an apparent transfer gap on one mechanism, rather than a candidate one.

---

## PART I — Differences between text and protein generative models

### 2. The panel

Twelve autoregressive decoders under one code path, each fed in the format it was trained on. Eleven were staged and load-checked on GPFS in EXP-R2-058, ten of them with full source-to-GPFS SHA-256 verification while Qwen2.5-0.5B was recorded as byte-size checked only; ProGen2-small was load-checked on the pod and admitted in EXP-R2-068. `scripts/transfer/panel_contract.py` is the declaration — this table is a reading of it, not a second copy.

| arm | modality | layers x width | tokenisation | corpus / conditioning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| gpt2 | text | 12 x 768 | BPE 50257 | WebText |
| gpt2-medium | text | 24 x 1024 | BPE 50257 | WebText |
| gpt2-large | text | 36 x 1280 | BPE 50257 | WebText |
| gpt2-xl | text | 48 x 1600 | BPE 50257 | WebText |
| dialogpt-small | text | 12 x 768 | BPE 50257 | Reddit dialogue |
| qwen2.5-0.5b | text | 24 x 896 | BPE 151936 | Qwen2.5 mixture |
| llama-3.2-3b | text | 28 x 3072 | BPE 128256 | Llama-3 web corpus |
| protgpt2 | protein | 36 x 1280 | multi-residue BPE 50257 | UniRef50, FASTA |
| zymctrl | protein | 36 x 1280 | residue 458 | EC-labelled, tag-conditioned |
| progen2-base | protein | 27 x 1536 | residue 32 | corpus contrast partner |
| progen2-medium | protein | 27 x 1536 | residue 32 | corpus contrast partner |
| progen2-small | protein | 12 x 1024 | residue 32 | UniRef90+BFD30; protein-side scale rung |

**Design properties that make specific contrasts identifiable:**

- `gpt2-large` / `protgpt2` — identical architecture, depth, width and vocabulary size, and identical parameter count — **774,030,080**, verified from both checkpoints (2026-08-01); the previously recorded 773,891,840 was wrong by 138,240, though the *identity* it asserts is exact. The matched modality pair.
- `gpt2` / `gpt2-medium` / `gpt2-large` / `gpt2-xl` — within-lineage scale ladder, 124M to 1558M, holding architecture, tokeniser and corpus fixed.
- `gpt2` / `dialogpt-small` — same architecture and size, different corpus. Text-side corpus contrast.
- `progen2-base` / `progen2-medium` — matched architecture and parameter count, different corpus. Protein-side corpus contrast.
- `qwen2.5-0.5b`, `llama-3.2-3b` — cross-lab lineage contrast at comparable scale.

**Known structural limit:** the only model family spanning both modalities is GPT-2, with five text arms against one protein arm. Every modality coefficient is therefore carried by ProtGPT2 alone.

**Three further declared arms sit outside this table; two of them also sit outside the campaign.** `src/transfer/arms.py` declares fifteen members. `panel_contract.CAMPAIGN_PANEL` schedules **thirteen** — the twelve above **plus `bygpt5-medium-en`** — and records the remaining **two**, `bygpt5-small-en` and `bygpt5-base-en`, in `panel_members_not_staged` with the reason: they are refused by `paa_census` on head grids too small for `hit@k` to distinguish a working census from one that returns the grid, and no other campaign stage admits a `t5_decoder` arm. **Corrected 2026-08-10:** the sentence above read "schedules the twelve above and records the other three … in `panel_members_not_staged`" until today, which contradicted its own next sentence and the code — verified against `panel_contract.CAMPAIGN_PANEL` (13) and `PANEL_MEMBERS_NOT_STAGED` (2). The 2026-08-09 correction below repaired the capability and architecture clauses of this paragraph and left its arithmetic behind. **Corrected 2026-08-09:** this paragraph said until then that all three sit outside the campaign, that they carry `budget` and `lens` capability only, and that `t5_decoder` is in no measuring module's architecture declaration. All three statements are now false — `bygpt5-medium-en` is a full D2.c campaign arm (F2, D2.e, EXP-R2-129), `arms.py` grants it `{budget, circuits, lens}`, and `prediction_addressed.PAA_ARCHITECTURES` carries `t5_decoder` — and they were made false by the work that admitted the byte-level control. They are not decoration: §1 quotes ByGPT5 as the byte-level *English* control that refutes an alphabet reading of the recurrence statistic. But the same arm cannot be extended to L22, and the refusal is explicit rather than silent — `path_patching.require_supported_layout` raises because `t5_decoder` has no declared attention output projection. Answering alphabet-versus-modality on L22 would need that declaration verified against the module's head slicing, which is a change to a frozen instrument for one arm family (EXP-R2-114). Recorded so the question is not mistaken for unanswerable in principle.

### 3. What was claimed, and what survived

| claim | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| Fewer induction heads, not weaker | **survives on the matched pair only; the panel-level form is refuted** | tail-only; probe-dependent; n=1 protein identification — and, from 2026-08-12, a byte-level *text* decoder sits inside the protein range on the published statistic (EXP-R2-171, §4) |
| QK/OV dissociation is modality-specific | **retracted** | GPT-2-lineage property; Qwen 0.357 and Llama 0.445 sit at chance beside ProtGPT2 0.361, while ProGen2 copies at 1.000 |
| Attention pathway more indirect in protein | **refuted** | path patching, matched pair +0.020 CI [−0.121, +0.166] |
| Relational structure readable only from attention | **retracted** | cohort-selection artefact; margin fell ~0.10 → 0.03–0.05 under seeded permutation, ~0.03 over separation-only control |
| Tokenisation explains the modality gap | **retracted** | plug-in unigram estimator biased shares by up to +1.02 nats |
| Aperture / J-Lens axis separates modalities | **null** | data contrast exceeds modality effect; instrument too noisy to resolve anything |
| MLP-share modality coefficient | **underpowered** | collapses under tokenisation adjustment at n=9 |
| Induction head-count deficit is explained by within-protein homology | **not supported within the measured protein cohorts; cross-modal contribution unresolved** | corrected full-UniRef50 control finds overlapping head-count strata and a synthetic negative control, but no genuine low-homology stratum or symmetric GPT-2 corpus comparison |
| Induction deficit is exact-repeat artefact | **refuted** | survives approximate-repeat criterion |
| ProtGPT2 is off-distribution | **retracted (own defect)** | input-rendering bug; EOT + 60-col wrap worth 1.42 nats/token |

### 4. Final status of the induction result

> ### RETIRED — the panel-level ordering, and the statistic built to rescue it (EXP-R2-171, 2026-08-12)
>
> **Read this before anything below.** EXP-R2-155 put the panel-level half of this section at risk and named the instrument that would settle it: score the same heads on length- and composition-matched probes carrying no planted repeat, and read every arm against its own null instead of against uniform attention. That instrument was pre-registered with its decision rule, its voiding gates and its bounds fixed before any number existed, then run on fifteen arms at 256 probes — sixteen times the published probe count — under `27_collision_null_census.py`. **Every pre-declared gate passed:** on every arm and both null draws the mean same-token antecedent count is identical between the real probes and their nulls to every reported digit, the fraction of scored pairs whose key really follows a copy of the query is 1.000 on the real probes against 0.02–0.13 on the nulls, and the two null draws differ. The null is matched and it destroyed the repeat. **Its own statistic is nevertheless void, and the question it was built to settle is answered without it.**
>
> **What the census contributes durably is a ceiling, not a count.** Measured per arm on the probes this section actually scores, rather than on a corpus at some other length, the identity ceiling is the attention a head disambiguating on single-token identity could place on the aligned key while splitting evenly across every earlier copy of the query token:
>
> | group | arms | same-token antecedents | identity ceiling | fraction of heads above the fixed 0.10 cut |
> |---|---|---:|---:|---:|
> | BPE text | gpt2 lineage, dialogpt-small, qwen2.5-0.5b, llama-3.2-3b | 1.61–1.67 | **0.842–0.852** | 0.0402–0.1597 |
> | multi-residue BPE protein | protgpt2 | 1.06 | **0.976** | 0.0181 |
> | **byte-level text** | **bygpt5-medium-en** | **6.82** | **0.321** | **0.0208** |
> | residue protein | zymctrl, progen2 x3 | 6.59–6.61 | **0.229–0.231** | 0.0000–0.0260 |
>
> **So the fixed 0.10 cut asks a residue arm for 44% of its attainable maximum and a BPE text arm for 12%.** That is the confound EXP-R2-155 predicted, now measured on the right probes.
>
> **Three statuses follow, and they must not be collapsed into one.**
>
> **1. The panel-level ordering is REFUTED as a modality contrast, correction-independently.** Among the original twelve arms "worst text above best protein" holds on the fixed cut by 1.55x — llama-3.2-3b 0.0402 against ProGen2-small 0.0260. Admitting one byte-level **text** decoder breaks it: bygpt5-medium-en scores **0.0208**, below the weakest text arm and inside the protein range, with bygpt5-base-en at 0.0278 on its upper edge. No correction enters this reading and none of the failed corrections below touches it. **The statement is retired permanently and may not be quoted as a modality contrast again**; the matched-pair ratio in status 3 below is the only panel-level induction claim this section still makes. *One arm cannot isolate the cause:* ByGPT5 differs from the BPE text arms in architecture, pretraining corpus and scale as well as in alphabet — the irreducible limit EXP-R2-129 already carried. Two things bound it. Scale does not explain it, since dialogpt-small at 124M reads 0.0694, 3.3x higher. And the measured ceilings predict a 2.6x depression (0.842/0.321) against an observed 1.9x relative to the weakest BPE text arm, so alphabet is quantitatively sufficient for the gap to the text band.
>
> **2. Whether the alphabet *fully* explains the byte arm has MOVED and has NOT CLOSED.** The null-calibrated count is retired (below), so its disagreement with the ceiling-normalised reading is no longer evidence about that arm — one of the two parties to it is invalid. What remains is the ceiling-normalised effect size on its own: read at 0.11 of each arm's own measured ceiling, bygpt5-medium-en is **0.0417**, interval-separated from **every** protein arm including ProGen2-small at 0.0365, and interval-overlapping **llama-3.2-3b at 0.0432 and nothing else**. Read alone, that says the alphabet accounts for the byte arm's deficit within the resolution available. It is **one reading resting on one mechanism assumption** — the single-token-identity head the ceiling is defined by, which bounds nothing about a head using multi-token context — and not the agreement of two independent corrections that would have closed the question. ByGPT5's architecture and corpus differences remain unaddressed by any of it.
>
> **3. The matched pair is UNTOUCHED and is reproduced independently.** gpt2-large 0.0986 against ProtGPT2 0.0181 is **5.46x**, reproducing the published seeded-census figure to three significant figures on a run with sixteen times the probes. It reads **5.62x** ceiling-normalised and **2.47–2.55x** studentised, text above protein on every reading taken. And it is *anti*-confounded: ProtGPT2's identity ceiling is **0.976** against gpt2-large's **0.842** — higher, because multi-residue BPE makes token recurrence rarer than in text — so the collision objection predicts ProtGPT2 should score *above* gpt2-large, and it scores 5.46x below.
>
> **A head count read as "excess distinguishable from its own null" is retired with cause, and that is a methodological finding rather than a caveat.** Four floors were specified for that count and each was defended on its own grounds: the family-wise maximum of the null-versus-null difference; a position-matched decoy floor; a reflected negative-tail floor; and a studentised family-wise maximum, the textbook fix for the heteroscedasticity the first three were argued over. Three of the four could be tested against the pre-declared specificity gate — the same probes, the same nulls and the same cut applied one position off the aligned key — and **all three failed it**: the decoy counts 61–138% of the prefix-matching count under the raw cut, 29–111% under the reflected tail and 81–139% under studentisation, against a declared ceiling of 50%. The fourth floor's gate is vacuous by construction, because its cut *is* the decoy maximum, so it cannot be quoted as passing either. **The diagnosis is not in the floor.** Offset-specificity is intact where the signal is — peak prefix-matching excess 0.87–0.95 against peak offset-two excess 0.006–0.41 — and what saturates the count is the weak tail: at 256 probes a head with an excess of 0.001 and a standard error of 0.0002 is four standard errors from its null and is counted, on the aligned key and on the decoy alike, because the permutation removes the repeat entirely and nearly every head responds to that a little. **A significance cut against a matched null is a *detection* criterion, and counting induction heads needs an *effect-size* criterion.** The same artefacts show the gap plainly: the significance readings put 20–85% of every arm's heads above the cut and the effect-size reading 0.3–17%. Three principled corrections disagreeing is the finding, and no fourth cut is specified.
>
> **Bounds.** Synthetic probe only — it is the probe the panel comparison was read on and the only one a ByGPT5 arm can enter, because the natural-repeat construction needs a fast tokenizer to align a repeat in symbol space and ByGPT5's is not one. A natural-repeat collision null is a separate measurement and was not run, so the refutation in status 1 is established on the primary probe and not on all three. One cohort draw, one probe count, one copy length; the null-calibrated count is probe-count-dependent and is not comparable to a run at another count in any case. `bygpt5-small-en` and `bygpt5-base-en` were scored as unqualified supporting rungs — their cohorts have never been through `cohort_power` — and carry no verdict on their own; bygpt5-medium-en is the deciding arm and is qualified at +2.462 nats.
>
> *An unplanned replication, recorded because it is checkable and because it costs one line.* The dispatch retries left a second complete text lane on disk from an attempt whose protein lane died on a degenerate head. It is superseded and labelled as such in the log rather than left to be inferred from two timestamps, and comparing it with the reading is free: the two lanes are **identical on every studentised count at every level, on both statistics, and on every per-head t across all nine text arms**, differing only in their timestamps and in two diagnostic fields the repair added. So the degenerate-head repair changed nothing on the text side — which its own reported bound predicted, the largest excess set aside on any text arm being 7.83e-19 times chance attention — and the text lane is replicated across two independent dispatches of one configuration. Neither fact was designed, and an accidental replication of one lane is not a replication of the census.

**Threshold robustness (synthetic probe) — passes.** Ordering holds at 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30 and the data-driven cut, and continuously across raw cuts 0.00525–0.4196, 62% of the informative grid with all four fixed cuts interior. The 0.10 threshold was not cherry-picked.

**Distributional separation — fails.** Pooled AUC 0.595; 8 of 12 pairs above 0.5; **0 of 12** show stochastic dominance; quantile dominance fails at q50, q99.5 and q100; model-level median test p = 0.257. ProGen2-medium's best head reaches 87.6x uniform, beating every text arm but two. **The result is a tail statement, not a distributional one.**

> ### D1.b answered — the battery re-run on the seeded 817-of-817 census (EXP-R2-073)
>
> Those figures came from a results tree that no longer exists; `12_induction_robustness.py` was hard-wired to it and raised `FileNotFoundError` on every path, so the instrument D1.b needed had to be repaired before the question could be asked. It now takes its source as an argument. Re-run on `results/transfer_20260730/panel12/`, synthetic probe, cut 0.10, on the same eleven arms:
>
> | statistic | recorded above | re-run on the seeded census |
> |---|---|---|
> | pooled AUC | 0.595 | **0.624** |
> | pairs above 0.5 | 8 / 12 | **20 / 28** (min 0.400, median 0.581, max 0.729) |
> | stochastic dominance | 0 / 12 | **2 / 28** |
> | quantile dominance | fails at q50, q99.5, q100 | fails at the same three — **and separates at q75, q90, q95, q97.5, q99** |
> | survival grid | 62% | 61.9% |
> | model-level exact test | p = 0.0286 (fraction) | **at the floor, 1/330 = 0.00303, complete separation** on fraction, mean and q99; the median statistic does not separate |
> | ladder slope / decade | −0.272 [−0.455, −0.088] | **−0.268 [−0.515, −0.020]** |
>
> **The conclusion is unchanged in direction and less absolute in wording.** "0 of 12 show stochastic dominance" cannot be restated as "none": two of twenty-eight pairs now do. And the middle of the distribution is not as featureless as the old summary implied — the ordering separates at every quantile from q75 to q99 and fails only at the median and in the extreme upper tail, where counts are single heads. **It is still a tail statement rather than a distributional one**, and the reason is now visible: the separation lives between the 75th and 99th percentiles of the per-head score, not across the distribution.
>
> **One published figure changes materially.** "Only **25.4%** of the modality indicator survives projection" was computed by dividing by an *uncentred* sum of squares, whose ceiling is n_protein/n rather than 1 — the statistic could not reach 1 even if scale and lineage explained nothing about modality. On the centred denominator, which is one minus the R² of the modality indicator on the nuisance block, the same fit gives **36.3%**. **This block is authoritative for the decomposition; §4's paragraph below is the superseded reading and points here.**
>
> **Two of the three increments moved as well, and "increments are unchanged" — the wording carried here until 2026-08-09 — was wrong.** The same design re-fitted on the seeded census gives modality **+0.270** against §4's **+0.220**, lineage **+0.053** against **+0.061**, and scale **+0.003**, which is the only one genuinely unchanged. The older values are not deleted: they are the file-order census's, and they stay in §4 as the record of what moved. Re-derived from `results/transfer_20260730/panel12/` on the ten arms the design uses — ZymCTRL has no logarithm and ProGen2-small post-dates the fit — which reproduces 25.4% as the uncentred ratio and 36.3% as the centred one from one fit, so both figures and both increment sets are the same design read two ways and on two censuses.
>
> **And the 2.34x scale-adjusted shortfall is withdrawn as an interval verdict.** `lineage_scale_ladder.predict()` extrapolated the GPT-2 line onto every other arm and published `observed_below_prediction_interval` for all of them, on an interval that prices residual scatter among four GPT-2 rungs and prices nothing about a different lineage, tokeniser or corpus. `in_fitted_lineage` is now carried through and the verdict is withheld off-lineage. The point ratio stands as descriptive; the interval statement does not. Independently, the arm that anchored the old comparison was `dialogpt-small`, which §5.05(a) retracts as off-distribution.

**Probe dependence — fails on the best probe.**

| probe | ordering |
|---|---|
| synthetic | holds at every threshold |
| natural exact | breaks at 0.20, 0.30 |
| **natural approximate** | **inverts at 0.10** (dialogpt-small 0.0000, ProtGPT2 0.0097), p = 0.114 |

`circuits.py` records the natural probe as the one to trust for wrapped arms and `scaling.py` declares it primary; the headline table used the synthetic probe. The natural approximate criterion is the one Pomerants et al. showed subsumes exact detection.

> **The inversion at 0.10 was an artefact of the repeat cohort's draw, and does not reproduce (EXP-R2-068).** The census's *repeat* cohorts were never seeded: under the approximate criterion the stage took **32 of 817** matching proteins and **32 of 968** matching documents in corpus file order — a four per cent head-of-file prefix on the cohort that carries this entire result. Re-run on a seeded draw at the whole matching population (**817 of 817** protein, so on the protein side it is a census and order cannot matter; 817 of 1022 text), on all eleven arms:
>
> | probe | t = 0.05 | t = 0.10 | t = 0.20 | t = 0.30 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | synthetic | holds | holds | holds | holds |
> | natural exact | holds | holds | holds | **inverts** |
> | natural approximate | holds | **holds** | **inverts** | **inverts** |
>
> At the headline threshold the ordering now holds on **all three probes**. `dialogpt-small`'s natural-approximate fraction, the 0.0000 that produced the recorded inversion, reads **0.0278** on the seeded cohort; the worst text arm (llama-3.2-3b, 0.0268) sits above the best protein arm (ProtGPT2, 0.0181). Matched pair at 0.10: **5.46x** synthetic (against the recorded 5.38x), 4.07x natural exact, 2.92x natural approximate.
>
> **The probe-dependence problem is reduced, not removed.** It moves to the permissive-tail thresholds: natural approximate inverts at 0.20 and 0.30, natural exact at 0.30. Both inversions are driven by the worst text arm reaching *exactly zero* while a protein arm retains one or two heads out of 720–1728 — a small-count tail effect, not a distributional statement, and the same reason the distributional separation fails. Nothing here converts the result into a modality claim: the structural n = 1 limit of §2 and the corpus-repeat confound of the closing paragraph are untouched. Artefacts: `results/transfer_20260730/induction_seeded/`.

> ### The twelfth arm breaks the synthetic probe, and one head decides the headline (EXP-R2-073)
>
> The table above is the **eleven**-arm panel. ProGen2-small was admitted after it was written, and it is the best protein arm on every probe. Re-derived on all twelve arms from `results/transfer_20260730/panel12/`:
>
> | probe | t = 0.05 | t = 0.10 | t = 0.20 | t = 0.30 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | synthetic | holds | holds | **inverts** | **inverts** |
> | natural exact | holds | holds | **inverts** | inverts |
> | natural approximate | holds | holds | inverts | inverts |
>
> **"Synthetic holds at every threshold" is panel-dependent and no longer true.** At 0.20 the worst text arm is llama-3.2-3b at 0.0238 against ProGen2-small at 0.0260; at 0.30, 0.0179 against 0.0208. Neither inversion is the zero-arm artefact the paragraph above invokes — llama's values are non-zero, and the inverting protein arm is ProGen2-small in every cell. The recorded explanation covers the eleven-arm inversions and does not cover these.
>
> **At the headline threshold the ordering still holds on all three probes, by less than one head.** On the natural-approximate probe the margin is llama-3.2-3b **0.0268** against ProGen2-small **0.0260** — 0.0008, where one head of ProGen2-small's 192 is **0.0052**. Six heads instead of five inverts it. ProGen2-small contributes **five heads** to every probe, and the same five heads set the protein-side scale slope. The headline ordering on the probe the literature says to trust is therefore decided by a single head on a single arm.
>
> This does not overturn the result — the matched pair remains 5.46x and the protein arms sit at 0.007–0.026 against text 0.027–0.216 — but it fixes its precision. ~~**The defensible statement is the matched-pair ratio and the panel-level pattern, not the worst-text-above-best-protein ordering**, which no longer survives its own threshold sweep and turns on one head at the cut where it does.~~ **Superseded 2026-08-12: the panel-level pattern goes too, and it goes at the headline threshold rather than at a permissive one.** This block located the precision limit in a single head of one protein arm; EXP-R2-171 relocated it in the alphabet, by admitting a byte-level *text* decoder that lands inside the protein range at the headline cut. The defensible statement is now the matched-pair ratio alone — see the retirement block at the head of this section.

> **The text-side scale slope does not transport to protein, and the scale adjustment below over-corrects (EXP-R2-068).** Admitting ProGen2-small gave the protein side its first within-lineage scale contrast — 151M against ProGen2-medium's 765M, architecture, residue tokeniser and UniRef90+BFD30 mixture all held. Measured on the seeded census at threshold 0.10, protein cohorts being the whole matching population:
>
> | probe | text slope / decade (GPT-2 ladder, 4 rungs) | protein slope / decade (ProGen2, 2 rungs) | ratio |
> |---|---:|---:|---:|
> | synthetic | −0.0735 | **−0.0172** | 4.3x shallower |
> | natural exact | −0.0572 | **−0.0172** | 3.3x shallower |
> | natural approximate | −0.0446 | **−0.0238** | 1.9x shallower |
>
> ProGen2-small reads 0.0260 on all three probes (5 heads of 192) against ProGen2-medium's 0.0139 / 0.0139 / 0.0093 — so the **direction transports**: a larger protein decoder also puts a smaller fraction of heads in the upper tail. The **magnitude does not**. The 2.34x scale-matched restatement below was computed by carrying the text ladder's slope onto the protein side, which nothing had tested; a slope 3–4x shallower removes correspondingly less of the raw matched-pair gap. *(That gap was written here as "5.45x", which is neither of this document's two measured values — **5.38x [5.23, 5.54]** on the file-order census, §4's table, and **5.46x** on the seeded census that replaced it. Recorded as unreconciled and corrected to the unquantified form on 2026-08-10: the argument is about the slope, and nothing rests on the third digit.)* **Scale therefore explains *less* of the shortfall than recorded, not more, and the 2.34x figure should be read as a lower bound on the adjusted shortfall rather than as its value.**
>
> Two rungs give a slope and no curvature, and no interval; ProGen2-small and ProGen2-medium differ in depth and width together, exactly as the text ladder's rungs do. At matched scale the protein corpus contrast is small: ProGen2-base 0.0116 against ProGen2-medium 0.0139 on the synthetic probe, both 765M. Artefacts: `results/transfer_20260730/panel12/`.
>
> **The shallower-protein-slope pattern replicates on an unrelated statistic, with a fixed-architecture data control (EXP-R2-068).** `estimand_power` asks a different question of a different measurement — what fraction of the 76 ablation estimands is *powered*, rather than what fraction of heads is in the prefix-matching tail — and the same shape appears:
>
> | lineage | rungs | powered fraction | slope |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | GPT-2 | 124M / 355M / 774M / 1.56B | 88.2% → 73.7% → 68.4% → 63.2% | **−22.5 points/decade** |
> | ProGen2 | 151M / 765M | 100.0% → 94.7% | **−7.5 points/decade** |
>
> Same sign, **3.0x shallower**, landing inside the 1.9–4.3x band the census gives. The text ladder is monotone across four rungs over a 12x parameter range, so its slope is not two-point noise. **And the protein rungs now carry a control the census version lacked:** ProGen2-base and ProGen2-medium are architecturally identical here — both 1536-wide, 27-layer, 32-symbol — so they differ only in pretraining mixture, and they read 92.1% against 94.7%. A corpus difference at fixed architecture moves this statistic by 2.6 points where a 5x size difference moves it 5.3, which is what licenses reading the lineage slope as a size effect rather than a data effect.
>
> *What this does not show.* Powered fraction is a property of the estimand battery's sensitivity on each arm, not of the arm's mechanism, and a ceiling is in play — ProGen2-small is at 100.0%, so its rung cannot move up and the protein slope is bounded below in magnitude by that alone. The modality ranges themselves **overlap heavily** (text 0.0–96.1%, protein 65.8–100.0%) and separate nothing. `dialogpt-small` reads 0 powered estimands and 0 context-valid estimands, which is §5.05(a)'s off-distribution arm again and is why the text range reaches zero.

**Scale — real, and does not explain the descriptive shortfall.** The within-lineage ladder falls monotonically: 0.1597, 0.1380, 0.0972, 0.0833; slope **−0.272/decade [−0.455, −0.088]** with head *count* rising 23 → 100. This explains llama-3.2-3b's low value. Restated against the ladder's scale-matched prediction, the shortfall is **2.34x descriptively**; the artifact does not report a scale-adjusted inferential test. Its reported **p = 0.0286** belongs instead to the unadjusted, one-sided exact permutation test over four text and three protein models for `fraction_above_0.10`, where complete separation reaches the minimum attainable p-value. Variance decomposition: scale adds **+0.003** given modality and lineage; ~~modality +0.220; lineage +0.061~~ — ~~but only **25.4%** of the modality indicator survives projection~~. **Superseded, not deleted (EXP-R2-073; see the D1.b block above, which is authoritative).** Those are the file-order census's figures under an uncentred denominator. Re-fitted on the seeded census and divided by the centred denominator: modality **+0.270**, lineage **+0.053**, scale +0.003 unchanged, and **36.3%** of the modality indicator surviving projection.

**Corpus — roughly half the modality contrast in log terms.**

| contrast | ratio |
|---|---|
| ~~gpt2 / dialogpt-small~~ | ~~2.30x [2.30, 2.40]~~ **RETRACTED — see §5.05(a); dialogpt-small is off-distribution at −4.08 nats context information and cannot anchor a contrast** |
| progen2-medium / progen2-base | 2.00x [1.20, 2.00] |
| gpt2-medium / qwen2.5-0.5b | 1.93x [1.82, 2.04] |
| gpt2-xl / llama-3.2-3b | 2.15x [1.92, 2.29] |
| **modality, matched pair** | **5.38x [5.23, 5.54]** |

**The identification limit, which no experiment can remove.** Induction heads are useful only on corpora containing repeated subsequences. Approximate repeats occur in **32.3% of text documents against 0.402% of protein entries** — an eightyfold difference.

> *The eightyfold figure is not unit-free, and the direction of the asymmetry flips with the unit (EXP-R2-073).* The two criteria are declared in **content symbols**, which is correct and is what keeps them free of the token confound that withdrew the far-band result — but they are not the same criterion. Text requires a repeat unit of **40 characters** with 15 distinct symbols and scores similarity by identity; protein requires **16 residues** with 8 distinct symbols and scores by BLOSUM62 non-adverse substitution. In symbols the text criterion is 2.5x the stricter; converted through each arm's measured symbols per token it is 40/4.4 ≈ **9 tokens** against 16/1.0 = **16 tokens**, so in tokens the *protein* criterion is the stricter one. Both choices are domain-motivated — a 40-character phrase and a 16-residue motif are the natural repeat units of their corpora — and the qualitative conclusion is untouched, because no plausible re-parameterisation closes two orders of magnitude. What should not be quoted as a unit-free fact is the multiplier. A decoder allocating fewer heads to prefix-matching on a repeat-poor corpus is allocating capacity efficiently against its data. Breaking the confound would require a protein decoder trained on a repeat-rich corpus, which cannot exist: a synthetic protein corpus with 32% repeat prevalence is not a protein corpus. **Even the terminal 2x2 training design cannot fix this**, as it matches architecture, parameters, scale and budget but explicitly not the data-generating process.

---

## PART II — Limitations of transferred interpretability methods

### 5. The catalogue

**The organising distinction:** a limitation demonstrated *on the text control* is a property of the **method**; one that appears only on protein arms is a property of the **transfer**. The programme repeatedly discovered the first kind and recorded it as an embarrassment rather than a result. The `scope` column is the spine of this section.

| # | limitation | method family affected | evidence | scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | Recovery gates set without checking attainability; single-MLP estimand has ~0.02 nats/token causal footprint against an 80% gate | SAE / transcoder / CLT | P0-2b (`evidence/p0_2b_fidelity_20260727/`, `evidence/p0_2_adjudication_20260727/`); estimand power sweep | **method** — unattainable on the text control |
| L2 | A 0.1-nat mutual-information gate applied to a design with a 0.0066-nat analytic ceiling | attribution / explanation channel | explanation-channel analysis | **method** |
| L3 | FVU is not comparable across models; ranking by FVU does not track behavioural fidelity | SAE / CLT | TG-03, TG-08 budget sweep | **method** |
| L4 | Loss-recovered denominators are arm-specific; ablation-baseline choice moves the measured denominator substantially | SAE / CLT, activation patching | denominator-guard work | **method** |
| L5 | The tested `paa_specific` proxy does not rank copy-suppression heads by measured causal effect on GPT-2-large (Spearman −0.062, p = 0.71), and the screened positive control has little dynamic range (5/56 heads vs induction 70/720) | this PAA screen for a copy-suppression prevalence census | EXP-R2-059 | **method-screen limitation on one text model** — no protein arm was scored; other screens and exhaustive causal censuses remain open |
| L6 | Positional baseline dominates the *body* of per-head attention statistics; decoy correction drops head median sixfold (0.0083 → 0.0013) while leaving the tail count intact (70 → 69, Jaccard 1.000). *The correction applied here is not identical to the PAA one and cannot be made identical — see §5.1* | attention-pattern analysis, circuit census | EXP-R2-059 | **method** |
| L7 | Attribution graphs hold attention patterns fixed and do not explain how they are computed; the price is measurable | attribution graph | TG-06 frozen attention | **method** |
| L8 | Output aperture is rank-(V−1); lens-family readouts are bounded by vocabulary size, which differs ~4700-fold across the panel (32 to 151936) | logit lens, tuned lens, DLA, J-Lens | EXP-R2-031, EXP-R2-045 | **transfer** — algebraic and vocabulary-conditional, not modality-specific |
| L9 | Explanation closure: an explanation drawn from an annotation channel cannot exceed that channel's information content | natural-language autoencoder, automated interpretability | analytic | **transfer** |
| L10 | J-Space / property-conditioned Jacobian is not defined for properties that are not functionals of the next-token distribution; directions come from a probe, making the object the Jacobian of the probe | J-Lens / J-Space | analytic, three independent reviews | **method**, fatal |
| L11 | Input rendering is load-bearing and silently wrong by default; ProtGPT2 moved **1.42 nats/token** between raw sequence and EOT + 60-column FASTA wrapping | every method that scores sequences | EXP-R2-028 | **transfer** |
| L12 | Plug-in unigram estimators bias information decompositions by up to **+1.02 nats** on protein arms vs +0.009 at residue level; held-out estimation is required | information-theoretic decomposition | EXP-R2-031, EXP-R2-033 | **transfer** — bias is vocabulary-dependent |
| L13 | Iterating a biological corpus in file order manufactures effects; three separate instances, most recently ProGen2 context information moving **+1.01 nats** by reading past the first 48 records | cohort construction, all methods | EXP-R2-059; the third instance is recorded in the *historical* `check.md` §0B.2, the exploratory document this audit superseded — the section does not exist here | **transfer** |
| L14 | Aligned-pair task construction collapses coverage on subword arms, 0.943 → 0.421 | probe design, circuit analysis | EXP-R2-048 | **transfer** |
| L15 | Conditioning leak: ZymCTRL's EC tag supplies 1.73 nats, inflating confidence-conditioned statistics | probes, erasure, any conditioned readout | EXP-R2-034 | **transfer** |
| L16 | Decodability and reliance dissociate sharply; probe accuracy does not establish that the model uses the feature | linear/MLP probe | EXP-R2-040 | **method** |
| L17 | An intervention that moves everything needs a control for moving everything; raw A6 numbers read −0.82 vs −0.83 (indistinguishable), against a matched random-direction control −0.441 vs −0.208 | activation patching, steering, path patching | EXP-R2-059 | **method** |
| L18 | An environment default silently narrowed a nine-stage campaign's text side to **one model** while every downstream number remained well-formed | any multi-arm pipeline | EXP-R2-057 | **method** |
| L19 | RMSNorm-on-LayerNorm algebra passes a reconstruction gate at 0.49% error while producing systematically wrong attribution; the loud failure (omitted bias, 0.518) is safer than the quiet one | OV/QK circuit analysis on modern architectures | EXP-R2-055 | **method** |
| L20 | **No remote predicate the campaign controller relied on could return false.** The access layer returns 0 whatever the remote command exits with — measured, `h200_pod_exec.sh -- bash -c "exit 7"` and `h200_pod_bash.sh "exit 5"` both return **0**. Three consequences, each verified on the cluster: a worker that refused a campaign at preflight and scheduled no GPU was reported as `campaign complete`, exit 0; the remote half of the code-freeze guarantee was never actually checked; and the append-only invocation manifest was **never pushed at all** — every run directory on GPFS holds `INVOCATIONS=0` while the controller logged "already present and verified". A campaign's verdict, its frozen code and its provenance record all travelled on a channel that cannot say no | any remote-execution pipeline | EXP-R2-068 | **method** — the L18 shape, one layer above the ledger built to prevent it |
| L21 | A generated contract can verify on the host that generated it and disagree on the host that runs it. The arm-to-environment-variable map was *inferred* by comparing resolved paths; the pod sets `TRANSFER_TEXT_MODEL_BASE_DIR="${TRANSFER_MODEL_BASE_DIR}"` because all checkpoints share one GPFS directory, so the comparison aliased and six of seven text arms classified as protein-root arms **on the pod only** | any host-portable declaration derived from resolved paths | EXP-R2-068 | **method** |
| L22 | **A head-prevalence census's selector ranks causal importance on text decoders and does not on protein decoders.** **SCOPE NARROWED (EXP-R2-120): the separation is not identified against layer depth, and most of it is that confound.** The causal readout is depth-biased on every arm without exception (`r(layer, \|effect\|)` = +0.36 to +0.77, since the patch and metric act at the query row only), and **ProtGPT2 is the sole arm whose census score *falls* with depth** (−0.387 against +0.00 to +0.50 elsewhere) — an all-grid Spearman between a depth-rising readout and a depth-falling score must land near zero, which is its −0.106. Controlled for depth the separation **holds in all four conditions but collapses in size, from a raw gap of +0.195…+0.248 to +0.019…+0.069** — smaller than gpt2-xl's own draw standard deviation of 0.039–0.050. The sign survives; the magnitude does not survive as a modality claim. **The defensible statement is a method limitation demonstrated on the text control as much as on the protein arms: an all-grid rank correlation between a census score and a query-local causal readout is confounded with depth.** The original claim and its evidence follow. With every head patched, the census-to-causal rank correlation separates completely: **text minimum +0.371 above protein maximum +0.282 in all 48 arm × condition cells over seven text and five protein arms**, matched pair included, pooling every draw measured. The failure is *recall in the bulk* — over the top-32 census heads ProtGPT2 reaches +0.808, above every text arm — and it is **not** distribution mismatch (`dialogpt-small`, a text decoder at −4.08 nats of context information, reads +0.518 to +0.640) and **not** architecture: two rotary/GQA text lineages read +0.479 to +0.575 while ProtGPT2, which shares gpt2-large's exact architecture, reads −0.226 to −0.006 (EXP-R2-079). The ordering survives an independent corpus draw on the four gap-defining arms, with the matched pair moving *apart* (EXP-R2-077), and survives correction for differential measurement error in all 24 cells where reliability is measured, worst-case gap +0.095 (EXP-R2-081) — so it is **not** an artefact of protein effects being estimated less precisely. An unknown part of §4's head-count gap therefore belongs to the instrument | head-prevalence census, circuit census, any selector-then-count design | EXP-R2-071, EXP-R2-072, EXP-R2-077, EXP-R2-079, EXP-R2-081, EXP-R2-120 | **method** — re-scoped 2026-08-04 (EXP-R2-120, quantified by EXP-R2-115). The all-grid statistic is depth-confounded on **every** arm including the text controls, and on the K-invariant dispersion measure the raw separation is **+5.08 to +7.18** pooled boundary-arm standard deviations against **+0.55 to +1.39** within-layer — inside one standard deviation in three of four conditions. Under §5's own organising rule, a limitation that appears on the text control is a property of the method. *Originally scoped **transfer**, on the evidence that follows:* demonstrated on the matched pair, absent on the text control including its off-distribution and cross-lab arms. *Margin quoted against a measured spread* (EXP-R2-093, extended to 39 draws and K up to 7 by EXP-R2-095): separated in all four conditions with **each side at its most adverse draw** (gpt2-xl worst above ZymCTRL best by **+0.108 to +0.142**), and protein arms **not** the noisier ones (median cell range 0.047 against text 0.048; the 0.052 recorded here until 2026-08-04 was the text *mean*, where the statistic named is the median — EXP-R2-093 reads text median 0.0475, mean 0.0535). **Attacked on its binding side and strengthened:** gpt2-xl is the text minimum in every condition, and K=3 → K=7 moved its pooled value *up* from +0.437 to +0.458. **Sub-clause corrected:** "each gap exceeds both boundary arms' draw ranges" now holds in **2 of 4** conditions, because an observed range grows with K (~1.69σ at n=3, ~2.70σ at n=7) and so penalises the better-measured arm. On a K-invariant statistic the **gap is 3.36–3.95 pooled boundary-arm standard deviations in all four conditions**. **Scope limit, EXP-R2-096, and it is material:** the all-grid statistic is dominated by the 576 heads a census never reports, and on induction *that stratum is at the noise floor on both arms* — median \|effect\|/SE **0.99** on gpt2-large and **0.92** on ProtGPT2, with 12% and 7% of heads above SNR 2. Decomposed by census rank, ProtGPT2 reads **+0.724 in the top 5% and +0.685 in the top 20%** against gpt2-large's +0.558 and +0.658, and its census top-20 retrieves **15.7/20** of the causally largest heads against the text control's **11.7/20** (chance 0.56; all four conditions, every draw agreeing in sign). This is the "recall in the bulk" clause above, now measured rather than asserted, and it carries a consequence that clause did not draw: **the separation is a statement about the unreported bulk, so it does not license the inference that a protein census's output is untrustworthy.** Where the census actually speaks it is at least as faithful as the text control's. **Extended to the whole panel (EXP-R2-101): the all-grid separation holds and every stratified version of it fails** — top 5% by −0.561, top 20% by −0.139, bulk 80% by −0.016 — because the aggregate is substantially measuring *heterogeneity*: the four GPT-2/ProGen2-lineage protein arms are the four most stratified in the panel, ProtGPT2's top-5%-minus-bulk spread reaching **+1.014 at K=7** against gpt2-large's +0.292 on an architecturally identical grid (EXP-R2-104). **The row's claim therefore holds as written for the aggregate and resolves into two unlike failures.** *Mode 1* — ProtGPT2 and all three ProGen2 arms — is the one described above. *Mode 2* is **ZymCTRL alone**: mediocre in every stratum **and failing to retrieve** (**+0.131** at K=6, hit@20 **2.9/20** against gpt2-large's 11.4 on the same grid), the single arm on which an untrustworthy-census reading is supported. *The criterion is retrieval rather than the top-minus-bulk spread:* llama-3.2-3b has the panel's lowest spread yet retrieves 8.5/20 at 14× chance, so a flat census can still be usable |
| L24 | **A released checkpoint can load "successfully" into a model whose entire feed-forward stack is random.** ProGen3-112M ships in megablocks packing — eight experts stacked along dim 0, `9216 = 8 × 1152`. `ProGen3ForCausalLM.from_pretrained(path, moe_implementation="eager")` **succeeds**, warns "newly initialized", and returns a model with every expert and every router randomly initialised at std ≈ 0.02 while attention, embeddings and norms load correctly. The upstream file carries a TODO saying the eager/megablocks state-dict substitution is not implemented; nothing enforces it. The gap is caught only by scoring: NLL **17.15** random against **1.983** converted on the same SwissProt cohort — so anyone who checks "did it load" rather than "does it compute" gets a plausible-looking pipeline built on noise. The repair is a loader that converts explicitly and calls `load_state_dict(strict=True)`, refusing on any missing or unexpected key, plus a scored self-check with a declared sanity band. **The mapping cannot be inferred from shapes alone** and its errors are quiet in the same way: swapping `w1`/`v1` (gate versus up projection) gives 3.201 and rolling the gate rows by one expert gives 3.173, both worse than residue-shuffled protein at 2.940 | any reuse of a third-party checkpoint whose packing differs from the loading path's | EXP-R2-130 | **method** — an interface defect in the external artefact, demonstrated before any measurement was taken from it |
