| *(circuit census — not on the original list)* | yes | **L22: an all-grid rank correlation between a census selector's score and a query-local causal readout is confounded with layer depth on every arm, the text controls included; L5: `paa_specific` screen failure for copy-suppression on GPT-2-large** | method |

## PART III — Toward methods for biological knowledge and discovery

### 7. Standing rejections

Recorded so they are not re-proposed under new names. Both have been rejected multiple times on unchanged grounds.

- **Property-conditioned Jacobian subspace** (rejected 3x). `J = ∂logits/∂h` is defined against the model's output. Secondary structure, Pfam, EC and fitness are not functionals of the next-residue distribution, so their directions can only come from a trained probe — making the object the Jacobian of the probe. Concept erasure answers the underlying question better because it measures reliance rather than decodability — on the evidence of L16's two cells specifically (ProtGPT2/Pfam, ProGen2-medium/ss3), which is what that comparison rests on rather than the EXP-R2-040 campaign as a whole.
- **Cross-position residue-pair sparse features** (gated). Motivation retracted: the attention margin over per-position marginals fell to 0.03–0.05 after correcting structure-selection order, and to ~0.03 over the separation-only control. Requires the relational effect to be re-established at adequate power before any construction. **D3.d's Phase A was that powered attempt; it has run, and it does not discharge this gate (2026-08-12).** Its relational estimand was its own and pre-registered — measured pairwise epistasis against a model's predicted pairwise interaction, with a construction-matched permutation null and a synthetic planted-coupling attainability gate — so it neither revisited the retracted attention-margin measurement nor inherited its power, and it built nothing: no pair dictionary was trained. The gate stays closed on the evidence that came back. The instrument is validated on the text control, so its protein nulls are measurements rather than shrugs, and the two protein arms fail differently: ProGen2-medium reads inside its own null with every planted repeat surviving tokenisation, and whether that is an absent mechanism or a probe off-distribution for proteins is open; ProtGPT2's reading carries no attribution at all, because its tokenizer destroys four planted repeats in five (L31). **A relational effect re-established at adequate power is what this gate asks for, and none of that is one.**

### 8. Where a proposal is justified

A proposal must be traceable either to a catalogued limitation or to a declared biological knowledge question that the audited methods cannot answer faithfully. The best-supported openings, in order:

0. **From L22 (new, EXP-R2-071/072) — a census-validity diagnostic, and it is the first opening this programme has earned rather than assumed.** The measured limitation is specific: a head-prevalence census's selector orders causal importance on text decoders and does not on protein decoders, the failure is in the bulk of the grid rather than at its top, and an off-distribution *text* control does not reproduce it. That licenses a small, general instrument — before a prevalence census is reported for any model, exhaustively patch a bounded random sample of heads and report the selector-to-causal rank correlation against the text control's value; below it, the count is not interpretable and must be published as a selector diagnostic rather than as a mechanism count. It is cheap, it is threshold-free, its attainability is demonstrated on the positive control by construction, and it is the rare case where the adapted method *is* the measurement that exposed the limitation. ~~**Still gated**: it rests on one mechanism, so D2.c must replicate it on copy suppression first. If D2.c returns a text-like correlation on protein, this opening closes.~~ **Closed — and not on that criterion (EXP-R2-094, EXP-R2-096).** D2.c did *not* return a text-like correlation: ProtGPT2 reads **+0.154 to +0.244** on copy suppression against the matched control's **+0.449 to +0.482**, separated by +0.205 at the most adverse draw pair and surviving two-sided disattenuation (+0.211), an instance-count control (gpt2-large reads +0.452 to +0.521 at *fewer* instances than ProtGPT2) and a keys-per-instance correction worth ~5% of the gap. On the pre-registered test the opening survives. **It closes because its statistic is refuted on its own terms.** The instrument would patch a bounded sample of heads, compare the selector-to-causal rank correlation against the text control, and declare a census uninterpretable below it. Applied to ProtGPT2's induction census it rejects at −0.106 against +0.520 — at the arm whose census top-20 retrieves 15.7/20 of the causally largest heads against the text control's 11.7/20, and whose top-5% correlation is +0.724 against +0.558. **The instrument is anti-correlated with what it is meant to certify**, because an all-grid rank correlation answers a question no census asks. A replacement would have to be stated on the reported stratum — retrieval of the causal top-k by the census top-k, which is selection-free and has a computable chance baseline — and on that statistic the protein arms do not fail, so it is a diagnostic without a demonstrated failure to detect. **The one protein deficit that survives with measurable effects behind it is the copy-suppression bulk** (+0.034 against +0.385 at K=6, ≈35% of heads above SNR 2 on both arms), which is not what a census reports either. **K=6 confirms the whole decomposition (EXP-R2-097)**: three further draws per arm left both all-grid ranges unmoved endpoint for endpoint, and the stratified separation widened rather than regressing — ProtGPT2 **+0.640** in the top 20% against gpt2-large's +0.202, and census top-20 retrieval of **8.5/20** against **4.7/20** at a chance level of 0.56, sign-stable in every stratum on all six draws.

0b. **From EXP-R2-113 (new) — difficulty-matched instance selection, and it is traced to a measured defect rather than to a hunch.** The measured limitation: **a PAA census's instance filter selects positions of very different difficulty in the two modalities.** Expressed as the margin at the census's instances over the margin at *all* recurrent positions of the same arm, it reads **0.143–0.199 on six text arms and 0.283–0.927 on four protein arms** — disjoint, with the filter picking positions five to seven times harder than typical on every text arm and only 1.1–3.5× harder on the protein ones. Everything D2.c reports as an effect-*size* difference is downstream of that: the absolute knockout effects overlap between modalities (0.0156–0.0626 against 0.0089–0.0159), and the whole separation is the denominator the filter selects. **The adaptation is to stratify instance selection on the arm's own margin distribution** — draw PAA instances to match a common difficulty profile, in the arm's own units, instead of taking whatever the decoy/induction/distance filters happen to leave. Then a cross-arm effect-size comparison is over positions the models find comparably hard. ~~*Attainability is demonstrated by construction on the text control* (Appendix B rule 2): the text arms have the widest margin distribution.~~ **That claim is false and is withdrawn (EXP-R2-116)** — ProtGPT2's PAA-pool margin distribution reaches q90 ≈ 19 and max ≈ 67 against gpt2-large's q90 ≈ 2.7, so the text arm is the *narrower* distribution, not the dominating one. It is also now moot: the distribution that motivated this item was 28–33% layout tokens. **This item is suspended pending re-measurement of its motivating statistic on layout-excluded pools**, and Appendix B rule 2 is recorded as breached here for the fourth time — inside an item written to fix an interface defect. *The gate*: re-run D2.c under matched selection and report whether the denominator gap and the ranking gap survive. **A null result is a complete answer and closes this item** — if both survive matched selection, the filter is not what drives them and the effect-size reading stands as first written. Cost is one D2.c campaign, ~2.5 GPU-h per arm-draw at the current settings. **Not yet built; the instrument is frozen and this is a change to its instance selection, so it is proposed here rather than implemented in passing.**

1. **From L5** — a causal-effect-based mechanism comparison over a fixed budget of exhaustively tested heads, requiring no valid cheap screen. Trades coverage for validity. Per-instance ΔM-gap matrices were retained, so its feasibility is answerable without a rerun. *Partly discharged for induction by EXP-R2-071/072; open for copy suppression as D2.c.*
2. **From L1/L3/L4** — a dictionary-evaluation protocol whose estimand is power-checked on the text control before any protein arm is scored, reporting loss recovered and KL rather than FVU.
3. **From L8** — a readout that does not route through the unembedding, since the rank-(V−1) aperture is a hard vocabulary-conditional algebraic constraint and small residue vocabularies make it especially restrictive.

4. **D3.g — language-mediated causal concept alignment, candidate only.** Freeze a checkpoint qualified in both modes and use genuine protein-sequence–curated-description pairs to map sequence-level or declared residue-level protein activations into a pre-declared text concept subspace or shared bottleneck. Raw activation equality and unpaired token positions are not estimands; each side is aggregated, whitened, or calibrated to concept percentiles or attainable causal-response units before comparison. Run mean, affine or orthogonal, nearest-neighbour retrieval, shuffled-pair, rank-matched, and bridge-specific-concept baselines first; train a small bottleneck Adapter MLP only if non-linearity adds family-disjoint value, and report its capacity beside every result. Pass requires the alignment to beat every applicable baseline, survive description and homology leakage checks, preserve unrelated concepts, transfer at least one graded protein-model intervention in the predicted direction, and reproduce on an unseen protein family. Retrieval controls, protein-model intervention, and external biological evidence—not linguistic plausibility or reconstruction—decide whether the model encoded biological knowledge. Estimated at 5–12 GPU-h after a paired-data power audit and zero if an affine or retrieval baseline saturates the target.

5. **D3.h — paired-input Model Diffing Crosscoder, candidate only.** On identical verified inputs and a common tokenizer, compare ProLLaMA base → continued pretraining and continued pretraining → instruction tuning separately within each mode, with exact correspondence of scored token positions, layers, and activation sites. Raw activation differences, affine or orthogonal alignment, matched-capacity independent dictionaries, shuffled pairing, and cross-seed stability precede Crosscoder training. The base protein mode is unmeasurable on the current target, so it can identify adaptation-associated representation changes but cannot establish knowledge acquisition. A biological knowledge claim requires both compared arms to be measurable, a pre-declared knowledge estimand and retrieval baseline, causal intervention on a model-specific latent, preservation of unrelated behaviour, and family- or time-disjoint external evidence. Stop if exact pairing is unavailable, a simpler baseline saturates the target, or the result is only reconstructive. Cost is estimated only after an activation-footprint and paired-input power audit.

   > **Status 2026-08-12 (EXP-R2-175): the protein mode now runs, and what unblocked it is itself an Objective-2 result.** The cell had been refusing to start, and the guard was right: a record-level held-out split is not held out on Swiss-Prot, and the exact-string check that was catching duplicates reached at most two fifths of the leakage. L30 carries the measurement, the near-duplicate-group remedy and its calibration. **The completed text-mode cell was verified genuinely clean rather than assumed to be** — every group of its pool is a singleton, so the group split *is* the record split there and it reproduces the old training and held-out lists exactly, including record order — so the text result stands without a re-run and **the two modes are one procedure**, which is what makes them comparable at all. That localises this limitation to the **evaluation interface** and not to the method. **The two-mode reading, and it is representational.** Cross-checkpoint held-out residual under true pairing, mean over layers: ridge **0.400** on protein against **0.684** on text, both far below their own shuffled-pairing values of 1.002 and 1.001 — so **both modes are measuring correspondence rather than capacity**, and neither is the `d²` free parameters. No affine map removes the difference in either mode: where the residual is largest, ridge still leaves 0.73 on protein and 0.90 on text. **The depth profiles differ in shape**: the text residual rises with depth and stays high to the top (0.65 at layer 31), while the protein one peaks in the middle third (0.73 at layer 13) and falls back to 0.11–0.28 at layers 29–31. The skip-offset sensitivity is flat — a second protein pool at a disjoint offset moves every quantity the verdict reads in the third decimal. **One aggregate row must not be used**: the text side's mean adjacent-layer *identity* residual of 206.5 is carried almost entirely by layer 1, which alone reads 6,347.7 because its next layer's held-out variance is tiny; the ridge-based adjacent unit is unaffected and the per-layer ratios stand. **No R2.4 decision is recorded, by design.** The stage declines to declare one, both numbers are representational rather than behavioural, and the base checkpoint's protein mode remains behaviourally unmeasurable — which is the bound this item already carries and which no representation result relaxes.

   > **Status 2026-08-14 (EXP-R2-191, completed by EXP-R2-194): the independent-dictionary step is finished on all four cells, and its basis-adequacy criteria — fixed as numbers before any of the four dictionaries existed — were read here as blocking the protein side. Both criteria have since gone void on their own text control; the struck words below mark the attribution that was withdrawn, and the next block is the record of the withdrawal.** Four per-layer transcoders were trained at one recipe, 2x expansion at `k` 32, on `Llama-2-7b-hf` and `ProLLaMA_Stage_1` in both modes. **Criterion D3.h-B1 is void on its own text control.** Both text cells fail it, which makes it a badly-formed statistic rather than a badly-chosen threshold — it divides by the cross-checkpoint residual, which is small at the shallow layers, so the ratio is unstable exactly where neither quantity is large — and its column is struck rather than reported. The void was foreseen in the pre-declaration, whose own orientation figures said the text dictionary "would fail on max", and the cut was left where it was rather than moved once it was clear which side it excluded. ~~**Criterion D3.h-B2 is not void, because the text control clears it in both cells, and both protein cells fail it**~~ — **struck on the same day by the block below: D3.h-B2 read at the resolution it was specified at fails on its own text control, so it is void exactly as D3.h-B1 is.** What the cell measured stands: mean live latents per layer read **7,608** pre-adaptation and **4,900** adapted on text against `d_model` **4,096**, and **2,188** and **1,634** on protein. ~~Both protein dictionaries are under-complete — they cannot span the space, so they cannot decompose it into more features than it has dimensions, which is the premise a sparse over-complete dictionary is read under — and therefore **a feature diff over these four dictionaries cannot be a statement about two models on the protein side; it would be a statement about two fitted bases.**~~ ~~The shortfall is a live-basis problem — a training-recipe question about revival, sparsity, budget or width.~~ *One clause of the original does survive quotation and is now the whole of what these four cells license about the protein side:* nothing here bears on whether a protein feature diff is possible **in principle**. **The four matched certificates read exactly as predicted before training.** The two within-checkpoint cross-mode pairs certify `MATCHED` with `disagreements` empty; the two cross-checkpoint diffing pairs certify `MISMATCH` with `disagreements` exactly `['backbone_sha256']`; all four agree at `max_tokens [1024, 1024]`. **No comparator was loosened and no fourth verdict was added**, which is the point of the certificate: the backbone is the contrast the diff exists to make, and a verdict that could be made to read `MATCHED` across it would certify nothing. The behavioural half of these same dictionaries is D2.h's, where EXP-R2-194's recovery comparison is recorded; the base checkpoint's protein mode is still behaviourally unmeasurable and this item's standing bound is unchanged.

   > **Status 2026-08-14, second entry: both adequacy criteria are void, the protein cells keep their measurement and lose their attribution, and the successor gate is per layer (EXP-R2-203 on the dictionaries, EXP-R2-202 and its corrections on the activations, EXP-R2-201 and EXP-R2-204 on the levers).** This is a withdrawal under a rule fixed before the numbers existed, and it is recorded as such rather than as a qualification.
   >
   > **D3.h-B2 is void on the condition that voided D3.h-B1, and a resolution defect is what kept that hidden.** EXP-R2-191's preamble required both criteria to hold "in all four cells, at the layers a difference is reported on", while the B2 clause stated its statistic as a *mean* live latents per layer; the two sentences disagree, and only the mean was ever readable, because `Transcoder.objective` collapsed a per-layer dead mask into one cross-layer scalar before anything downstream saw it. **So the gate that was blocking R2.4 had never been evaluated at the resolution it was specified at, in either direction** — neither to confirm the protein FAIL nor to confirm the text PASS. Recovered per layer from the `silent_steps` buffer every checkpoint already carried, and re-censused on the held-out cohort each dictionary was scored on, **the text control fails**: `base/text` averages **7,607.9** live latents per layer and keeps **90** at layer 1, one failing layer of 32; `stage1/text` averages 4,900.3, keeps 94 at layer 1 and stays under 3,000 across layers 5–14, failing **11** layers with ten of them inside the interior window 2–29, and under the stricter held-out firing definition it fails on the mean as well, at **3,625.1** against 4,096. EXP-R2-191 named the consequence before any of these numbers existed: a gate the control cannot pass is a specification defect and not a protein result. **Both of this unit's criteria are therefore void — B1 as an ill-formed statistic, B2 by its own control — and neither may judge a protein cell.**
   >
   > **What is withdrawn is the attribution and not the measurement, which is the shape of D2.h's withdrawal of EXP-R2-182 as well.** "A feature diff over these dictionaries compares two fitted bases rather than two models" was licensed by D3.h-B2 being a gate its control passed; it is not, so the sentence is withdrawn rather than softened. **The descriptive asymmetry is untouched and it is large**: 7,607.9 and 4,900.3 live latents per layer on text against 2,187.8 and 1,633.5 on protein, with per-layer minima of 90 and 94 against 906 and 95. The withdrawal does not run the other way either — D3.h-B2 stops being a *reason to refuse* the protein diff without becoming a *reason to proceed* with it, so R2.4's protein side is for the moment blocked by nothing that has been measured, which is a different and worse position than being blocked by something.
   >
   > **The successor measurement carried a specification defect of its own, and it is this programme's rather than the checkpoints' (EXP-R2-202, corrected the same day).** That entry measured the activations' own effective dimension at the dictionary site — `r99`, the smallest number of eigen-directions carrying 99% of the variance — under a three-branch rule keyed to a threshold of `d_model` = 4,096. **That threshold is unattainable at any sample size.** `r99` cannot exceed `ceil(0.99 × d)` = **4,056** even on a flat spectrum with infinite samples, because reaching `d_model` would require the smallest eigenvalue alone to carry more than 1% of the total; the realised draw of 65,536 positions returns an isotropic-control ceiling of **4,026**, already 99.3% of that maximum, and a sixteen-fold larger draw buys 24 of the remaining 70 points. **The rule's Rule C branch was therefore guaranteed to fire, and its firing carries no information**; it is not reported as a finding. This is B1's failure class one level down — attainability assumed rather than demonstrated, inside an entry that required attainability before application — and it is attributable to the pre-registration. The isotropic control is what caught it, which is what a control is for.
   >
   > **What the spectrum establishes needs no threshold, and it is the durable result.** Median `r99` at the block-output site: `base/text` **3,670**, `stage1/text` **2,954**, `stage1/protein` **2,709**, `base/protein` **2,588** — over the interior layers 2–29, 3,690 / 3,160 / 2,800 / 2,617; as fractions of the attainable ceiling, 91.2% / 73.4% / 67.3% / 64.3%. **The four cells do not group by modality.** `stage1/text` sits **6.1 points** from `stage1/protein` and **17.8 points** from `base/text`: the adapted checkpoint's text mode is nearer the two protein cells than it is to its own lineage's pre-adaptation text mode, and separating "both text" from "both protein" would need a cut in **(2,709, 2,954]**, a band with no stated meaning and choosable only after seeing the answer. **This corroborates the withdrawal above independently** — EXP-R2-203 reached the refusal condition from the dictionaries alone, on the same day and by a different route, and neither result depends on the other. It also resizes the protein shortfall: measured against the dimension the activations occupy rather than against `d_model`, the median gap is **−229** live latents per layer on `base/protein` and **−946** on `stage1/protein`, roughly **15% and 40%** of the bar rather than the 2.5x under-completeness the earlier framing implied. That framing is not defensible and is not to be quoted.
   >
   > **The layer that made the point vivid is a massive activation, and it is an illustration rather than the argument.** At layer 1 in both text cells the block output's largest eigenvalue carries about **0.9999** of the variance (`r99` = 1, participation ratio 1.00), which is why no recipe can put 4,096 live latents there. It is not a capture artefact: dropping each record's first content position moves the top-eigenvalue share only in the sixth decimal, the direction is essentially axis-aligned — the coordinate-independent null reads `r99` = 2 with one coordinate carrying 73.6%, the LLaMA-family massive-activation signature — and both protein cells are ordinary at the same layer under identical code, masking and batching (top-coordinate share 0.4% and 1.7%). It holds 28.1% and 26.3% of each text cell's summed centred variance. **Nothing above rests on it**: excluding layers 0, 1, 30 and 31 leaves the medians at 3,690 / 3,160 / 2,800 / 2,617 and every reading intact.
   >
   > **The successor admission rule, fixed here going forward, and it is per layer because that is what the original preamble asked for.** With both criteria void, R2.4's operative rule is admissibility at a layer rather than a gate on a whole model: **a model diff may be reported at layer `l` only where both cells' dictionaries carry at least that layer's own `r99` live latents.** It is well-formed where D3.h-B2 was not — data-relative rather than measured against a yardstick the activations never reach, symmetric across the two modes, and per layer — and the text control passes it at **32 of 32** layers (`base/text`) and **31 of 32** (`stage1/text`, failing layer 10 alone). The protein cells clear it at **11 of 32** (`base/protein`) and **6 of 32** (`stage1/protein`). **A diff needs both sides of the pair, and the two protein sets are nested**: `stage1/protein`'s six admissible layers are a subset of `base/protein`'s eleven, so the intersection **this clause alone** returns is layers **0 and 27–31**, of which only 27–29 lie inside the interior window D3.h-A is read on — computed from EXP-R2-203's per-layer live curves against EXP-R2-202's per-layer `r99` vectors. ~~A Crosscoder restricted to that intersection is defensible now and a diff outside it is not.~~ **That sentence is withdrawn, and the rule it rested on is amended in the block below: five of those six layers clear the bar only because the bar collapses there.** **Clearing this rule clears only itself:** EXP-R2-201's three standing conditions on a cleared gate are untouched, the well-formed question underneath the void B1 is still unanswered and its replacement statistic is still deliberately unwritten, and admissible was never the same as informative.
   >
   > **The rule as first fixed above is incomplete, and the amendment removes an admissibility claim rather than adding one.** Clause (a) — both cells' dictionaries carry at least that layer's own `r99` live latents — passes at layers where `r99` is anomalously *low*, and a criterion satisfied because its threshold collapses is D3.h-B2's failure class inverted: there the bar was unreachable, here it is unreachably low. Measured at the six intersection layers, `r99` reads **533 / 396** at layer 0, **2,516 / 2,364** at 27, **2,232 / 1,563** at 28, **1,914 / 939** at 29, **1,920 / 606** at 30 and **471 / 87** at 31 (`base/protein` / `stage1/protein`), against cell medians of 2,588 and 2,709. Only layer 27 clears a bar near typical in both cells. **This is the third criterion in this unit whose range was never demonstrated, and the third is the worst of them:** EXP-R2-204's width bands were uninterpretable *in principle*, because doubling `d_hidden` at fixed `k` halves each latent's expected share of the `k` slots per token, so sub-linear growth of the live population is the expected behaviour at 2x width **on any data at all** and a ratio below 2.0 evidences nothing by itself — that sweep has since been amended to read a protein-versus-text ratio of live fractions against a text control arm, pre-registered before any protein result was read.
   >
   > **Amended rule, both conditions required.** A model diff may be reported at layer `l` only where **(a)** both cells' dictionaries carry at least `r99(l)` live latents, **and (b)** `r99(l)` is itself non-degenerate in both cells — `l` inside the interior window **2–29**, which is the exclusion this document already applies to layers 0, 1, 30 and 31, *and* `r99(l)` **≥ 0.5 × that cell's median `r99`**, which says the layer's activations occupy at least half the effective dimension that cell's typical layer does. The quantitative half is needed because the window alone is not sufficient: layer 29 sits inside it while the adapted cell's bar there is 35% of its own median. **Importing a window is legitimate here where EXP-R2-203 refused it for D3.h-B2, and the asymmetry is the reason**: that refusal was against narrowing a gate so that failing layers fall outside it, whereas this restriction can only *remove* a pass and never excuse a failure, so it cannot be a window chosen after seeing which layers fail.
   >
   > **The fraction is swept rather than asserted, and the conclusion does not depend on it (Appendix B rule 17).** Across `f` from 0 to 0.9 the protein pair's admissible intersection reads {27, 28, 29} for `f` ≤ 0.30, **{27, 28}** for 0.35 ≤ `f` ≤ 0.577, {27} for 0.6 ≤ `f` ≤ 0.85 and empty at 0.9 — one to three layers everywhere, and one or two over the whole central range. The declared 0.5 sits inside the band where the answer does not move, and layer 28 is **marginal by construction**, entering at 0.577 × the adapted cell's median and leaving just above it. **Attainability is demonstrated on the text control across the entire sweep, which is exactly what D3.h-B2 lacked**: the text pair retains 27 of the 28 interior layers at `f` = 0.5 — every interior layer except 10, which fails clause (a) on `stage1/text` — and still retains 24 at `f` = 0.85.
   >
   > **So the corrected reading, and it is a withdrawal.** Under the amended rule the protein pair's admissible intersection is **{27, 28}**, with 28 marginal on the adapted side, against 27 of 28 interior layers on the text control. A Crosscoder built on this basis would be a **one-to-two-layer result**, and it must be described as one rather than as a six-layer diff; whether R2.4's protein arm has an interior band to work on at all is a question **EXP-R2-204 has now answered, and the answer is yes** (2026-08-15). The intersection above is a property of the `d_hidden` 8,192 / `k` 32 recipe rather than of protein activations: `r99` is an activation property and never moves, so a recipe that raises the live basis clears clause (a) at more layers. At `k` 64 the admissible set is {24–28}; at `d_hidden` 16,384 it is {19, 20, 22–28}, and the text control passes 28 of 28 interior layers there. **Reported by binding margin over `r99`, because three of those nine are admitted by rounding** — layers 19, 20 and 22 clear by 0.3%, 0.4% and one latent — the width cell carries **six contiguous robust layers, 23 through 28**, every one clearing by at least 12.9% on the binding side. The same standard is less kind to the baseline than the sentence above was: at 8,192 layers 27 and 28 clear by only 4.8% and 9.3%, so the starting position is **zero robust layers and two marginal admissions**, not a one-to-two-layer result. **Admissible is still not informative** — EXP-R2-206 fitted a Crosscoder at layers this rule admits and its readout is void on C3 at every one of them — so this removes one of the two obstacles to R2.4's protein arm and leaves the other standing. **One measurement independently disqualifies the layer that looked best under the unamended rule:** layer 31 admitted a diff at `r99` 87 and 471, and EXP-R2-201's per-layer reading shows its live count moving by **−1,123** and **−1,333** latents under nothing but a change of the auxiliary revival constant, while 30 of 32 and 31 of 32 layers moved by at most 52 and 54. A basis that swings by a thousand latents under a hyper-parameter the verdict calls inert is not a basis to diff two models on, and clause (b) excludes it on grounds measured before that was known.
   >
   > **Two levers on the protein shortfall, one spent and one running.** *Spent — the auxiliary revival budget.* EXP-R2-201 raised `auxk` from 192 to 2,048 on both protein checkpoints, which is the value the reference rule this trainer reproduces returns at this backbone's width and a **10.7x** larger budget; both cells finished **inside** the pre-declared ±100 no-effect window and **downward**, at 1,586.2 and 2,146.2 live latents per layer, and the base-minus-adapted separation is invariant to the mechanism (560 against 554). **Read per layer when that verdict was filed, the null is sharper than its own mean**: at 30 of 32 layers on the adapted cell and 31 of 32 on the base one the movement is at most 52 and 54 latents with mixed signs, and nearly all of the mean's movement is a single layer — the last, where the bar is `r99` 87 and 471 — so admissibility is unchanged at every layer of both cells. Revival is excluded, and it is excluded in the interior rather than on average. *Running — capacity against data.* EXP-R2-204 is training four protein-mode dictionaries to ask whether the shortfall is capacity-limited or data-limited: `d_hidden` 8,192 → 16,384 as the deciding lever, `k` 32 → 64 as a declared-secondary one, both on both checkpoints. **Its original absolute bands (`L16/L8` ≥ 1.7 capacity-limited, ≤ 1.2 data-limited) are superseded before any result existed**, for the mechanical reason given above; the reading of record is now **R = protein `f16/f8` ÷ text `f16/f8`** on the live *fraction*, matched by checkpoint against a text control arm added at the same two widths — below 1 is protein-specific saturation, at or above 1 is ordinary capacity behaviour for this trainer, and |R − 1| < 0.05 is not resolvable and is reported as no difference. **No result exists and none is anticipated here.** A capacity verdict would say the basis can be bought; a data verdict would say R2.4's protein arm must be reformulated inside the `r99`-dimensional subspace rather than retrained.
   >
   > **Bounds.** One site — one module's two tensors — and not the residual stream a lens or a probe reads. One draw and one seed per cell. `r99` is a variance-based effective dimension and not an algebraic rank, so nothing bounds how much computation the discarded tail carries; at `r999` the four cells read 4,026 / 3,848 / 3,771 / 3,716, far closer together, so the *size* of a shortfall depends on where the tail is cut even though the ordering does not. The live counts come from a training-time counter and the spectrum from the held-out draw, which are not the same population. The corpora are Swiss-Prot and OpenWebText, so a mode difference here is never only a mode difference. One lineage, one adaptation step, four cells.

6. **D3.i — cross-modal causal circuit translation, gated and unranked.** Admit only after D3.g yields a stable, leakage-controlled concept bridge and both source and target circuits address the same pre-declared concept task. The protein circuit and the language circuit must each pass behavioural preservation, node intervention, and edge/path intervention gates in their original models; the protein circuit must additionally be grounded before translation in a pre-declared DMS, structural, or functional referent. A frozen-attention attribution graph is not an admitted source unless its approximation error is measured and passes its own gate. Align nodes only at the sequence or declared concept level inherited from D3.g. Use Jacobians to rank candidate edge correspondences, never to certify them: on pre-registered representative edges, source-node intervention, edge or path ablation, or path patching must reproduce direction, sign, and graded effect in both models. Protein → language → protein cycle consistency is necessary but not sufficient. Compare against node-only nearest-neighbour, layer/degree-matched graph, shuffled-edge, random-description, and language-only explanation baselines. Final validation must use family- or time-disjoint biological evidence that was not used to build either circuit, fit D3.g, select the translation, or name its concepts. If only model-internal causality survives, the output is a model-mechanism hypothesis; if only fluent labels or node matches survive, circuit translation fails. The method aims to reduce free-form per-feature naming; it does not remove human-defined concepts, curated paired descriptions, intervention design, or biological adjudication. Cost and model scope remain undefined, and implementation receives zero work until every prerequisite passes.

Any method that is built is benchmarked against SAE, CLT, probes, dense low-rank and random controls, on fidelity, causal selectivity, cross-seed stability and generalisation to disjoint protein families.

**Construction order, and it is a sequence rather than a preference: dense first, then MoE, then a unified method — and there is no requirement to reach the third.** A method that works on a dense decoder is testable against this programme's whole existing panel, where every arm is dense and where the text controls that scope a limitation as method-intrinsic or transfer-specific already live. A method built for MoE first has exactly one model to be tested on (ProGen3-112M, which is not a panel arm), so its failures cannot be attributed to method, model, data or interface — which is the attribution this programme exists to make. Unification is attempted only if a dense method and an MoE method both work and their difference is understood; a "universal" method proposed before either is speculative generality with an architecture diagram.

This is a rule about **constructed methods**, not about measurement. Measuring a MoE model to characterise a failure — D2.g, and EXP-R2-137's routing question — is part 2 work and is not gated behind a dense construction, because it is diagnosing an external artefact rather than proposing ours.

---

## 9. Foundational programme record

This section records the pure-text and pure-protein plan used from 2026-07-30 through 2026-08-09. Its identifiers, gates, costs, preregistrations, and closed branches remain part of the evidence trail. The forward tracks in §0 govern new work; `docs/RESEARCH_PLAN.md` is their executable guide, and this section must not be read as the current admission list.

**No programme-level budget figure is maintained here, and the one that was is withdrawn (2026-08-09).** This paragraph read "Remaining budget ~55–95 H200 GPU-hours. The programme has spent about 8 to date, which is the main reason the estimate has fallen: measurements that were costed as campaigns turned out to be minutes once the case counts were sized to the gate rather than to the panel." The spend figure is contradicted by this section's own tables, which book D2.b′ at ~16 GPU-h, D1.a at ~6 + ~6, EXP-R2-080 at ≈44 and D2.g at ~3 — already an order of magnitude above 8 — while D2.c's ~55 arm-draws are priced in draws and never in hours. Since a total was never maintained, a remainder derived from it cannot be either. The per-item costs in the tables below are the only spend figures this document supports, and they are per item rather than cumulative.

### D1 — Differences between the model families

**Established, and narrowed to the matched pair on 2026-08-12.** ~~One difference survives as a bounded, reproducible statement: on
repeat probes drawn from the whole matching population, protein decoders put a
smaller fraction of heads in the upper tail of the prefix-matching distribution
than text decoders.~~ **That panel-level form is refuted and retired (EXP-R2-171; §4 carries it).** What survives is the designed matched pair: on repeat probes drawn from the whole matching population, gpt2-large puts **5.46x** the fraction of heads above the headline prefix-matching cut that ProtGPT2 does, reproduced at sixteen times the probe count, anti-confounded by ProtGPT2's *higher* identity ceiling, and stable at 5.62x under ceiling normalisation. As of EXP-R2-068 that ordering holds at the headline
threshold on **all three** probe constructions — the recorded
inversion on the best probe was an artefact of the repeat cohort's file-order
draw. It remains **not a modality claim**: five text arms against one protein arm
in the only family spanning both (§2), and an eightyfold corpus repeat-prevalence
confound that no experiment can remove.

Two further differences are open rather than closed:

| item | question | status | cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D1.a** | Do the far-band propagation *magnitudes* differ, once resolved? | **Reopened, then re-answered, and the answer changed (EXP-R2-073 / 074).** The token-band result is withdrawn: two protein arms swap order on the unit choice alone. Re-measured over five windows with the band *and* the corruption span declared in content symbols, the **level** contrast reverses — text is highest at short content distance — and what separates is the **decay rate**: across **seven** text controls the separation is clean at threshold 0.50 (worst text 0.397 below lowest protein 0.500, 136 of 140 paired comparisons) and **overlaps by 0.015 at 0.25**, where the off-distribution arm dialogpt-small reaches 0.611. Like the far-band level result, it lives in the large-effect tail. The matched pair separates on this estimand for the first time; the text decay ratio is flat across a 6.5x scale range where the protein one rises, so a larger protein arm would widen the gap rather than close it. Residual unit caveats in §5.1 | ~6 + ~6 GPU-h, spent |
| ~~**D1.b**~~ | Is the tail statement a *distribution* statement at full population? | **Answered (EXP-R2-073), CPU, no GPU spent.** No: it remains a tail statement, and the tail is now located — the ordering separates at every quantile from q75 to q99 and fails at the median and in the extreme upper tail. Pooled AUC 0.624, 20 of 28 pairs above 0.5, 2 of 28 showing stochastic dominance against the recorded 0 of 12. Two published figures moved with it: modality variance surviving projection 25.4% → **36.3%**, and the 2.34x scale-adjusted shortfall loses its interval verdict as an out-of-lineage extrapolation. Detail in §4 | 0 GPU-h, spent |

**Exit condition unchanged in spirit:** D1.a and D1.b existed to explain the foundational transfer failures, not to expand the completed pure-model panel for its own sake. Their closure does not prohibit D1.d or D1.e, which ask newly admitted questions about joint checkpoints, knowledge source, and novelty.

### D1.c — Candidate arms and corpora, with the risk each carries

Reviewed 2026-07-30. Recorded here so the survey is not lost, and so that nothing
on it can be admitted without its stated risk being answered first. **None is in
the panel.** The panel grew by exactly one arm this session, ProGen2-small, and
only after a load check on the pod.

| candidate | what it would buy | why it is not admitted yet |
|---|---|---|
| **MolCrawl protein GPT-2-large** | The highest-value candidate: a *second* protein arm on GPT-2-large's 36x1280x20-head backbone, so the modality coefficient would stop resting on ProtGPT2 alone — the structural limit of §2 | Matched on backbone, **not** on parameter count (a small protein vocabulary shrinks the embedding and output layers); reported parameter counts disagree with the checkpoint; `bos_token_id`/`eos_token_id` disagree with the tokenizer's special symbols; no peer-reviewed validation; ProteinGym-finetuned variants must be excluded. "Both trained on UniRef50" does not fix release, filtering or compute budget. It would *reduce* the single-arm risk; it cannot on its own establish a causal modality difference |
| **RITA** (one medium checkpoint) | Tests whether the conclusions are specific to the GPT-2 / ProGen2 lineages | Untried here; admit one rung before four |
| **ProGen3-112M + ProGenMech** | The external baseline a D3 construction must replicate against, and the first sparse-MoE protein decoder in scope | Mandatory to replicate *before* D3, and must not be treated as already passing this programme's gates |
| **Tranception, retrieval off/on** | A clean D2 interface-attribution experiment: identical weights, external homology retrieval toggled | Not part of the modality measurement; separate design |
| **Pythia / PolyPythia multi-seed** | Seed variance and learning dynamics on the text side | Instrumental calibration only. It must **not** become extra text-side voting evidence — the text side already has seven arms against five protein |
| **Dayhoff UR50/UR90 variants** | Controlled data-mechanism study | Only if the evidence points at a training-data confound |
| **ProGen2-large** | Same lineage, same 31-token alphabet, a 1600x wider output head — a natural experiment on whether L8's rank-(V-1) aperture tracks the output *matrix* or the reachable *symbols* | Load-checked: 2779.4M parameters, works, but `vocab_size` 51200 against a 31-token tokenizer. Every `config.vocab_size`-derived statistic would be over a mostly dead alphabet. Deserves its own gated design; see `panel_contract.STAGED_BUT_NOT_ADMITTED` |
| **ProGen2-xlarge** | Would extend the protein ladder to 6.4B, a 43x range | Load-checked: 6443.6M parameters, forward pass returns width 32 — but the config carries **no** `vocab_size`, only `vocab_size_emb`/`vocab_size_lm_head`, and `budget.arm_power` reads `config.vocab_size`. Admissible once the panel reads a *declared* alphabet size rather than trusting a key two checkpoints in one lineage spell differently |

**Corpora, as evidence packages rather than more FASTA.** Four are worth building, in this order, and each carries a trap that has to be designed around rather than noticed afterwards. A *temporal* extrapolation set per model cutoff, from UniParc first-observation timestamps against a pinned Swiss-Prot release — but UniRef cluster IDs are rebuilt between releases and cannot be the identifier, and new appearance is not novelty without homology exclusion. A *PDB-CATH* set of experimental structures released after each cutoff, split out-of-family on CATH superfamilies with S20/S35 sensitivity beside the S40 already here, distinguishing an old sequence with a new structure from a genuinely new sequence. An *independent DMS* collection from late MaveDB assays absent from the model papers and from ProteinGym, grouped by wild type and homologous cluster — millions of mutant rows over one wild type are not independent units, which is the same error L1's cohort made at smaller scale. And a *repeat-mechanism* 2x2 (repeat present/absent x low/normal complexity) from RepeatsDB, Swiss-Prot repeat records and synthetic constructs, matched on length, taxonomy, domain architecture, compositional entropy and maximum training-set similarity — which is the only design that tests the corpus-repeat confound §4 calls irremovable, rather than restating it. MGnify is optional and cannot be a clean gold standard: it is largely predicted proteins and may overlap BFD.

**A standing caution this survey earns.** Predicted structures, pLDDT and sequence-inferred labels support stratification and covariate adjustment. They cannot demonstrate functional competence or "acquired protein knowledge". F10 now bounds one narrow question on the current fitness interface; functional competence on independent evidence and generative novelty still have no qualified instrument.

### D2 — Where the methods transfer, and whose fault it is when they do not

The deliverable. **Thirty-one** limitations are catalogued in §5 as L1–L31, each scoped as **method** or **transfer** under that section's organising rule — a limitation demonstrated on the text control is a property of the method, one appearing only on protein arms is a property of the transfer. No row carries any other scope. *(This preamble read "Twenty-three limitations … each scoped as method, model, data or interface" until 2026-08-09 and "twenty-seven … L1–L27" until 2026-08-12, when L28 to L31 were added; the count is stale as often as it is written, and the wider vocabulary was never what the `scope` column uses. The rows are unchanged and are not renumbered — L22 to L26 appear out of order in the table, which is how they were added.)* What remains:

| item | pre-registered gate | cost |
|---|---|---|
| **D2.a** | Input-contract certification retro-applied to every quoted number; must reproduce the 1.78-nat rendering delta and the file-order delta as positive controls. TG-00 now does both and TG-01 has run, so this is partly discharged | ≤4 GPU-h |
| ~~**D2.b**~~ | Causal effect-size distributions on induction: top-20 Jaccard ≥ 0.8 against the census. **RUN, EXP-R2-068 — the gate is not answerable by this instrument.** `11_induction_path_patching.py` computes a causal effect only for heads the census already selected (verified: the per-head records are *exactly* the selected sender set, 57 / 14 / 6 / 8 heads). Three arms were selected by `prefix_matching >= threshold`; **ZymCTRL's 8 came from the `top_k_no_head_above_threshold` fallback with `n_above_threshold: 0`**, so it entered the causal stage with no head above the census cut at all, so a top-20 Jaccard against that same census is **1.0 by construction** on all four arms. The comparison is circular and its result carries no information. Answering it needs causal effects for heads the census did **not** select — the exhaustive causal census §8 item 1 already flags as requiring a new, independently gated design. Restated as D2.b′ below | 0.4 GPU-h, spent |
| ~~**D2.b′**~~ | Exhaustive per-head causal effect on induction, every head patched rather than every selected sender. **RUN (EXP-R2-071). The gate fails on every arm including the text control, and the threshold-free statistic separates the matched pair completely** | ~16 GPU-h, spent |
| **D2.c** | **CLOSED 2026-08-06. Answered by EXP-R2-126, narrowed by EXP-R2-128, and given its scope by EXP-R2-129.** One condition, both layout guards, A1 PASS throughout; final K per arm in §D2.c. **The surviving statement**: a prediction-addressed-attention census on a **residue-tokenised protein** decoder does not recover the causally important heads above its *own* chance level — a median of one hit apiece on the three ProGen2 arms and 0.9× on ZymCTRL, across two architectures, three scales, three corpora and both conditioning states — where every text decoder measured clears 3.6× and the one subword protein decoder reads 7.2× at K=13. Architecture, conditioning, scale, corpus **and symbol granularity** are each excluded by a control built to exclude it; the byte-level text arm at 5.3× against ProGen2-small's 0.5× on an identical 192-head grid is what removed the last of them. **What does not survive as a resolved separation** is the modality ordering on either rank correlation: gaps of +0.0804 and +0.1416 at the most adverse draws, each smaller than one boundary arm's own spread — and EXP-R2-129 showed that statistic is confounded with tokenisation as well as with depth, so it carries nothing. **No further draws of this census are authorised**; the proximal cause is now a CPU question (D2.f) and not a sampling one | spent; ~55 arm-draws across both machines |
| **D2.d** | ~~Complete the TG campaign: six of eleven measured stages have no artefact in the corrected tree, so `SUMMARY.json` can only be produced in partial mode~~ **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-06.** The item was file completeness, not a question: no claim in this document rests on tg02, tg04, tg05, tg06, tg08 or tg10, and `SUMMARY.json`'s partial mode already declares what is absent. Spending ~6 GPU-h to make a summary file whole is the definition of work that cannot change a conclusion. The missing-artefact list stays recorded so the gap is visible; it is reopened only if a claim comes to depend on one of those stages | 0 GPU-h |
| **D2.g** | **Reproduce ProGenMech, and gate it (arXiv:2606.16044, code at `amirgroup-codes/ProGenMech`, CC BY-NC-ND). The main line from 2026-08-06.** It trains cross-layer transcoders on **ProGen3-112M** (10 layers, d_model 384) and reports sparse circuits recovering that model's generative distribution and zero-shot fitness. Feasibility verified before the item was written: ProGen3-112M through -1b are reachable on the mirror, the code is public, and ProteinGym and UniRef50 are already staged. **The reproduction is the cheap half; the contribution is the gates this document already owns.** Their headline reads Spearman **0.28 ± 0.12** for the full CLT and **0.23 ± 0.13** for the circuit, quoted as ~95% and ~80% "performance recovery", and ~60% likelihood recovery — a recovery ratio on a weak base is exactly the shape L1 was earned on, where an 80% gate was applied to an estimand with a 0.02 nat/token footprint and no attainability check. So: is the recovery denominator valid (L4)? Is the gate attainable on a positive control (rule 2)? Does behavioural recovery imply **causal** recovery — do ablations of the original and the replacement rank the same, and does the circuit recover the original's causal top-k above a sparsity-matched random circuit? Does it survive recomputed attention and free-running generation rather than frozen attention and teacher forcing (L7)? Does it hold on family-disjoint assays? **The valuable outcome is symmetric**: if behaviour reproduces and causality does not, the finding is that a protein replacement model can imitate a model it cannot explain — which is this programme's own thesis arriving on someone else's method, and is worth more than another arm of ours. Third-party code stays under ignored `external_resources/baselines/` and is not vendored. **Feasibility settled by EXP-R2-130**: ProGen3-112M runs here with no megablocks via the vendored eager MoE, validated against the paper's NLL and against two negative controls that both score worse than shuffled protein; their released **PLT** weights load and reconstruct correctly from our activations. **Two bounds travel with the item.** Their **CLT** weights are unobtainable — the mirror refuses that directory with HTTP 403 while serving the PLT one on the same token, and there is no direct route — so this audit gates their **baseline, not their headline**, and their central CLT-beats-PLT claim is out of reach until the weights are; retraining a CLT with their code hits the same wall, since `pytorch_lightning`, `polars` and `wandb` cannot be installed in an offline pod. Consequently the harness depends on torch/transformers/numpy/scipy alone and uses their weights with our measurement code, which is what the gating requires anyway. **First result in (EXP-R2-132), and it is a measurement rather than a verdict on the paper.** Under sequential replacement — ground-truth attention, transcoder-reconstructed MoE — the released **PLT** recovers **12.6–13.2%** of the clean-to-fully-ablated NLL gap on two corpus draws, its causal rankings fail against a sparsity-matched control in all six family × run cells, and **attainability passes at ceilings of 0.984–0.994**, so those failures are facts about the replacement and not about the cohort. The sharp asymmetry is that the original resolves all 60 attention heads and all 10 MoE blocks above zero while the replacement resolves ~34 and 5–7. Rule 15b is satisfied by a bfloat16-against-float16 control, fp32 being unavailable on this checkpoint. **It is not yet a discrepancy with ProGenMech**: their headline is the CLT we cannot obtain, their figures are for sparse circuits rather than full replacement, and our reconstruction NMSE sums to 4.42 against the 3.54 their own checkpoint records — with a bit-identical forward — so corpus and masking alone move their metric by about a quarter. Establishing that correspondence is the next step. **Third result in (EXP-R2-135), and it qualifies the first: the verdict is a function of the cohort band.** Across four bands at 128 sequences each, behavioural recovery spans **4.1×** — 0.0425 at 600–1022 to 0.1750 at 64–120, with non-overlapping paired intervals — and the attention-family causal gate **flips to PASS at 64–120** (ρ 0.620, lower bound 0.569 against a 0.5 gate, exact control p = 0.0078) while failing at every longer band. Attainability passes on all four (0.984–0.994) and the original resolves all 60 heads above zero throughout, so this is a property of the replacement and not of the cohort's resolving power. EXP-R2-132 was measured at **64–246, a band chosen for compute**, and sits mid-range; its verdict stands *at its declared band* and is not withdrawn, but no reading of it as a band-free property of the released PLT survives. This is L13's shape arriving on a replacement model, and it shows the cost of an undeclared band is a reversed verdict rather than a shifted number. The band nearest ProGenMech's own cohort is where the replacement is at its worst on our estimand. **Second result (EXP-R2-133), on the fitness arm, and it relocates the whole question.** Their fitness estimand, read from their released code rather than their prose, is a full-sequence mean per-token log-likelihood with no wild-type subtraction; their "~95%" is 0.28/0.29 and their "~80%" is 0.23/**0.28**, whose denominator is the CLT rather than the model; their "~60% likelihood recovery" is `exp(ΔNLL)` between **different sequences** — each model scoring its own samples — and so is a sample-quality ratio, not a fixed-cohort recovery; and their strongest fitness condition, `clt_direct`, replaces **only layer 9 of 10**. Against that, rule 28 was applied to the base itself: on their own eight assays, at their sample size, ProGen3-112M's zero-shot fitness is **not separable from a BLOSUM62 lookup** — +0.066 [−0.110, +0.241] under their class-balanced sampling design and +0.045 [−0.099, +0.190] under a uniform draw, 5 assays of 8 either way. Our reproduction of their base agrees with it (0.3024 and 0.2951 against their 0.29), which is what makes the comparison theirs rather than a different quantity sharing a name. **Bounded immediately by EXP-R2-134, and the bound corrects the strong reading rather than supporting it.** Over the **whole 217-assay** benchmark the model *does* clear the free baseline: +0.0647 [+0.0386, +0.0909], winning 143 of 217, shuffled-label control at +0.0017. So "its zero-shot fitness is not above a substitution matrix" is false of the model. **The effect size is the same on both cohorts — +0.0647 over 217 assays against +0.0637 over their eight — and only the power differs**, [−0.110, +0.241] at n=8 against [+0.039, +0.091] at n=217. The defensible statement is therefore about their **evaluation design**: their panel cannot resolve the very advantage their recovery ratios are quoted against, so a ratio on it cannot separate a circuit that captured the model's fitness computation from one that captured a substitution matrix. That is L1's shape — a ratio applied to an estimand whose footprint is too small to carry it — measured rather than argued, and it is a limitation of the interface rather than of any dictionary built on it. One open question is left rather than closed: ProteinGym records **0.497** for this identical checkpoint over six of the same assays, the sampling design is *not* the explanation (the uniform arm was run to test exactly that and refuted it), and the two largest per-assay gaps are the two most mutation-dense assays. **Their CLT weights stopped being the binding constraint on 2026-08-06 (EXP-R2-136): both architectures are now trained here under one protocol, so the headline is gateable and not only the baseline.** Four such runs existed unlogged and are recorded-not-quoted — the auxiliary loss compared a normalised-space prediction against a raw-space residual, distorting its gradient by up to **25.5×** and misdirecting it (cosine 0.652 at layer 0), **asymmetrically across the two arms under test**, since the self-layer decoder is the PLT's only contributor to its layer and one of up to ten in the CLT. Their held-out cohort was also a corpus-head prefix averaging 394 residues against a 932-residue training stream. Both are repaired and a six-arm campaign is running under one frozen snapshot. **It carries the control their own scripts do not**: a CLT at `d_hidden` 4608 has **3.25× the PLT's parameters**, so "CLT beats PLT" at equal width cannot separate cross-layer connectivity from capacity, and a PLT at `d_hidden` 14963 matches the CLT's parameter count to 3.2e-5 relative (L25). **Item 4 answered, and the pre-registered null holds (EXP-R2-137).** Routing beats a cardinality-matched random grouping on five of ten layers with intervals excluding zero — but the two layers where it removes a materially non-zero share of the residual, +0.0122 and +0.0086, are the two layers with almost no residual to remove (NMSE 0.0024 and 0.1800), while across layers 4–8, where NMSE runs 0.57–0.78 and the replacement genuinely fails, the routing grouping removes **no held-out error at all** (−0.0007 to −0.00002) and merely costs less than a matched random partition, by +0.0002 to +0.0008, against an arithmetic ceiling of 0.0022–0.0034; the term incremental to residue identity is indistinguishable from zero on four of five. Every layer was measurable, so the FAILs are about routing and not about resolving power. **"The router was abstracted away" is a true description of the method and not an account of why the replacement fails** — and EXP-R2-140 closed the one gap that reading had, by testing the *margin* to the routing boundary rather than the identity of the selected pair: that grouping is weaker at every layer and removes nothing at all where the replacement fails, so neither which experts fired nor how close the router came to firing differently accounts for the error, which corroborates arXiv:2606.10703 and arXiv:2604.09780 on a model class neither measured. A text MoE arm is therefore **not earned** on the condition declared in advance. One part-1 result stands on its own: the accuracy of predicting a token's selected expert set from its amino acid alone falls non-monotonically, in skill over the majority-cell rate, from **0.870 at layer 0 to zero by layer 7**, so ProGen3's early routing is very nearly a residue lookup and becomes contextual with depth — which is also why layer 0's incremental term is negative | 10–30 GPU-h; ~3 spent |
| **D2.f** | **CLOSED — FAIL, as pre-registered (EXP-R2-131).** No proximal cause was found at the instance level, so the pre-registered consequence applies: iterative patching of this census ends, and D3.e closes unattempted. F3 carries the result and D3.e's row carries the closure. The pre-registration follows, left standing so the gate is visible. **The proximal cause of the D2.c failure, from retained artefacts only (CPU).** Every alternative *arm-level* explanation is now excluded, and what has never been measured is which property of an instance or a head makes the census's ranking uninformative. Pre-registered factor list, discovery/held-out draw split, grid-matched comparisons only. The cheapest and most likely hypothesis is tested first and would relocate the finding entirely: if almost no head on the failing arms has a causal effect distinguishable from noise, the "causally largest 20 heads" is a ranking of noise and **no** selector could retrieve it — an evaluation-interface limitation rather than a census one. EXP-R2-096 measured exactly this for induction and it has never been done for copy suppression. **PASS** — one factor accounting for roughly half the gap, surviving on held-out draws, with the text controls still above 3.6× under the same adjustment — licenses D3.e. **FAIL** ends iterative patching of this census rather than motivating another round | 0 GPU-h, ≤12 if a held-out confirmation is needed |
| **D2.e** | **ANSWERED at K=4 (EXP-R2-129): the byte-level text control clears the text floor at 5.3x its own chance — hit@20 = 11 on all four draws — against ProGen2-small's 0.5x on an identical 192-head grid, so the census failure is protein-specific and residue tokenisation is not the operative property. The tokenisation account EXP-R2-128 proposed is withdrawn; its exclusions of architecture, conditioning, scale and corpus stand.** A second finding came with it and is the more portable one: the byte arm is protein-like on both rank correlations (all-grid +0.0001 to +0.0355) while text-like on retrieval, so symbol granularity depresses that correlation without impairing the census — a second confound in a statistic already known to be depth-confounded. The pre-registration as written follows. Pre-registered gate, written before the arm could run: `bygpt5-medium-en` — text modality, 384-symbol byte vocabulary, one token per character, a 12x16 grid of exactly **192 heads** and therefore grid-matched to ProGen2-small — enters the PAA census at the panel's declared condition. **If it retrieves above its own chance level like every subword text arm (≥3.6×), the census failure is protein-specific and residue tokenisation is not the operative property.** If it retrieves at or below its own chance the way every residue-tokenised protein arm does, the failure is a property of **symbol-level tokenisation and the census's instance filter**, which makes it an evaluation-interface limitation demonstrated on a text control — a **method** scope under §5's organising rule, not a transfer one. A result between the two bands is reported as unresolved for this arm. Two costs are declared rather than discovered: at pool width 192 a byte-level arm sees 192 characters where gpt2-large sees ~845, which is matched in *symbols* to the residue arms and unmatched in *content* to the BPE text arms (standing rule 26 — the unit is declared, and both readings are reported); and a 384-symbol alphabet may prove ban-depth-pathological the way the 32-symbol residue tokenisers are (L6), in which case the A1 cascade says so and the arm is reported unmeasurable rather than failing. `bygpt5-small-en` (24 heads) and `bygpt5-base-en` (72 heads) are **not** admitted: `hit@20` on a 24-head grid has a chance level of 16.7 against a ceiling of 20 | instrument extension + ~4 arm-draws |

#### EXP-R2-071 — the prefix-matching census is a causal proxy on text and is not one on protein

Four arms, every head patched (720 / 720 / 432 / 720), 64 path cases, all four sender × case conditions, 4 h wall-clock on four GPUs. This is the first measurement in the programme that can see the census's **misses**.

**(i) The all-head rank correlation separates the matched pair completely, and it is stable.** Spearman ρ between prefix-matching score and causal-effect magnitude over every head, range across the four conditions:

| arm | census selects | ρ range across 4 conditions |
|---|---:|---|
| gpt2 | 18/144 (12.50%) | **+0.657 to +0.706** |
| gpt2-large | 57/720 (7.92%) | **+0.428 to +0.507** |
| zymctrl | 0/720 (0.00%) | +0.216 to +0.271 |
| progen2-small | 5/192 (2.60%) | +0.128 to +0.194 |
| progen2-medium | 6/432 (1.39%) | +0.041 to +0.207 |
| protgpt2 | 14/720 (1.94%) | **−0.155 to −0.006** |

The text minimum (+0.428) exceeds every protein maximum (+0.271) in all 24 arm × condition cells.

> **Correction to the first reading of this table.** It was recorded here that the census "carries essentially no information about causal rank" on protein. **That is wrong.** The all-grid ρ is low on protein because the bulk of the grid is unordered, not because the top of it is: restricted to the 32 heads the census scores highest, ρ is **+0.808 on ProtGPT2** against +0.647 (gpt2) and +0.554 (gpt2-large), while over the remaining heads it collapses.
>
> **Corrected again on the nine-arm panel (EXP-R2-072), and one half of it does not generalise.** The top-32 statement was made on two text and two protein arms. Across the range of all four sender × case conditions on nine arms it reads:
>
> | arm | ρ over the top-32 census heads | ρ over the remaining heads |
> |---|---|---:|
> | gpt2 | +0.481 to +0.647 | +0.342 |
> | gpt2-medium | +0.554 to +0.679 | +0.477 |
> | gpt2-large | +0.554 to +0.670 | +0.350 |
> | dialogpt-small | +0.477 to +0.703 | +0.268 |
> | protgpt2 | **+0.792 to +0.824** | **−0.223** |
> | progen2-base | +0.584 to +0.686 | +0.094 |
> | progen2-medium | +0.551 to +0.790 | +0.088 |
> | progen2-small | +0.417 to +0.496 | +0.133 |
> | zymctrl | −0.100 to +0.225 | +0.259 |
>
> **"Protein arms' top heads are ranked better than text's" survives only for ProtGPT2.** ProGen2-base and ProGen2-medium overlap the text range; ProGen2-small sits below every text arm and ZymCTRL is near zero. The *second* half survives and is the load-bearing one: **the remaining-head correlation separates the modalities** — text +0.268 to +0.477 against protein −0.223 to +0.259 — so the census orders the bulk of a text grid and does not order the bulk of a protein grid, which is what makes a head *count* a bad summary of a protein arm.
>
> **Two of the four originally published remaining-head figures were not reproducible and are replaced above.** The recorded +0.128 for gpt2 re-derives as **+0.342** from the cited artefact, and +0.275 for gpt2-large as **+0.350**; ProtGPT2's −0.265 and ProGen2-medium's +0.089 re-derive within 0.04. The cause is Appendix B rule 12 in its sharpest form: these statistics had **no implementation in the repository** and were computed by unversioned throwaway code, so the split that produced them cannot be recovered. They now live in `path_patching.py` with tests, and the direction of the error was conservative — the true text bulk correlation is *higher*, so the contrast is larger than was reported.

**(ii) The structural difference underneath is concentration, and it is head-count free.** ~~*Suspended pending re-derivation — see EXP-R2-072 (v). … Do not cite this table or its qualification until the noise-corrected variant lands.*~~ **Un-suspended (EXP-R2-081, 2026-08-01).** The suspension named a "noise-corrected variant" that was never implemented and never will be — see the correction at (v). It has been replaced by a measurement of the quantity it was worried about. These statistics are head-count free but not **case**-count free, and they sum \|effect\| over a grid whose majority is individually indistinguishable from zero; the question was how much of that integrated mass is estimation noise, and whether its size differs across arms. It does not, on exact probes: probe-clustered reliability of the per-head \|effect\| is 0.861–0.980 on text against 0.823–0.934 on protein, so **the table below is interpretable as it stands and the ZymCTRL exception is real.** The *approximate*-probe qualification stays withdrawn, because there protein reliability falls to 0.466–0.706 and concentration falls with it, and these artefacts cannot separate the two. Every figure in the table re-derives exactly from the retained artefacts under the versioned implementation. Effect concentration across the head grid, using statistics that do not depend on how many heads an arm has:

| arm | Gini of \|effect\| | share of grid carrying half the total effect |
|---|---:|---:|
| gpt2 (text) | 0.829 | 4.9% |
| gpt2-large (text) | 0.826 | 3.2% |
| progen2-small | **0.941** | **1.6%** |
| protgpt2 | **0.940** | **1.0%** |
| progen2-medium | **0.944** | **0.7%** |
| zymctrl | 0.612 | 10.6% |

Three protein arms sit at Gini 0.940–0.944 against 0.826–0.829 for both text arms, with half the causal effect carried by 0.7–1.6% of heads against 3.2–4.9%. **Non-overlapping.** This is the same shape §5.1 reports in the distance dimension — text spreads many small effects, protein concentrates fewer large ones — now measured in the head dimension on an independent estimand. Two measurements, one structure.

> **Qualification, and it is a serious one: the contrast is specific to the exact-repeat case set.** The table above is computed on `cases_exact`. Recomputing every arm's Gini on `cases_approximate` — the same heads, the same run, the other case set — gives gpt2 0.759, gpt2-large 0.752, ProGen2-small 0.772, ProtGPT2 0.822, ProGen2-medium **0.754**, ZymCTRL 0.564. **The separation largely collapses**: ProGen2-medium falls *below* gpt2, ProGen2-small is within 0.02 of it, and only ProtGPT2 remains clearly above. So the honest statement is not "protein concentrates causal effect" but **"protein concentrates causal effect on exact-repeat probes, and the contrast does not survive the approximate-repeat probes"** — which makes it a statement about a probe regime as much as about a modality, and removes the clean "two measurements, one structure" reading of its relation to §5.1. Recorded before the wider panel lands rather than after, because it was found by a check that cost nothing and would have been easy not to run.

**ZymCTRL is the exception and runs the other way** (Gini 0.612, 10.6% of heads for half the effect — the least concentrated arm on the panel). It is also the one arm with a conditioning prompt, so this is a rendering-and-conditioning contrast rather than a modality one, and it is flagged rather than absorbed: any statement of the form "protein concentrates" is false of ZymCTRL as rendered.

**(iii) Noise is not the explanation, and this was tested rather than assumed.** Per-head effect estimates carry SEMs, so the reliability of each arm's head ranking is computable: **0.916 to 0.991** across the six arms, and the SNR of the mean effect is 1.26–6.15. Disattenuating the Pearson correlation for measurement error moves it by at most 0.04 on any arm. The low protein ρ is not attenuation from noisier estimates.

> **Corrected (EXP-R2-072): that range is computed on the sampling unit this module declares to be the wrong one.** `path_patching.py` publishes both a case-level `sem` and a `sem_probe_clustered`, and states in two places that the probe record, not the case, is the sampling unit. The 0.916–0.991 figures reproduce exactly, from the **case**-level SEM on effect *magnitude*. Recomputed by the versioned implementation on the probe-clustered SEM the same statistic reads **0.827–0.975** on the exact-repeat cases, and on the approximate ones ZymCTRL falls to **0.008** — a grid that cannot be ranked at all. **The inference (iii) draws still holds where it matters:** on approximate cases gpt2-large and ProtGPT2 have near-equal probe-clustered reliability while their ρ are far apart, so differential attenuation does not explain the matched-pair gap. But reliability is a *variance* statistic dominated by the few large heads, so a value near 1 was never capable of establishing that the median head is resolved — which is the separate problem EXP-R2-072 (v) records against the concentration statistics. Both units and both scales are now emitted, each labelled, so the choice is visible in the artefact rather than made in an analysis script.
>
> > **Corrected again (EXP-R2-078): the 0.008 is not a reliability at all, and the paired figure is 0.170.** The correction above changed the standard error and left the *centre* alone. `head_effect_reliability` read the observed variance from the case-weighted `mean` under both sampling units, so the probe-clustered figure divided a probe-unit error variance by a case-unit observed variance — two different estimators. `sender_recoveries` states the pairing rule at the point it builds the record: `mean` weights each probe by how many cases it contributed, and `sem_probe_clustered` describes `mean_probe_clustered`, which weights probes equally. Recomputed with each standard error against the centre it belongs to, on the same artefacts:
> >
> > | arm / condition | as published | correctly paired |
> > |---|---:|---:|
> > | ZymCTRL, approximate cases, magnitude | **0.008** | **0.170** |
> > | ZymCTRL, approximate cases, signed | 0.189 | **0.443** |
> > | gpt2-xl, approximate cases, signed | 0.659 | 0.706 |
> > | gpt2-large, approximate cases, signed | 0.779 | 0.807 |
> > | every exact-case condition, ten arms | — | moves by < 0.006 |
> >
> > **What this changes.** "A grid that cannot be ranked at all" is withdrawn as stated: 0.170 is very low — 83% of the head-to-head spread on that condition is estimation noise — but it is twenty times the retracted figure and it is not zero. The exact-repeat range is unaffected, so **nothing that rests on the exact-case reliability moves**, including the (iii) inference that differential attenuation does not explain the matched-pair gap. The mismatch was invisible because the test fixture omitted `mean_probe_clustered` entirely and the two retracted values were pinned as expectations. They are now pinned as *retracted* values, reconstructed in the test from the artefact, so a regression to them fails loudly.

**(iv) The census's failure mode is recall.** ProGen2-medium's top-5 Jaccard is **1.000** and collapses to 0.250 by k=20. The strongest head the census *rejects* carries \|effect\| 0.0194 on gpt2-large against 0.0497 (ProtGPT2), 0.0290 (ProGen2-medium) and 0.3049 on ZymCTRL, where the census selects nothing at all.

> **The cross-arm reading of those four numbers is withdrawn (EXP-R2-072, verified against the artefacts).** A per-head effect is normalised by that arm's own clean-minus-corrupt denominator, and the denominators span **32x** across the panel. Restated in logits:
>
> | arm | eligible | mean denominator (logits) | rejected head, normalised | rejected head, **logits** |
> |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
> | gpt2 | 63/64 | 24.68 | 0.0337 | **0.832** |
> | gpt2-medium | 63/64 | 24.69 | 0.0158 | 0.391 |
> | gpt2-large | 63/64 | 24.10 | 0.0194 | 0.469 |
> | dialogpt-small | 36/64 | 12.41 | 0.0773 | 0.959 |
> | protgpt2 | 64/64 | 20.68 | 0.0497 | **1.027** |
> | progen2-base | 61/64 | 9.22 | 0.1179 | **1.087** |
> | progen2-small | 59/64 | 8.12 | 0.0596 | 0.484 |
> | progen2-medium | 62/64 | 9.36 | 0.0290 | 0.271 |
> | **zymctrl** | **24/64** | **0.76** | **0.3049** | **0.233** |
>
> ZymCTRL's headline 0.3049 — quoted as 15.7x gpt2-large's — is **0.233 logits, the smallest of the nine**. The sentence's force came entirely from a denominator a thirtieth the size of the text control's, itself a consequence of ZymCTRL clearing the eligibility floor on only 24 of 64 cases. **What survives is the within-arm statement**, which is the one that matters for the census: on ZymCTRL the census selects no head at all while heads with real causal effect exist, and the top-k Jaccard collapse with k is a within-arm quantity. Appendix B rule 27 now travels with any cross-arm effect quote.

**(v) What this does and does not say about §4's head-count gap.** It does **not** show that prefix matching is a broken selector on protein — at the top of the grid it is a better one there than on text. It shows that a **count** of heads above a fixed prefix-matching threshold is a poor summary of protein arms specifically, because their causal importance is concentrated in far fewer heads and the threshold sits in a region where the score no longer orders anything. A head count is a statistic about the bulk; protein causal structure is not in the bulk.

**(vi) The pre-registered gate is a specification defect, the second this session.** Top-20 Jaccard ≥ 0.8 is met by **no arm in any condition**; the best observed is 0.667 and the text control never exceeds 0.538. Appendix B rule 2 applies. Withdrawn as stated.

*Limits.* The causal effect is path patching on repeat probes, so a high-effect head is "causally important for repeat prediction", not "an induction head by mechanism". Effects are per-head means over 64 cases and ranking noise depresses Jaccard on every arm alike, which is exactly why the text control is the reference rather than an absolute cut. ProtGPT2's negative ρ values are small and marginal (p ≈ 0.04 at n = 720) and should be read as *uninformative*, not as anti-correlation. Artefacts: `results/transfer_20260730/d2bprime/`.

**D2.b′'s instrument, and why the precondition is enforced rather than documented.** `select_senders` gained an `exhaustive` criterion that admits every head, and `causal_census_agreement` computes the comparison the gate asks for. That function **raises** on a non-exhaustive sender set. The reason is that D2.b's failure is invisible in its own output: a top-20 Jaccard of 1.0 is exactly what a genuine agreement would also produce, so nothing in the artefact distinguished "the two rankings agree" from "the two rankings ranked the same six heads". A comment would not have stopped the next run from reproducing it. Two further points of the design: `above_threshold` became each head's own score against the threshold rather than the set-level answer broadcast to every head — identical on both pre-existing criteria, and the only truthful answer on a set that spans the threshold, which is precisely the field that says which causally-ranked heads the census missed.
