And the primary statistic is the **rank correlation over all heads**, which needs no cut at all; the top-20 Jaccard the gate was written in is reported beside it and swept over k = 5/10/20/32/40, per Appendix B rule 17.

> *Validated end-to-end on GPT-2, and the instrument is not returning the circular answer.* At a deliberately small validation size — 8 cases, 144 heads, 90 s on one L20 — the census ranking and the causal ranking give Spearman **ρ = +0.70** (p = 7×10⁻²³) and a **top-20 Jaccard of 0.538**, against the 1.0 the selective sender set returned by construction. Six of the causal top-20 are heads the census scores *below* threshold, the strongest being L8H9 at prefix-matching 0.018 but causal rank 8. **These are not results and must not be quoted as any:** 8 cases per condition is far too few for a per-head effect estimate, and ranking noise depresses Jaccard on its own. What they establish is that the gate is now answerable — the statistic can fall below 1.0, and census misses can appear in it, neither of which was possible before. The measurement itself is queued behind EXP-R2-070 on four arms.
#### EXP-R2-072 — the off-distribution text control does not collapse, so the census result is not distribution mismatch

The exhaustive census was extended from four arms to nine (gpt2-xl still running), at the identical `--exhaustive-senders --repeat-cohort-size 48` configuration, so all nine combine without a case-count confound. The cohort digests are byte-identical across arms within a modality, which is what licenses pooling them.

**(i) `dialogpt-small` is the control this measurement most needed, and it answers the alternative explanation.** §5.05(a) shows dialogpt-small is off-distribution on the evaluation corpus at −4.08 nats of context information — a *text* decoder that is unmeasurable the way protein arms are often suspected of being. If the protein arms' near-zero census-to-causal correlation were distribution mismatch rather than modality, dialogpt-small should collapse with them. **It does not:** ρ **+0.518 to +0.640**, squarely inside the text range, and *above* gpt2-large. Being off-distribution on the scoring corpus does not destroy the census's causal ordering; being a protein decoder does.

**(ii) The separation is complete on ten arms.** All-head ρ between prefix-matching score and causal-effect magnitude, range across the four sender × case conditions:

| arm | modality | params | heads | census selects | ρ range |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| gpt2 | text | 124M | 144 | 18 (12.50%) | +0.657 to +0.706 |
| gpt2-medium | text | 355M | 384 | 44 (11.46%) | +0.552 to +0.643 |
| dialogpt-small | text | 124M | 144 | 7 (4.86%) | +0.518 to +0.640 |
| gpt2-large | text | 774M | 720 | 57 (7.92%) | +0.428 to +0.507 |
| gpt2-xl | text | 1.56B | 1200 | 78 (6.50%) | **+0.371 to +0.458** |
| zymctrl | protein | 710M | 720 | 0 (0.00%) | **+0.216 to +0.271** |
| progen2-medium | protein | 765M | 432 | 6 (1.39%) | +0.041 to +0.207 |
| progen2-small | protein | 151M | 192 | 5 (2.60%) | +0.128 to +0.194 |
| progen2-base | protein | 765M | 432 | 3 (0.69%) | +0.050 to +0.176 |
| protgpt2 | protein | 774M | 720 | 14 (1.94%) | −0.155 to −0.006 |

The text minimum (+0.371) exceeds every protein maximum (+0.271) in all **40** arm × condition cells, on five text and five protein arms, two of which — gpt2-large and ProtGPT2 — are the matched pair. gpt2-xl's artefact was orphaned by a transport drop and its controller correctly refused to report it complete; it was admitted only after both files were verified against the SHA-256 manifest the worker wrote beside them.

**(iii) The text side carries a monotone scale effect over four rungs, and it runs against the finding rather than producing it.** ρ falls **0.657 → 0.575 → 0.428 → 0.371** across 124M / 355M / 774M / 1.56B, roughly −0.26 per decade. Two consequences, and the first is the one that matters. Extending the ladder puts the panel's *lowest* text correlation at gpt2-xl, so the separation is now measured against the strongest available text counter-example: the margin narrows from 0.16 to **0.10** and does not close. And scale supplies no alternative account — ProGen2-small (151M, 12 layers) reads +0.128 to +0.194 against gpt2 (124M, 12 layers) at +0.657 to +0.706, matched depth and matched scale for a fivefold gap. A pure scale story needs the protein arms on the text ladder's line; at 151M that line predicts ≈+0.65 and the arm reads +0.16. **What the ladder does establish is that this statistic is not scale-free**, so a future protein arm an order of magnitude larger than ProGen2-medium is the measurement that could still overturn it.

**(iv) A second seed moves the statistic by less than the modality gap — but it is a weaker check than its name suggests.** Re-running gpt2-large and ProtGPT2 at `--seed 20260731` gives +0.461 to +0.538 (from +0.428 to +0.507) and −0.180 to −0.055 (from −0.155 to −0.006): both shift by ~0.03 against a gap of ~0.6. **However, the repeat-cohort digests are byte-identical across the two seeds**, because `--seed` governs case sampling and the bootstrap while the corpus draw is governed by a separate `--cohort-draw-seed` (`arms.DEFAULT_CORPUS_DRAW_SEED`). So these are **case-resampling** robustness runs, not cohort-draw robustness runs. Given §5.05(b) and Appendix B rule 22 — cohort sensitivity is a property of the statistic and protein arms carry more of it — a cohort-draw check on this statistic is still owed.

**(v) The concentration statistics of EXP-R2-071 (ii) are under re-derivation and should not be quoted meanwhile.** Adding ProGen2-base at Gini 0.958 (highest on the panel) and dialogpt-small at 0.769 preserves the recorded shape on exact-repeat probes, but the statistic sums \|effect\| over the whole head grid, and more than half of each arm's heads are individually indistinguishable from zero — so the Gini is partly integrating estimation noise whose mass is not matched across arms with unequal eligible-case counts. Both the ZymCTRL exception and the "collapses on approximate probes" qualification are decided in that unresolved bulk. They are suspended, not withdrawn, ~~pending the noise-corrected variant now implemented in `path_patching.py`~~.

> **Correction and resolution (EXP-R2-081, 2026-08-01).** The struck clause was **false**: no noise-corrected concentration variant is implemented in `path_patching.py` or anywhere else in the repository. `effect_concentration` (`path_patching.py:2173`) computes `gini` on raw \|effect\| with no correction. What EXP-R2-078 implemented was `head_effect_reliability`, a *different* statistic — the signal fraction of the observed cross-head variance, not a corrected Gini. As written, this item suspended a result pending a fix that did not exist.
>
> That statistic nevertheless decides item (v), by measuring the very mass the item calls unresolved. Probe-clustered `reliability_magnitude_ranking` over the exhaustive conditions: **exact probes — text 0.861–0.980, protein 0.823–0.934; approximate probes — text 0.745–0.865, protein 0.466–0.706.**
>
> - **On exact probes the noise mass *is* matched across modalities, so concentration is interpretable there, and the ZymCTRL exception is real** — its Gini of **0.607** against **0.78–0.94** across the arms measured in the same alternate draw stands at reliability 0.823 and cannot be deflated into agreement with them. *Draw and arm set stated because they matter:* 0.607 is the exact-probe total-effect Gini on the 2026-08-01 draw; the reference draw gives **0.612**, which is the figure in the (ii) table, and across the full twelve-arm reference panel the other arms span **0.769** (dialogpt-small) to **0.958** (ProGen2-base). The exception holds on either draw and against either comparison set.
> - **On approximate probes it is not matched.** ZymCTRL reaches 0.466, i.e. over half its observed cross-head variance is estimation noise. The "collapses on approximate probes" qualification is therefore confounded with an estimation-noise collapse on the protein side and **stays withdrawn**: concentration does fall on approximate probes for every arm, but on protein the reliability falls with it and these artefacts cannot separate the two.

*Limits.* Same as EXP-R2-071: path patching on repeat probes measures causal importance for repeat prediction, not induction by mechanism; 64 cases per condition. ~~Every text arm on this panel is GPT-2 architecture, so a cross-lab text arm would strengthen (ii) — `induction_path_patching` refuses `qwen2` and `llama` because `SUPPORTED_ARCHITECTURES` has no module layout for them, which is a recorded instrument limit and a candidate extension, not an omission.~~ **Both limits are discharged below: the instrument now resolves the llama and qwen2 layouts (EXP-R2-078) and two rotary/GQA text arms have been measured (EXP-R2-079).** Artefacts: `results/transfer_20260730/d2bprime_ext/`, `results/transfer_20260730/d2bprime_seed2/`.

> **Both owed checks are now in flight (2026-08-01). Neither result is in this document yet; do not anticipate them.**
>
> **The cohort-draw check (iv) asks for — EXP-R2-077.** Four arms at the identical configuration under a different corpus draw: gpt2-xl (the text minimum, the arm whose fall would close the gap from above), ZymCTRL (the protein maximum, whose rise would close it from below), and the matched pair. One alternate draw, not two: at K = 2 a shifted arm cannot be told from a noisy one, so this is posed as an ordering question rather than a variance estimate. **Part of the statistic is immune to the draw by construction and that is worth stating**: Swiss-Prot yields exactly 48 exact-repeat matching records of 203,063 eligible against a request of 48, so the protein exact-repeat cohort is a corpus *census* and its identity cannot change under any seed. The check therefore bites on the approximate conditions and on the text arms.
>
> *A protein-specific fragility surfaced before any GPU time was spent.* ProtGPT2 reaches only **58 of 64** approximate-repeat path cases under draw 20260801 and **57** under 20260803, because a multi-residue BPE puts the repeat's key token outside the unigram support fit on the analysis cohort; ZymCTRL, residue-level, reaches 64 on every draw tested. The entry point refuses to run short, so the arm ran at 20260802 instead and the rejected draws are recorded. **This means ProtGPT2's 64-case parity in EXP-R2-071/072 was itself draw-contingent**, on roughly half the draws tried, and nothing said so — a new instance of the protein-side cohort sensitivity of §5.05(b), reaching the case set rather than the estimate.
>
> **The cross-lab control this limits paragraph asks for — EXP-R2-079.** The instrument limit is lifted: `path_patching` now resolves the llama and qwen2 layouts, validated end to end on a Qwen2.5-0.5B checkpoint at 336 exhaustive senders with head-write linearity at 2.2e-06 relative error under grouped-query attention (EXP-R2-078). qwen2.5-0.5b and llama-3.2-3b run at the identical configuration. **Pre-registered, because the scale trend makes one arm ambiguous:** extrapolating (iii)'s −0.26 per decade predicts ≈+0.60 for qwen (494M) and **≈+0.29 for llama (3.21B) — within reach of the protein maximum of +0.271**. A llama value near 0.29 is therefore what the scale trend predicts and is *not* evidence against L22; a value near zero or negative, like ProtGPT2's, would be. Written down before the run, because reading a narrow margin either way afterwards is the failure Appendix B rule 2 keeps finding.

**Both checks are in (2026-08-01). Results below; the pre-registration above is left standing so the miss is visible.**

**EXP-R2-077 — the cohort draw.** Case seed held fixed so only the corpus draw moves. All four cells per arm, reference → alternate draw:

| arm | exact/exact | exact/approx | approx/exact | approx/approx |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| gpt2-large | +0.4276 → **+0.5350** | +0.4402 → **+0.5034** | +0.4725 → **+0.5089** | +0.5071 → **+0.5217** |
| gpt2-xl | +0.4087 → **+0.4448** | +0.3711 → **+0.4028** | +0.4576 → **+0.4261** | +0.4404 → **+0.4150** |
| zymctrl | +0.2706 → **+0.2823** | +0.2197 → **+0.2468** | +0.2490 → **+0.2098** | +0.2160 → **+0.2269** |
| protgpt2 | −0.0763 → **−0.1513** | −0.1554 → **−0.2259** | −0.0058 → **−0.0547** | −0.0537 → **−0.0610** |

The ordering survives, and it survives in the informative way: **the matched pair moved in opposite directions** — gpt2-large up in all four cells, ProtGPT2 down in all four — so this is not a common-mode shift of the panel. Text minimum +0.3711 → +0.4028, protein maximum +0.2706 → +0.2823, gap **+0.1005 → +0.1205**. The pre-stated census property held in the artefact: the protein exact-repeat cohort digest is byte-identical across draws (`3ea68a87c593c532`).

**EXP-R2-079 — the cross-lab control, and a miscalibrated pre-registration.** Exhaustive over every head; all nine structural invariants pass on both arms — eight quantitative checks at ≤2.5e-06 against a 1e-03 tolerance, worst case `head_write_linearity`, plus the `freeze_only_perturbed` positive control, which is required to *move* the metric rather than to leave it unchanged.

| arm | arch | grid | exact/exact | exact/approx | approx/exact | approx/approx | pre-registered |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| llama-3.2-3b | llama | 672 | +0.5034 | +0.4794 | +0.5046 | +0.5038 | ≈ +0.29 |
| qwen2.5-0.5b | qwen2 | 336 | +0.5749 | +0.5267 | +0.5746 | +0.5555 | ≈ +0.60 |

qwen landed near prediction. **llama did not — predicted ≈+0.29 "within reach of the protein maximum", observed +0.4794 to +0.5046, high by ≈+0.19.** The scale-trend extrapolation under-predicts this lineage. The miss is in the direction that strengthens L22, which is precisely when it is tempting to leave unstated, so it is stated: the −0.26-per-decade trend of (iii) does not transfer across lineages and must not be used to pre-register a cross-lab arm again.

**L22 is now architecture-controlled.** Its 40 cells had every text arm on GPT-2 architecture, so modality was confounded with lineage — and ProtGPT2 *being* a GPT-2 made that the obvious attack. At 48 cells over twelve arms the confound is cut both ways: two text lineages from different labs, with different tokenizers, position encodings and attention layouts (llama, qwen: **+0.4794 … +0.5749**) sit above the protein ceiling, while the arm sharing ProtGPT2's exact architecture sits furthest above it. All-text minimum **+0.4028** against protein maximum **+0.2823**, separated in every cell. **The separation tracks modality, not architecture.**

*The limit that is now binding.* Largest single-cell movement between draws **0.1073**; mean absolute movement over sixteen cells **0.0398**; closest modality approach **0.1205**. One cell moved almost as far as the whole gap, so at K = 2 the gap cannot yet be quoted against a spread — only as a surviving ordering. **EXP-R2-080** (launched 2026-08-01, ≈44 GPU-h) takes the four gap-defining arms to K = 4 (K = 3 for gpt2-xl, which costs 11 GPU-h per draw — a budget asymmetry, recorded not smoothed) and gives the eight remaining arms their first alternate draw, so all 48 cells contribute.

**EXP-R2-081 — the attenuation objection, tested and survived.** The most serious objection available to L22 is that protein \|effect\| estimates are noisier, so the rank correlation is attenuated harder on protein — *toward zero, the direction of the hypothesis*. It is the failure `corruption_effects` had to fix for the matching gate ("a conclusion the estimator manufactured out of alphabet size"), and it had not been tested. **The objection has a real basis:** probe-clustered reliability of the per-head \|effect\| is 0.823–0.934 on protein against 0.861–0.980 on text for exact probes, and **0.466–0.706 against 0.745–0.865** for approximate probes — ZymCTRL's approximate conditions are over half estimation noise. **The separation survives correction in all 24 measured cells.** Under classical disattenuation r → r∕√reliability the gap widens in two conditions and narrows in the two carried by ZymCTRL's noisy approximate cells, worst case exact/approx at **+0.1560 → +0.0946**; pooled over all 24 cells, text minimum +0.4560 against protein maximum +0.3613. Correcting for the differential noise does not close the gap. *Limits:* only the causal side is disattenuated (census-score reliability is unmeasured, so these are lower bounds, and the same one-sided assumption is applied to both modalities); the formula is derived for Pearson and applied to Spearman; and the reliability used is a variance ratio for the per-head means, not literally the rank variable's. A sensitivity analysis, not an exact correction — decisive only because the conclusion does not depend on the correction's size.

### D2.c — copy suppression: what the census recovers, and what it does not

D2.c asks whether a head-prevalence census picks out causally important heads for a *second* mechanism, copy suppression, as L22 asked for induction. It is measured on twelve arms at one condition (recorded as eleven until 2026-08-09; `panel_contract`'s `paa_census` eligible list holds twelve, ZymCTRL being refused there and run at its own configuration) — pool width 192, `--census-ban-depth 3`, `--census-sequences 600` — with both layout guards applied, every arm passing its A1 candidate-pool gate, three to five corpus draws each, and the statistic computed by `prediction_addressed.census_causal_agreement` rather than by analysis code.

**The surviving claim.** Retrieval of the causally largest heads by the census's own top-20, measured against **each arm's own chance level** so no cross-grid comparison is involved: every text arm exceeds its chance by **≥3.6×** at the median, and all three ProGen2 arms sit **at or below** it — 1.1×, 0.5×, 0.0× over fifteen draws spanning three model sizes and two pretraining corpora. That is the condition under which a published head count carries no information about the mechanism.

> **The claim is no longer about a lineage. ZymCTRL breaks the architecture confound (EXP-R2-128, four draws) — but read the EXP-R2-129 block below before taking its positive account: the tokenisation reading offered here was refuted by the byte-level text control built to test it.** Across the four protein arms EXP-R2-126 measured, **architecture and tokenisation covary perfectly**: ProtGPT2 is gpt2-architecture with multi-residue BPE and retrieves at 4.5× its own chance; the three ProGen2 arms are progen-architecture with residue tokenisation and retrieve at 0.0–1.1×. ZymCTRL is the cell that separates the two accounts — **gpt2 architecture with residue tokenisation** — and it is admissible here because the surviving statistic is a *within-arm* classification against the arm's own chance level, which needs no shared cohort. Its own configuration, forced by its conditioning prompt: width 406, band [396, 396], n=400, reference band decoupled from the cohort band.
>
> | draw | A1 | all-grid ρ | within-layer | hit@20 | × own chance |
> |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
> | 20260728 | 137,262 | −0.1911 | −0.1330 | 0/20 | 0.0× |
> | 20260801 | 137,293 | −0.3358 | −0.2298 | 0/20 | 0.0× |
> | 20260802 | 137,297 | −0.2492 | −0.2246 | 1/20 | 1.8× |
> | 20260803 | 137,283 | −0.3326 | −0.2065 | 1/20 | 1.8× |
>
> Median **0.9× its own chance**, inside the ProGen2 band; all-grid ρ spans **[−0.336, −0.191]**, the most negative on the panel. Sorted by the two candidate explanations:
>
> | arm | architecture | tokenisation | conditioned | × own chance |
> |---|---|---|---|---:|
> | gpt2-large | gpt2 | BPE 50257 | no | 12.6× |
> | ProtGPT2 | gpt2 | multi-residue BPE 50257 | no | 4.5× |
> | **ZymCTRL** | **gpt2** | **residue 458** | **yes** | **0.9×** |
> | ProGen2-base | progen | residue 32 | no | 1.1× |
> | ProGen2-small | progen | residue 32 | no | 0.5× |
> | ProGen2-medium | progen | residue 32 | no | 0.0× |
>
> *ProtGPT2's 4.5× is EXP-R2-126's K=4 figure and it reproduces exactly — both ρ endpoints to the digit — once its four draws are found: two in the single-arm `d2c_protgpt2_dfix_*` roots and two in the panel root, all at one condition. **At K=13 the arm reads 7.2× its own chance**, on `0,1,2,2,3,3,4,5,6,6,7,7,8`, with 12 of 13 draws at or above chance. A bimodality recorded here at K=8 — four draws at 0–2, four at 5–7 — **was a hole in a small sample and is withdrawn**; the six draws run to test it closed the gap. What survives is that **this arm's retrieval is broadly dispersed across corpus draws**, spanning 8 hits where gpt2-large spans 2 on the same 720-head grid at the same condition. That is the protein-side cohort sensitivity of §5.05(b) on a further statistic (F9 carries the count) and on the designed matched pair, and it is held as an observation rather than a measurement until the text control reaches comparable K — an arm with more draws has more opportunity to show its tails.*
>
> **Architecture is ruled out** — ZymCTRL shares ProtGPT2's architecture and sits at the opposite end. **Conditioning is ruled out too**, and that is a correction to EXP-R2-128's own first reading, which recorded conditioning as a surviving alternative: it is a property of ZymCTRL alone, while the three ProGen2 arms are unconditioned and fail. Conditioning stays a caveat on ZymCTRL's *magnitude* (L15, 1.73 nats) rather than an account of the class. Scale and pretraining corpus were already excluded across the three ProGen2 rungs. The comparison is also **grid-matched**, which this statistic requires: gpt2-large, ProtGPT2 and ZymCTRL all carry 720-head grids. **Those exclusions are what this arm contributes and they all stand; the tokenisation reading it suggested does not survive EXP-R2-129, immediately below.**
>
> **The confound the panel could not break has now been broken, and it broke against the tokenisation reading (EXP-R2-129, K=4 — recorded here as K=2 until 2026-08-09, against the four draws in this block's own table, the "Limits" note below it, D2.e's row and F2).** Every symbol-level-tokenised arm in the panel was a protein model and every text arm was subword-tokenised, so *residue tokenisation* and *protein modality* were collinear across the failing set. `bygpt5-medium-en` separates them: text modality, one token per character, a 384-symbol vocabulary of which 106 occur, and a 12x16 grid of exactly **192 heads** that grid-matches ProGen2-small — the only comparison `hit@k`'s grid-dependent chance level permits.
>
> | | ByGPT5-medium (byte, **text**) | ProGen2-small (residue, **protein**) |
> |---|---:|---:|
> | draws | 4 | 4 |
> | instances retained | 84,667 – 84,713 | 90,936 – 91,323 |
> | lost to an empty decoy pool | 266 – 306 (**0.3%**) | 3,401 – 3,716 (**3.7%**) |
> | **hit@20** | **11, 11, 11, 11** | **0, 1, 1, 2** |
> | **x own chance** | **5.3x** | **0.5x** |
> | all-grid ρ | −0.1159 … +0.1355 | −0.2532 … −0.1186 |
>
> **The pre-registered gate is met on its "≥3.6x" branch, which says the tokenisation account is wrong**, and the account is withdrawn. What EXP-R2-128 excluded stands: not architecture, not conditioning, not scale, not corpus. What it proposed in their place does not: a decoder with one token per symbol and an alphabet no larger than ZymCTRL's recovers its own causally important heads at five times its chance level. Instance count (7% apart, where a 3x change moves this family of statistics by 0.002–0.038), alphabet pathology (the byte arm is the *least* affected of the three) and cohort qualification (+2.462 nats, measurable) are all answered from the artefacts.
>
> **The surviving statement is therefore about the protein arms and not about their tokenizer:** every residue-tokenised **protein** decoder measured fails to recover its own causally important heads — two architectures, three parameter scales, three pretraining corpora, both conditioning states — while every text decoder measured recovers well above its own chance level, **including one whose tokenisation is symbol-level**. The one subword protein decoder does not fail either, so within the protein modality tokenisation still separates the arms; what it does not do is explain the failure.
>
> **And the run produces a dissociation that is the more useful half of it.** On retrieval the byte arm is text-like; on both rank correlations it is protein-like (all-grid +0.0001 to +0.0355 against the subword text arms' +0.17 to +0.65, with negative top-5% and bulk strata like ProGen2-small's). **Symbol-level tokenisation depresses the all-grid rank correlation and does not stop a census from working.** That is direct evidence for what §8 item 0 concluded on other grounds: this correlation is not a measure of census validity. It was already known to be depth-confounded on every arm (EXP-R2-120); it is now also known to be tokenisation-confounded.
>
> *Limits.* K=4, satisfying evidence rule 4 — and the four draws return the *same integer*, 11, on the statistic that carries the claim, where ProtGPT2's spans 1 to 8 over seven draws. `hit@k` binds this arm to ProGen2-small's 192-head grid and to no other. At width 192 the arm sees 192 characters against gpt2-large's ~845 — matched in **symbols** to ProGen2-small, which is the axis in question, and unmatched in **content** to the BPE text arms, which is why the claim is made against ProGen2-small. And it is one rung: byte-level text is n=1 here in the way §2 records for the protein modality coefficient.

**What does not survive.** The two rank-correlation statistics order the modalities but do not separate them once a second text lineage is present:

| statistic | boundary arms | gap at most adverse draws | boundary arm's own draw range |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| all-grid ρ | llama-3.2-3b vs ProtGPT2 | **+0.0804** | ProtGPT2 0.109 |
| within-layer ρ | qwen2.5-0.5b vs ProGen2-small | **+0.1416** | ProGen2-small 0.224 |

Each gap is smaller than one boundary arm's spread across draws — the standard that narrowed L22 (§5, L22 row). The all-grid gap was **+0.2926** before llama entered at K=3 and the depth-controlled gap **+0.3339** on the matched pair alone before qwen did. **The cross-lab control is what turns a two-GPT-2-arm separation into a claim or refuses to, and here it refuses.**

**Three limits that do not move.** ZymCTRL is structurally excluded from any window ProtGPT2 can share (blocker 1 below), and that exclusion is unchanged by EXP-R2-128: it bars ZymCTRL from every **cross-arm range** in this section, and it does not bar the **within-arm** classification, which compares an arm's census against its own chance level and needs no shared cohort. The distinction was conflated until 2026-08-05 and is what kept the arm out of D2.c for a week. ProtGPT2 is intermediate on every statistic and carries the panel's least stable retrieval — the protein-side cohort sensitivity of §5.05(b) on a further statistic (F9 carries the count), and the reason further ProtGPT2 draws are the one place added compute can still change a D2.c reading. And `hit@20` has a grid-dependent ceiling and a grid-dependent chance level, so it is comparable only within a grid size: the 720-head grid now holds **three** arms — gpt2-large 6,7,7,8 against ProtGPT2 0,0,5,6 against ZymCTRL 0,0,1,1 — where the matched pair's ranges overlap and ZymCTRL's do not overlap gpt2-large's. **These are the K=4 draws; ProtGPT2's are superseded at K=13 by the block above** (`0,1,2,2,3,3,4,5,6,6,7,7,8`, 7.2x its own chance), which also withdraws the K=8 bimodality. The three-arm comparison stands at K=4, where all three arms have draws.

**Two instrument repairs stand behind these numbers**, both found after the earlier readings were published and both changing them:

- *The layout guard was absent, then incomplete.* `prediction_addressed.py` had no layout-token guard at all, so 28–33% of ProtGPT2's PAA instances were FASTA line breaks carrying >100% of its clean-margin mass (EXP-R2-116). The repair (EXP-R2-118) covered the predicted token and left the **decoy pool** open, and the decoy mean is the subtrahend of every census score: 4.30% of ProtGPT2's decoy keys and 2.74% of gpt2-large's were still the wrap (EXP-R2-124). Both paths are now closed and the arithmetic is paired: ProtGPT2 +0.1987 → +0.0743, gpt2-large +0.4495 → +0.4603 on an identical draw.
- *The first correction of the first defect was one-sided and is withdrawn.* EXP-R2-116 recomputed from retained matrices, but `paa_specific_matched_per_sequence` is a per-sequence aggregate and cannot be filtered after a run, so it correlated a contaminated census score against a clean causal effect (EXP-R2-119). Its −0.088 is reproduced by nothing; the rebuilt pools give +0.1778 to +0.1987 at K=2. **Every EXP-R2-116 and EXP-R2-117 figure is superseded**, including the "gap +0.364, ratio 2.49" restatement and the layout-corrected columns derived from it.

**The record below is the design pass and the superseded readings.** It is kept because the reasoning that produced the wrong prescriptions is the point — blocker 1's band-versus-width diagnosis, the attainability gate and its re-specification, and the sequence of partial answers the two defects were hiding. **No number below is current.**

#### Record — the design pass, and the three things that had to be built first

> **Status (2026-08-01): all three are cleared; one attainability check stands between this and the panel.** Blocker 3 was cleared in EXP-R2-078 (the positive control exists on the magnitude scale), blocker 2 closed in code in the same pass, and blocker 1 was re-measured in EXP-R2-082 — where the prescription written below turned out to be **wrong**, and the fix is the pool width rather than the cohort band. The section is left standing with its errors struck and corrected in place, because the reasoning that produced the wrong prescription is the point. Read the blocker-1 correction block and the revised order of work at the end.

Costed at "~13 GPU-h" while it was one line in a table. A design pass
(EXP-R2-078) put that at **12–25 H200-hours** for a ten-arm panel and found that
the measurement as imagined cannot be run at all on the two arms that matter
most. Recorded here before any compute is scheduled, because the plan's own
history is that a gate written into a table and costed by analogy is how
Appendix B rule 2 keeps being violated.

**What D2.c is.** L22 says a head-prevalence census's selector orders causal
importance on text decoders and not on protein decoders, measured on *one*
mechanism. §8 item 0 is gated on replicating that on a second mechanism, copy
suppression: score every head with `paa_specific`, measure every head's causal
effect by knockout, and compare the two rankings — the same design as D2.b′, a
different selector and a different metric.

**Blocker 1 — the matched pair cannot enter the estimand.** Verified directly
against the tokenisers, not inferred:

| arm | tokens per residue | residues needed for a 512-token row | admitted at width 512, band 520–800 |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| progen2-medium | 1.001 | ~511 | yes |
| zymctrl | 1.016 | ~504 | **only at exactly 502 residues** |
| protgpt2 | 0.349 | **~1468** | **none** — but **10037 instances at width 192** (EXP-R2-088) |

ProtGPT2's multi-residue BPE means no row inside the cohort band reaches the
pool's 512-token width, so `tokenised_rows` refuses the arm outright. ZymCTRL is
worse than a band problem: its conditioned rendering is
`{ec}<sep><start>{seq}<end>`, `content_bounds` requires exactly one `<end>` inside
the window, and the two conditions intersect at the single residue length
`width − 10`. **So D2.c as specified would run on the GPT-2 lineage against the
ProGen2 lineage and would not include the matched pair** — the only
modality-identifying comparison this panel has (§2). A cross-lineage-only result
on the second mechanism cannot discharge a gate about the first. ~~Fixing it means
raising `--protein-max-len` past ~1500 for ProtGPT2, which moves the cohort band
and is an L13 exposure that must be declared and measured~~, and giving ZymCTRL a
window co-designed with its conditioning prompt — the same wall L15 put in front
of the far-band estimand.

> **Blocker 1 re-measured (EXP-R2-082, 2026-08-01, CPU on B, no GPU). The prescription above was wrong, and the matched pair is available at zero L13 exposure.** Four corrections and one replacement design.
>
> **(1) Raising `--protein-max-len` does not work, because it is not the binding parameter.** The census band is `[--census-protein-min-len, --protein-max-len]` and the *floor* binds. Admitted rows out of 400 at width 512: band `[520, 1500]` → 7; `[520, 2000]` → 17; **`[520, 4000]` → 36**, still below `--min-sequences 64`, so the arm keeps failing. Full admission needs the **floor** raised to ~1550 (`[1550, 4000]` → 400/400). The struck clause would not have fixed the arm.
>
> **(2) The refusal is width-conditional, not unconditional — and this is the fix.** In the **unchanged** band 520–800, ProtGPT2 admits **400/400 at width 128, 320–355/400 at width 192, 65/400 at width 256, and 0 at width ≥ 320**. (Two independent measurements: a direct tokenisation of the declared `fasta_wrapped` rendering and a run through `tokenised_rows` itself agree exactly at 256 and at ≥ 320 and differ by ~9% at 192, from cohort-construction filtering. Tokens per residue 0.352 against the table's band-restricted 0.351.) **Lowering the pool width admits the matched pair without touching the band at all.**
>
> **(3) ZymCTRL's "1.016 tokens per residue" is a band artefact, not a tokeniser property.** Its rendering is exactly `R + 10` tokens on 5000/5000 records — a *constant* prefix, so the "rate" is `1 + 10/R`. Reporting it as a rate disguises that the constraint is exact: `tokenised_rows` requires `total == width`, so the admissible length is the single point `R = width − 10`, which the same paragraph then states correctly. The EC prefix is 9 tokens for 212,144 of 212,165 eligible records and 10 for 21 of them (a three-digit EC field splits), giving **two disjoint points, not a range** — and the two cannot be mixed, since `tokenised_rows` rejects inconsistent content offsets.
>
> **(4) Not previously recorded: the single-length ZymCTRL window breaks `build_cohorts`.** `14_paa_census.py` draws its reference corpus from the *same* band with a skip, and no exact residue length can supply it — the largest single length holds 959 records against a request of 4000. A trial run died on exactly this at a request of 600. Eighteen further lengths are poisoned by the 10-token EC tags. So the ZymCTRL window is not merely narrow; as specified it does not run.
>
> **The replacement design — width, not band.** `--width 192` with the cohort band **unchanged** at 520–800: gpt2-large admits 400/400 (attainability on the control, rule 2), ProtGPT2 320–355/400. **L13 exposure is zero** — identical band, identical eligible stratum — so a D2.c result can actually be read against L22, which sits in the same 200–800 protein regime. Forwards are ~2.7× cheaper than at width 512. And blocker 2 nearly vanishes on the pair that matters: median key set 1 on ProtGPT2 against 2 on gpt2-large, so the residual mismatch runs **against** the hypothesis rather than for it, unlike the cross-lineage fallback's 17 against 3.
>
> **Measured, and it holds — EXP-R2-088.** ProtGPT2 builds **10037** instances at width 192 (the width-512 reading was *none*), and A2 passes for both protein arms at both ban depths against a gate of 2000: ProtGPT2 **11263** projected at ban 20 and **11545** at ban 3, ProGen2-medium **5318** and **11941**. gpt2-large's own key set is **2.86 keys per instance at width 192** against 5.52 at width 512, and the cheap selector agrees far better with the knockout-matched score in the narrow window — partial Spearman **0.580** against **0.390** — which is a second, independent reason the width route helps that the design did not claim. **The matched pair needs no declared ban-depth asymmetry**: at vocabulary 50257 the specified depth 20 costs gpt2-large and ProtGPT2 the same nothing. *One new constraint, from the pool rather than from assumption:* ProtGPT2 yields ~50 instances per sequence, so at the census default of 200 sequences it returns 10037 and would read **FAIL** against `--a1-minimum 20000`; D2.c needs a cohort request nearer 600 records, which at width 192 is cheap.
>
> *Three costs, declared not hidden.* ~~The blocker-3 positive control (+0.53 to +0.65) is a width-512 measurement and **must be re-established at width 192 before anything is applied** — if it fails there, this design is dead.~~ **Re-established and passed at width 192 (EXP-R2-087), but not against that number**, which is a restricted-range *unmatched*-score figure whose own reproducibility at fixed width is ±0.12; the exhaustive matched reading is +0.4515 [+0.401, +0.498] against gpt2-large's induction band of +0.428 to +0.535. Two of eight distance bins become unreachable at width 192, so the estimand no longer probes long-range PAA; symmetric across the pair, but it narrows the claim. And the width filter selects ProtGPT2 records on **BPE compressibility** (+1.20 sd of tokens-per-residue at width 192, raising mean cohort length 628 → 643) while rejecting no text document at any width — a selection that exists only on the protein side because only there does a token stop being a residue.
>
> **What the band route would have cost, now measured.** Moving to 1500–4000 shares **not one record** with the band L22's protein arms were measured on, shrinks the eligible stratum 12.6×, raises internal 3-mer repeat content 2.7× — the very property both mechanisms are defined on — and moves context information by **−1.75 nats at a common window, −2.31 under the gate0 formula**, against L13's catalogued 1.01. *The prescription in the struck clause would have cost 1.7–2.3× the incident that created L13.* (Validation scale, 96/1500 sequences; direction and magnitude secure, third decimal not.)
>
> **ZymCTRL remains irreducibly outside any shared window.** ProtGPT2 needs `t/r ≥ width/(width − 10) > 1` and never exceeds 0.40; `width − 10 ≥ 2.5·width` has no positive solution, and 0 of 581 records at R = 502 reach 512 ProtGPT2 tokens. **No width, at any cohort size, admits both.** ZymCTRL is therefore a separately declared per-arm configuration (`--width 348`, band 338–338, 400/400 admitted) or it is not run — and either way it cannot contribute to a statement about a common cohort.

**Blocker 2 — the census and the causal statistic are measured on different key
sets, and the difference is alphabet-size-dependent.** `paa_attention_scores`
scores attention onto `pool.antecedent`, the *nearest* earlier occurrence of the
predicted token. `knockout_effects` removes every earlier occurrence, via
`antecedent_sets`. Counted from the shipped pools, occurrences before the query:
median **3** per instance on gpt2-large against **13–17** on ProGen2-medium. The
selector therefore sees one of three keys on the text arm and one of thirteen on
the protein arm, so a rank correlation between the two is attenuated harder on
protein *by construction* — in the direction of the hypothesis. This is the same
error `corruption_effects` already fixed for the matching gate, whose docstring
says it plainly: "a conclusion the estimator manufactured out of alphabet size".

**Closed in code (EXP-R2-078), and the size of it is now measured rather than
argued.** `paa_attention_scores` emits `paa_specific_matched` beside
`paa_specific`: attention summed over the key set `antecedent_sets` returns — the
one the knockout removes — against a decoy baseline scaled to the same number of
keys, so the correction stays a positional baseline of matched size rather than a
per-key one subtracted from a sum. Both are returned, because `paa_specific` is
what EXP-R2-059 published and L5/L6 quote, and redefining it in place would make
those numbers unreproducible. *On a validation-scale run — 8 sequences, gpt2-large,
not a result and not quotable as one — the mean key set is 2.93 and the two scores
rank the 720 heads at Spearman **+0.567**.* Part of that is estimation noise at
this cohort size, but it establishes that the two definitions are not
interchangeable **on the arm where the mismatch is smallest**; on a protein arm at
13–17 keys the divergence can only be larger. D2.c must use the matched score.

**Blocker 3, now cleared — the positive control is probably attainable.** The
plan had inherited from L5 the belief that `paa_specific` does not rank causal
effect even on gpt2-large, which would make D2.c a gate no text control can pass.
That figure is on the signed effect; on the magnitude scale L22 uses, the same
heads read **+0.53 to +0.65**. See the L5 block in §5.1 for the table and for the
two limits on that reading.

**Cost, re-derived rather than carried over.** `knockout_effects` needs
`ceil(N/B) × (2 + H)` forward passes for `H` heads and `N` instances: 36,100 at
gpt2-large's 720 heads and 800 instances, against the 24 heads the gate stage runs
today. Anchored on EXP-R2-059's measured 0.73 s per (16, 512) forward, that is
**3–5 H200-hours for gpt2-large alone** and 23–39 for a ten-arm panel, with
gpt2-xl 42% of it. The stage also materialises the full `[batch, token, vocab]`
logit tensor on every pass where one position is read, allocates a dense
`(B, n_head, W, W)` knockout mask per head per batch, and leaves `use_cache` on —
`path_patching._readout` avoids the first of these and documents why. Fixing the
three brings the panel to **12–25 H200-hours**. TF32 would recover roughly 4x more
but must first pass the quantisation check that rejected bfloat16 for this metric,
which is cheap and has not been run.

**Order of work, and it is not "start the campaign".** ~~Fix blocker 2; decide and
declare the protein band and window that admit ProtGPT2 and ZymCTRL, or record
that the matched pair is unavailable and scope D2.c's claim accordingly;~~ run the
exhaustive census on gpt2-large alone and check the all-grid positive control
against the restricted-range +0.53 to +0.65; only then schedule the panel.

**Order of work as it now stands (EXP-R2-078, EXP-R2-082).** The first two items
are done: blocker 2 is closed in code, and the band question is decided — the fix
is the **pool width**, not the band, so the matched pair enters at **`--width 192`
with the cohort band unchanged** and at zero L13 exposure, while **ZymCTRL is
irreducibly excluded from any shared window** and is a separately declared per-arm
configuration or is not run. What remains, in order:

1. ~~**The attainability check, and nothing may be scheduled before it.** gpt2-large's
   exhaustive PAA census at **width 192**, read against the +0.53 to +0.65 the
   restricted-range reading gives at width 512.~~ **DONE, and the criterion was
   replaced before it was read — EXP-R2-087.** Both widths were measured
   exhaustively over all 720 heads (EXP-R2-083/084), which showed the
   pre-registered comparison cannot decide width:

   > **Why the stated criterion was withdrawn, on evidence that does not involve the
   > width-192 number.** The width-512 / restricted-24 / unmatched cell is nominally
   > *identical* between the historical run and a fresh one, and reads **+0.6078**
   > against **+0.4922** — a drift of **0.116 at fixed width**, against a width
   > effect of +0.08. A target reproducible only to ±0.12 cannot resolve a +0.08
   > difference. The census is not the culprit (Spearman +0.986 across the cohort
   > change, max |difference| 1.5e-3); the causal half contributes −0.065, the
   > cohort swap −0.020 and re-selecting the 24 heads on the new ranking −0.047.
   > **And the target is on the wrong score**: the historical artefacts are schema
   > `r2_transfer_paa_gate_v1` and contain no `paa_specific_matched` at all, so
   > +0.53–+0.65 is an *unmatched* figure while this section requires the matched
   > one. Appendix B rule 2, third instance. The correction is outcome-independent:
   > all of it is measurable at width 512 alone.

   **The replacement, and it is the one §5.1 already draws.** gpt2-large's width-192
   copy-suppression census must order causal importance about as well as
   gpt2-large's own **induction** census does — the text control L22 is measured
   against. Exhaustive, magnitude statistic, matched score, bootstrapped over the
   200 sequences with census and causal resampled jointly (alignment verified, and
   the resample regenerates every published per-head `delta_m_gap` exactly).

   | width | exhaustive ρ (matched) | 95% CI |
   |---:|---:|---|
   | 192 | **+0.4515** | [+0.401, +0.498] |
   | 512 | **+0.5309** | [+0.451, +0.547] |

   **PASS.** +0.4515 lands inside the induction band **+0.428 to +0.535** (same arm,
   same statistic, also exhaustive). The width penalty is **+0.079** (CI [−0.015,
   +0.120]) on the matched score and +0.083 (CI [+0.016, +0.118]) unmatched — the
   two agree, so width 192 costs about 0.08 and at most ~0.12. **The design is not
   dead and the width-512 fallback at band 1550–4000 is not needed**, so the full
   1.75–2.31 nat L13 exposure is not paid.

   > **And the pass is draw-robust — EXP-R2-089.** A margin of +0.0239 read off one
   > corpus draw is not a decision, given the same class of statistic was measured
   > moving 0.107 between draws on this very arm. Re-run at three further draws
   > (cohort digests all distinct, A1 instances 21415–21619): **+0.4515, +0.4669,
   > +0.5206, +0.4522** — **4 of 4 inside the band**, worst draw clearing the floor
   > by +0.0239. **The draw the gate was decided on is the lowest of the four**, so
   > the original reading was the conservative one. The statistic's own draw range
   > is 0.0691 matched and 0.0282 unmatched, against the 0.1073 the *induction*
   > reading shows on the same arm and condition — exhaustiveness buys draw
   > stability, which is a further argument for the exhaustive criterion over the
   > restricted-range one it replaced.
2. ~~Only then the protein arms~~ **Run — EXP-R2-094.** ProtGPT2 on the declared
   width-192 configuration, three corpus draws, exhaustive 712+8 over the same
   36×20 grid, `--census-sequences 600` matched to the text control because the
   census score is a mean over sequences and an unmatched count would hand one arm
   a noisier selector. Both narrowed-estimand costs stand in the claim: two of
   eight distance bins unreachable, and a cohort selected on BPE compressibility.

   | | 20260728 | 20260801 | 20260802 | range |
   |---|---:|---:|---:|---|
   | ProtGPT2 | +0.2444 | +0.1543 | +0.1926 | **[+0.154, +0.244]** |
   | gpt2-large | +0.4824 | +0.4627 | +0.4493 | **[+0.449, +0.482]** |

   **The L22 failure does not reproduce on copy suppression.** ProtGPT2's
   *induction* value is −0.226 to −0.006; every copy-suppression draw is positive
   and above that band. A text/protein deficit does survive — the arms do not
   overlap at their most adverse draws, gap **+0.205** — but it is a *partial*
   transfer rather than the near-zero induction reading, and three confounds were
   eliminated before it was called one (EXP-R2-096): **instance count** (gpt2-large
   reads +0.452 to +0.521 at 21.4k instances, *fewer* than ProtGPT2's 29.4k, and
   tripling it to 64.6k moves the statistic 0.002–0.038); **keys per instance**
   (+0.0144 per key measured across eight gpt2-large runs spanning 2.73–5.59 keys,
   so ProtGPT2's 0.74-key deficit buys ~5% of the gap); and **two-sided
   disattenuation** — the census side's split-half reliability is 0.996–0.999 on
   both modalities, the causal side's 0.67–0.91, and correcting both moves the gap
   from +0.205 to +0.211. The width lever is not an explanation either: paired by
   draw on identical digests, w192→w512 gives +0.079, +0.028, −0.014, +0.065, not
   one sign, and the w512 text readings stay far above ProtGPT2's range.

   *One direction the pre-registration got backwards.* Blocker 2 argued the
   unmatched score would attenuate the protein arm **toward** the hypothesis.
   Measured, it does the reverse — ProtGPT2 reads +0.360 to +0.420 unmatched
   against +0.154 to +0.244 matched. **The wrong score would have hidden this
   result, not manufactured it.**

   **What the result then exposed is in §8 item 0**: on both mechanisms the
   all-grid statistic reports the opposite of the stratum a census publishes.
3. ~~ZymCTRL separately or not at all, and if separately then `build_cohorts` needs
   its reference band decoupled from its cohort band first — as specified the
   single-length window does not run.~~ **Done, and it decided the panel's last
   architectural confound (EXP-R2-128).** The reference band is now separately
   declarable and defaults to the cohort band, so no other invocation changed. The
   admissible window was chosen on measurement rather than assumption — 240–420
   residues scanned, R=396 holds 802 records that all tokenise to exactly 406,
   where R=264 holds more but is inhomogeneous and `content_bounds` correctly
   refuses one of them — giving width 406, band [396, 396], n=400. Four draws,
   K=4. The "separately or not at all" framing was right about the *cohort* and
   wrong about the *claim*: the surviving D2.c statistic is a within-arm
   classification against the arm's own chance level, which never needed a shared
   window at all.
4. **A second protein decoder, now reachable (EXP-R2-096, EXP-R2-098 queued).**
   `14_paa_census.py` could not run on any ProGen2 arm at all: its shipped
   modelling code returns the attention pattern only when the module is asked for
   it, and the tap read the tuple position that holds a key-value cache there. The
   repair is in `tap_attention`, verified bit-identical on gpt2-large, ProtGPT2 and
   ZymCTRL and newly working on both ProGen2 arms. A1 is then attainable at ban
   depth 3 — 30108 instances on ProGen2-base against 2851 at depth 20 — while
   gpt2-large is unmoved (21649 against 21619), so the pair can be **matched at the
   relaxed depth** rather than the protein arm taking an exception. *Declared
   asymmetry:* ProGen2 returns 8.40 keys per instance against 2.86, worth roughly
   +0.08 **to ProGen2**, so a ProGen2 deficit would be conservative and a ProGen2
   advantage confounded.

5. **~~Run — EXP-R2-098, and it splits the protein family.~~ THE SPLIT IS WITHDRAWN (EXP-R2-116); the ProGen2 readings below stand.** ProtGPT2's positive value was its FASTA line-break token: 28–33% of its PAA instances predict a layout token and they carry 108–110% of its clean-margin mass, against 0.0% on every ProGen2 arm. Excluded uniformly, ProtGPT2 reads **−0.088 to −0.114** and joins the ProGen2 family. **Two further defects in the same runs:** `a1_candidate_pool.verdict` reads **FAIL** on all three ProtGPT2 ban-3/n=200 runs (9,958–10,040 against a gate of 20,000) and on all three dialogpt-small ban-3 runs — the failure step 2 of this section predicted, quoted here as settling the split without the verdict being read — and llama-3.2-3b is at K=2 (mean +0.2915), not the K=1 value quoted below. **The original item follows unaltered.**

    ProGen2-base reads
   **[−0.292, −0.159]** over three draws at ban 3 / n=200 against a matched ban-3
   gpt2-large control at **[+0.454, +0.507]**, with hit@20 of **0.3/20 against a
   chance level of 0.93**. That is the L22 induction failure reproducing on copy
   suppression — the opposite of ProtGPT2, which reads [+0.154, +0.244]. The
   declared keys/instance asymmetry runs against the result, so the deficit is
   conservative.

   **Not a noise artefact, and this was checked before the result was written
   down.** ProGen2's grid sits at the noise floor in both strata (median
   |effect|/SE 0.93 in the census top 20%, 1.12 in the bulk, against ~2.3/1.35 on
   text and ProtGPT2), so the causal side had to be shown to carry a signal at all.
   Measured three ways without reference to the census: the cross-draw rank
   correlation of |ΔM-gap| — a ceiling on what *any* selector could reach — is
   **+0.527** on ProGen2-base against +0.777 and +0.784 on the text controls and
   +0.712 on ProtGPT2, with 24.9% of heads carrying intervals excluding zero
   against ~43% elsewhere. The ordering is real but weaker. **That cannot produce
   the negative**: independent measurement error attenuates a rank correlation
   toward zero, it does not carry it past zero.

   ~~**The split may not be quoted yet, and the reason is a confound rather than a
   doubt.**~~ **Settled — the split is a property of the arm (EXP-R2-099, EXP-R2-100).**
   ProtGPT2 was measured at ban 20 / n=600 and ProGen2-base at ban 3 / n=200, so the
   comparison was confounded; the text control is unmoved across that change
   (+0.4685 against +0.4726), which bounded the condition effect **on text** at
   ~0.004 and bounded nothing on a per-residue alphabet. Run in ProGen2's exact
   condition, **ProtGPT2 reads [+0.1874, +0.2318] — a move of +0.0123** — positive on
   every draw and disjoint from both ProGen2 arms. **ProGen2-medium replicates
   ProGen2-base** at [−0.2819, −0.1385]; the two are architecturally identical down
   to the parameter count and differ only in pretraining corpus, so the agreement is
   neither a scale nor an architecture effect.

   **The split is a measurement; its explanation is not identified, and this panel
   cannot identify it (EXP-R2-113).** ProtGPT2 is the only protein arm that is not
   residue-tokenised (multi-residue BPE, vocabulary 50257 against ProGen2's 32) and
   the only one with a positive ρ; it is also the only protein arm carrying GPT-2's
   architecture, and it has its own pretraining corpus. **Arm identity,
   tokenisation and architecture covary perfectly across the four protein arms.**
   Across all ten arms, Spearman(recurrent fraction, D2.c ρ) is −0.437 at p = 0.21,
   which settles nothing.

   **Refined: the split is two effects, and the panel isolates one of them.** No
   single account fits the ordering. *Architecture* fails — ProtGPT2 carries gpt2's
   architecture and reads **0.174 below the lowest gpt2-architecture text arm**.
   *Tokenisation* fails — it is BPE-tokenised and reads **0.106 below the lowest BPE
   text arm**. *Modality alone* fails — it sits ~0.43 above every ProGen2 arm, all
   protein. What follows is:

   - **A modality effect of ~0.26 IS isolated, by the designed matched pair.**
     ProtGPT2 against gpt2-large holds architecture, tokenisation family and
     vocabulary size fixed and varies modality and corpus: **+0.2145 against
     +0.4726**. This is the contrast the matched pair exists for and it needs no
     further arm.
   - **A second effect of ~0.43 separates the two protein families and is NOT
     isolated**: ProtGPT2 against ProGen2 varies architecture, tokenisation,
     vocabulary and corpus at once. ZymCTRL — gpt2-family architecture with
     *residue* tokenisation — is the single arm that would break it, and it is
     excluded from D2.c's window. **That exclusion now carries a cost it did not
     carry when it was decided**, and the cost is recorded rather than the frozen
     design being reopened here. **EXP-R2-103 completes the family:
   progen2-small reads −0.1193, so all three ProGen2 arms are negative across three
   scales and both pretraining corpora.** progen2-small is the least negative of the
   three; scale, its 192-head grid and draw noise at K=2 are not separated by this
   evidence and no reading of that ordering is claimed.

   | arm | condition | K | range |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | gpt2-large | ban 20, n=600 | 6 | [+0.4493, +0.4824] |
   | gpt2-large | ban 3, n=200 | 3 | [+0.4539, +0.5071] |
   | ProtGPT2 | ban 20, n=600 | 6 | [+0.1543, +0.2444] |
   | ProtGPT2 | **ban 3, n=200** | 3 | **[+0.1874, +0.2318]** |
   | ProGen2-base | ban 3, n=200 | 3 | [−0.2924, −0.1594] |
   | ProGen2-medium | ban 3, n=200 | 3 | [−0.2819, −0.1385] |

   **Superseded by EXP-R2-118, and the ProtGPT2 row is not an available
   configuration.** With `circuits.layout_token_ids` imported by
   `prediction_addressed.build_instance_pool`, ProtGPT2 at n=200 yields **7,122**
   instances against A1's 20,000 gate — a FAIL harder than the one that already
   stood unread against these three runs. n=200 is therefore not available to that
   arm at all under a correct content definition, so the ban-3 / n=200 ProtGPT2 row
   settles nothing about condition-matching. The whole table is additionally
   pre-guard: it is the record of the reasoning, not current values. D2.c must be
   re-run at n=600 on every arm, which is what makes the ProGen2 rows owed as well
   even though their pools carry 0.0% layout tokens.

6. **The two readings of D2.c partition the panel differently (EXP-R2-099/100).**
   D2.c can be asked as a *ranking* question — does the census score order causal
   magnitude, which is L22's statistic — or as a *size* question, which is how the
   plan row poses it. They were treated as one question. They are not:

   | ordering | text worst | protein best | gap | within-protein spread | gap ÷ spread |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | **size** (ΔM-gap ÷ clean M-gap) | 3.19% | 0.89% | **2.30 pts** | 0.41 pts | **5.6** |
   | valence (top-minus-grid shift) | −0.128 | −0.015 | +0.113 | 0.051 | 2.2 |
   | ranking (census-to-causal ρ) | +0.3840 | +0.2145 | +0.170 | **+0.449** | **0.38** |

   Four text arms and four protein arms, over nine arm-conditions.
   **The cross-lab control D2.c had been missing is now in hand and confirms all
   three (EXP-R2-107): qwen2.5-0.5b — rotary/GQA from another lab, sharing neither
   architecture nor tokeniser nor corpus with the GPT-2 family — reads ranking
   +0.486, size 12.29% and valence −0.18, text-like on every one.** Until it ran,
   "text arms do this and protein arms do not" was equally consistent with "the
   GPT-2 lineage does", since every other D2.c number came from that family or from
   ProtGPT2, which is gpt2-large's architecture. llama-3.2-3b has since landed at K=2, mean **+0.2915** (draws 20260728 +0.3201 and 20260801 +0.2629) — EXP-R2-116. *Both figures are pre-layout-guard and are subject to the D2.c re-measurement above.*

   **One limit on the size result, and it is a within-family trend rather than a
   scale law.** Inside the GPT-2 family, with corpus, tokeniser and architecture
   held fixed, the statistic falls monotonically over four points — 124M **18.40%**,
   355M 13.13%, 774M 7.61%, 1558M **3.19%** (EXP-R2-109). **Across families it does
   not**: llama-3.2-3b at 3B reads **8.62%**, far above gpt2-xl's 3.19% at half the
   size, and qwen at 0.5B reads 12.29%. It is not a head-count artefact — A4 is a
   *maximum* over the grid, so gpt2-xl's 1200 heads bias it **up** against
   gpt2-medium's 384 and it still reads lowest. So **the like-for-like comparison
   is at matched scale, where the gap is ~12×** (gpt2-large 7.61% against ProtGPT2
   0.49% and ProGen2 0.62–0.65%), while the whole-panel 3.6× rests on gpt2-xl, and
   this panel has no protein arm above 774M to extend the other side.

   **All three separate text from protein**, which corrects an earlier statement in
   this section that ranking did not; the difference is in how much structure each
   carries *inside* the protein modality. By size the four protein arms sit in a
   tight band — ProtGPT2 0.77% and 0.49%, ProGen2-base 0.65%, ProGen2-medium 0.62%,
   ProGen2-small 0.89% — spanning 0.41 points against a 6.29-point gap to the
   nearest text arm, and gpt2-medium **raised** the text floor rather than breaking
   it, at 13.13% over three draws. By ranking the same four arms span **+0.449**,
   from ProGen2-base's −0.234 to ProtGPT2's +0.215 — **wider than the +0.254 that
   separates ProtGPT2 from the nearest text arm.** **A census can rank well on an
   arm whose effects are negligible and badly on one whose effects are not**, so a
   selector-validity statistic and an effect-size statistic are not interchangeable
   evidence about a modality; L22's choice of the former is what makes its result a
   statement about instruments rather than about protein models.

7. **What the census contributes is directional, and only on text (EXP-R2-099).**
   The concern was pre-registered above — "a magnitude ranking conflates suppressive
   heads with promoting ones" — and is now measured rather than carried. Fraction of
   measurable census-top heads that are *suppressive* (`delta_m_gap > 0`, the
   module's stated convention at `prediction_addressed.py:1125`):

   | arm | top 1% | top 5% | top 10% | top 20% | controls | grid |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | gpt2-large (ban 20, 6 draws) | 0.19 | 0.20 | 0.20 | 0.19 | 0.50 | 0.41 |
   | gpt2-large (ban 3, 3 draws) | 0.19 | 0.22 | 0.21 | 0.21 | 0.50 | 0.40 |
   | ProtGPT2 (6 draws) | 0.44 | 0.61 | 0.59 | 0.61 | 0.47 | 0.67 |
   | ProGen2-base (3 draws) | 0.11 | 0.08 | 0.24 | 0.29 | 0.89 | 0.35 |

   gpt2-large's top is a ~21-point enrichment for **promoting** heads against its
   own grid, with control heads at exactly 0.50 — stable over four cuts, two
   conditions and nine draws, top-5% median −0.00242 with a bootstrap CI excluding
   zero. ProtGPT2's top is not enriched either way against its own grid (0.61
   against 0.67; median +0.00106, CI includes zero). **ProGen2's apparent enrichment
   is a selection effect and is not claimed**: unrestricted by measurability it
   reads 0.40 against a grid of 0.41.

   **Re-checked on two ProGen2 arms and in both conditions (EXP-R2-100), and the
   call holds.** Restricting to measurable heads is itself a selection on effect
   size, so the shift computed over *all* heads is the honest one. Census top 5%
   minus that arm's own grid: gpt2-large **−0.22** and **−0.21**, ProtGPT2 +0.02 and
   +0.03, ProGen2-base −0.02, ProGen2-medium +0.01, ProGen2-small +0.04. **gpt2-large's census
   top is strongly directionally selective and it selects for *promoting* heads; no
   protein arm shows the enrichment in either direction.** *Qualified by one draw of gpt2-medium at −0.05
   (EXP-R2-105), then unqualified at K=3 (EXP-R2-106): gpt2-medium reads **−0.128**,
   and the two text arms span −0.13 to −0.22 against the four protein arms' −0.015 to
   +0.038.* Text worst −0.128 against protein best −0.015, so **the separation holds
   on two text arms** and the item is stated of text again — weaker on gpt2-medium
   than on gpt2-large, with EXP-R2-100's gpt2-xl lanes the independent check. ProGen2-base's −0.26 under the measurability restriction against −0.02
   without it is precisely the artefact the call named. Measurable-head fractions
   travel with this: ~43% on gpt2-large and ProtGPT2 in both conditions against
   **24.9%** and **26.3%** on the ProGen2 arms.

   Two consequences. First, the instrument's
   directional selectivity is — on gpt2-large at least — a sharper transfer
   failure than the magnitude gap and is not visible in any statistic reported
   before this. Second, **the mechanism label needs care even on the text control**
   — a census whose top is 80% promoting is ranking prediction-addressed attention,
   not isolating copy suppression, and the D2.c positive control should be read as
   the former.

### D3 — Toward methods for biological knowledge and discovery

**Admission rule.** A method enters this section only when it answers a declared biological knowledge question or a reproducible measurement failure, and the two standing rejections of §7 remain unchanged. Being listed here records a testable candidate, not a result or a biological claim.

**Reordered 2026-08-06.** The foundational programme has reached the point where another draw of an instrument this document has already characterised cannot change a conclusion, and the panel work is closed accordingly (D2.c, D2.d). What the programme has *not* done is measure itself against the current external state of the art, and that is now the main line: **D2.g reproduces ProGenMech under this repository's own gates**, because a reproduction that only re-derives an author's headline number adds nothing this catalogue does not already have, while one that applies attainability, denominator validity and power discipline to it can say something the original cannot. The remaining D3 items keep their gates and move behind it.

| item | gate | cost |
|---|---|---|
| ~~**D3.e**~~ | **DEAD 2026-08-06. D2.f returned FAIL and the gate was pre-registered, so this item closes without being attempted.** No instance-level cause was found, and the finding that arrived instead removes the premise: a selector that loses to the head's layer index on the failing arms does not need adapting, and adapting it would be patching an instrument this document has now characterised twice. The item as written follows, for the record. **Protein-adapted PAA selector. Conditional on D2.f returning a cause**, and dead otherwise. Built on one ProGen2 arm, changing exactly one selector factor, validated out-of-sample on a second ProGen2 arm and ZymCTRL, with the ByGPT5 and gpt2-large controls retained so that a protein repair cannot be bought by breaking text. Pass: a held-out protein arm at ≥3× its own chance with a lower confidence bound above chance, at least double the current census, reproduced on a second arm, and needing causal labelling of ≤10% of the grid. A method that needs >20% exhaustive labelling is not a screen and is worth nothing | 10–30 GPU-h, zero if D2.f fails |
| ~~**D3.f**~~ | **CLOSED 2026-08-06, unrun, on two independent grounds that agree — and the instrument was built before it closed, so this is a decision about the statistic rather than about the effort.** *From the literature gate:* Sutter et al. (arXiv:2507.08802) obtain **100% interchange accuracy on a randomly initialised model that cannot do the task** absent a linearity constraint, and Makelov et al. (arXiv:2311.17030) show subspace interventions succeeding through a dormant parallel pathway; Méloux et al. (arXiv:2607.00267) then find most validity metrics, interchange accuracy included, fail to separate valid from invalid abstractions over 10 systems and 30 metrics. An interchange accuracy reported without those controls is uninterpretable; reported with them it is their experiment. No DAS application to any protein, DNA or RNA model was found — unrun, but unrun is not promising. *From this repository's own audit of `18_das_subspace.py`, reached before the literature verdict and agreeing with it:* the protein arm cannot be built at the committed defaults (the exact-repeat census returns **48 matching records of 203,063 eligible** against `--records 96`, and 48 probes at 3 cases each cannot reach `--cases 192` either); an all-NaN basis passes the orthonormality guard and writes an interchange accuracy of ~0.0 to disk as a measurement; and, decisively, **the unembedding-difference baseline collapses in rank exactly on the protein arms** — its rows are differences of unembedding rows over the arm's alphabet, so a 31-token residue vocabulary gives rank ≤ 19 whatever dimension is requested, while the requested dimension is what the artefact records. The one control whose job is to stop a learned subspace being credited when it has only found the copy channel is weakest precisely where a positive protein claim would be made. The rotation algebra itself is correct (orthonormal to 2.4e-7, gradients through the QR, counterfactual label from the high-level model rather than the base model), which is why this closes on the statistic and not on the implementation — the same reason §8 item 0 closed. The item as written follows, for the record. **Distributed causal subspace pilot, one assay, one model.** Rationale: probes decode protein properties well (L16) while single-feature steering has never worked here, which is what a *distributed* representation predicts. **Admissible only where §7's standing rejection is not**: the high-level variable must be defined by a counterfactual on the model's own output distribution, never by a trained probe's direction. Pass: held-out interchange accuracy ≥0.70 and ≥0.10 above a random-subspace floor, ≥65% sign agreement with measured DMS effects, <5% degradation on unrelated sequences, **and family-disjoint validation** | 0 GPU-h spent on measurement |
| **D3.a** | Dictionary evaluation whose estimand is power-checked on the text control first, reporting loss recovered and KL rather than FVU; threshold frozen before any protein arm is scored. **Deferred behind D2.g and explicitly scoped against it**: if the ProGenMech reproduction already measures loss and KL recovery on a protein decoder against valid denominators, the overlap is most of this item and it shrinks to the matched-pair text control it was really about | 17–50 GPU-h, reassessed after D2.g |
| ~~**D3.b**~~ | **CLOSED 2026-08-12 — refuted on its own premise, and the negative is measured rather than an instrument failure (EXP-R2-173, EXP-R2-178).** Phase A ran on six arms in one cell, 400 Swiss-Prot proteins in the 64–246 band, an eleven-point relative-depth grid, family-split at 188 seen against 187 unseen Pfam families, with 1000 shuffled-property and 1000 rank-matched partition draws per protein arm. **The pre-declared gate fired on both of its conditions, so Phase B — which was the causal gate — was not authorised and was not run; nothing from this item speaks to causal dependence.** *(1) The aperture gain is on the text control.* The gain is the relative depth at which residue identity resolves minus the depth at which the property class resolves, and positive means the property is settled while the identity is not, which is the whole of the semantic-aperture motivation. On **ProtGPT2, ProGen2-base and ProGen2-medium the biochemical property resolves *later* than residue identity**, sign-invariantly across the τ sweep and across all three declared properties; the gain is largest on **gpt2-large, +0.195 at τ=0.50**, where no aperture problem was ever claimed and the vocabulary is 50,257. There is no hidden early coarse-structure stage for a property basis to recover. *(2) Two of the three declared properties clear nothing.* Against the rank-matched partition null on the unseen-family side, **charge and volume clear at 0 of 11 depths on every protein arm**; only hydropathy survives, only in the deepest third, and at a margin four to six times smaller than the text control's on its own analogue. *The single positive protein cell was a conditioning tag, and it dissolved under the pre-declared test.* ZymCTRL was the only sign-invariantly positive protein arm and the only EC-conditioned one; holding the tag constant at one value and changing nothing else takes hydropathy from **7/11 to 0/11**, its final-layer within-protein readout from **+0.847 to +0.140**, and charge and volume down by 14x and 8.9x — zero of eleven depths on all three properties, where the other three protein arms already sat on two of three. One caveat travels with that and is not removable at this estimand: the tag is worth **1.87 nats** of the arm's own next-residue prediction on this cohort, closely reproducing L15's independently measured 1.73, so under a fixed tag the model is simply worse at the task and part of the collapse is degradation rather than the tag carrying the property. The two readings cannot be separated here and need not be, because on both statistics normalised against the arm's *own* null at its *own* conditioning the effect is gone; the fixed cell is read as the collapse of a positive result and not as a measurement in its own right, since volume fails its own final-layer positive control there. An unconditioned cell is refused as structurally unavailable rather than skipped: an EC-conditioned arm's scored span is delimited by its own start and end tokens, so dropping the tag leaves no definition of which positions are scored. **What makes this a measured negative:** the final-layer positive control cleared on **all eighteen arm-concept cells** at the 99.9th percentile, so no arm is unmeasurable, and seen-minus-unseen differences held within **±0.029** on all five unconditioned arms, so the family split is not where the candidate fails. **What it does not say:** it does not show that protein decoders lack biochemical structure in their residual streams, only that such structure is not visible *through the output aperture, earlier than residue identity, above a matched null*. §8 item 3's standing opening — a readout that does not route through the unembedding — is untouched by it. **What it leaves behind is L28**, and that limitation is directly exposed on an existing lens readout. The item as written follows, for the record. **Concept-aligned Lens / semantic aperture, candidate only.** Motivated by the confirmed low-rank output aperture of residue-tokenised models: replace raw-token Logit Lens or DLA comparisons with projections onto a pre-declared property basis. Within protein models this basis may include hydrophobicity, charge, side-chain volume and externally measured structural labels; a text–protein comparison is admissible only on modality-neutral abstractions such as copying, distance dependence or constraint satisfaction, because biological properties have no text analogue. A candidate result must beat shuffled-property and rank-matched controls, survive a causal intervention rather than only decode, and reproduce family-disjoint. Until those gates pass it is a proposed interface correction, not an achieved method or a cross-model coefficient | Phase A spent; Phase B never authorised |
| **D3.c** | Oracle-grounded explanation normalised by measured channel capacity: after the L9 re-derivation, attainability shown on gpt2-large first | 4–8 GPU-h |
| ~~**D3.d**~~ | **HALTED 2026-08-12 at Stage 1, on its own pre-registered refusal; Stage 2 was never authorised and is not run (EXP-R2-176/177/179/180/184).** *Stage 0 stops it, and it stops it structurally.* EXP-R2-177 pre-declared the one way Stage 0 alone could halt the campaign — if the corpus coupling channel fails its own positive control, predicting measured epistasis above its own column-permutation null, on most proteins, then A3 is not interpretable on them. It fails on **all** of them: **0 of 22, verdict NOT_USABLE**. The six pair-rich proteins carry the reading — coupling between **−0.012 and +0.065**, below its own null on every one, on referents whose split-half reliability is 0.39–0.85 and whose pair counts run to a thousand. This is not a power failure of the referent. *The consequence is structural rather than a setback.* A3 — the second-order subtraction, the whole reason this item was admitted over F10 — cannot be built on this corpus interface. A2 remains well-posed and needs no coupling channel, but A2 alone measures only whether a model's predicted epistasis beats a separation-, magnitude- and conservation-matched null; it cannot separate learned biology from corpus statistics, which is the Objective-3 bar. **Without A3 this design cannot clear the bar it was admitted for — and that is established at zero GPU cost, rather than after the ten to twenty GPU-hours Stage 2 was booked at.** *What it bounds, narrowly.* It does **not** say protein corpora carry no pairwise coupling; direct-coupling analysis is a working technology. It says that the coupling estimable **from this interface** — DIAMOND hits against the staged UniRef50, reweighted as this repository's profile code does, at the Neff those searches return — does not predict measured specific epistasis above its own construction-matched null on any protein in this benchmark. The proximal cause is visible in the artefact and is the hazard the estimator was chosen to respect: a 20×20 joint distribution at Neff 29–210 is a fraction of an effective observation per cell, and a held-out estimator refuses to manufacture association from that where a plug-in one would have produced it and grown it as depth fell (Appendix B rule 3, L12). **Stated as a property of corpus-coupling baselines in general rather than as a fact about this run: the honesty and the powerlessness are the same property.** An estimator that will not invent a coupling is an estimator that cannot find a shallow one, and a channel too weak to serve as a baseline is not thereby evidence that the model has learned anything. Whether a deeper alignment — an HHblits or JackHMMER profile rather than a hit list — would restore the channel was left open here, and it is now answered against, from this stage's own artefact and at zero GPU cost (EXP-R2-189). **The defect is anti-correlated with what depth buys.** The 22 units carrying a measured coupling split by pair count rather than by alignment depth: six carry 218–1047 pairs against tight nulls of 0.029–0.102, which their couplings of −0.012 to +0.065 all fall short of, while the other sixteen carry 2 to 16 pairs and nulls reaching 1.000. Depth does not separate those groups — Neff spans 29–1585 across the pair-rich six and 104–2775 across the pair-poor sixteen, and the most pair-rich unit of all already sits at Neff 1585 and still reads −0.012 against a null of 0.036 — and the correlations say it directly: the null's 99th percentile against pair count is Spearman **−0.640 (p = 0.0013)**, against Neff **+0.216 (p = 0.33)**. What makes these nulls unresolvable is the number of position pairs the assay measures, not the depth of the alignment behind them. A deeper profile could therefore act only on the six pair-rich units, which is below this repository's eight-unit bootstrap floor, so even complete success there could not produce the pooled A3 the item was admitted for. And the cohort is exhausted rather than under-drawn: 166 assays were refused, 148 of them because no double mutant carries both of its constituent singles, so no larger pair-rich stratum exists in this benchmark. HHblits, JackHMMER and MMseqs2 accordingly buy nothing here and are declined for this purpose. *The null structure is what makes the refusal readable, and the counterfactual is concrete.* Twelve assays carry only 2–5 pairs and several read a coupling of exactly **+1.000**, because a Spearman over two points is ±1 by construction; their column-permutation nulls read +1.000 as well, so every one of them FAILS. **A design that had compared those against zero, or against a chance level derived from the number of pairs, would have reported perfect corpus prediction on four proteins.** *Stage 1 ran anyway, and its result is worth keeping.* A1 FAILs on its conjunction — the frozen rule required a pass on gpt2-large **and** on at least one protein arm — so the pre-registered consequence stands, and is in any case moot. **The instrument is validated on the text control:** at 128 probes the same estimator, arithmetic, within-probe permutation null and bootstrap recover a planted coupling at **+0.5529 [+0.5286, +0.5701]** against a null 99th percentile of **+0.0691**, retrieving the planted pair first in **124 of 128** probes against a chance level of 0.125, at a planted-minus-control epistasis of **+8.59 against +0.06** nats. A null returned by this instrument is a measurement and not a shrug.
